# Plan #1551: Settings coding-agent auto-update status list (supported agents, per-agent config, installed version, live updating state)

Author: ac-architect-v3, workgroup wg-13-ac-dev-team-v3. Full delivery: one new Rust module (a pure sink), additive gate/snapshot/event changes, one new read command with Tauri and WebSocket parity, an atomic multi-surface prompt lifecycle, one read-only Settings block, the Option 2 staged-progress timeline in the startup overlay with a persistent final summary (the user's choice, §18; consolidated with the two round-5 verdicts in round 6, §19), docs. No schema migration, no new dependency, no version bump.

Status: READY_FOR_IMPLEMENTATION

Revision: round 6 (2026-08-26), the consolidated revision that applies every binding finding of the two round-5 verdicts (FE `CHANGES_REQUIRED`: B1/M1, M2-M8, N1, N2, N6, N9 and the coordinator's decision on N4; Grinch `CHANGES_REQUIRED`: M1, M2, R1-R5) to the round-5 text (SHA-256 `E39723606B5FBB829B187FC5846F4B10E9B9DF3EE19A4A8E1331E13CD269B16C`, architect `PLAN_READY_ROUND5`). It is mechanical and decision-complete, reopens no Option 2 decision, and §19 maps every item to its sections and tests. Round 5 (2026-08-26) was opened by the coordinator after the user explicitly chose the `Opción 2 · Progreso por etapas` view of the final HTML prototype (`index-V1.html`, SHA-256 `A5A7149A662EC49802CBE0A7886BA2C6CD05A8984715FDD7D72092306268CEB6`, section `#opcion-2`). Round 4 (SHA-256 `193E2AEDB07E96FD2F75AA56B272C86730E59CBB451B4E4D5CDBA92FF716F5E7`, approved cold by both reviewers) is superseded only because the user fixed a materially different visual contract for the update experience; every round-4 decision stands except where Option 2 forces a change, and §18 lists each change, the five divergences the prototype declared and how each is resolved decision-complete, the re-audited inventory and arcs, and the reviewer recommendation. Round 4 was the one additional round the user authorized after the round-3 dual `CHANGES_REQUIRED`. Round 3 (SHA-256 `839B15ED7F3E9B6385725F2B1F094B17B0048F7B0DD2D8D6AE51D1E9F6F40572`) was rejected by both cold reviewers for one and the same finding, M1: the plan described `answer_prompt_enqueues_the_closure_before_releasing_the_loop` as failing under the superseded send-before-emit order, which a spawned loop stand-in cannot establish (a task woken by `oneshot::Sender::send` runs only when the sending task yields, so the test was green under both orders on the `current_thread` runtime of a plain `#[tokio::test]` and flaky on `multi_thread`). This revision applies the coordinator's round-4 mandatory change R4-M1 and nothing else (§17): the test's discriminator becomes a synchronous waker probe registered on the prompt's oneshot receiver, the API and the design of §5.4 step 9 stay exactly as they are, and every sentence that rested on the old claim is corrected (§5.4 step 10, §12 step 10, §15 R3-M1, §16 P7). Everything else stands unchanged: the round-3 mandatory changes R3-M1..R3-M5 and the residual map of the round-2 verdicts (§15), the round-3 preflight P7-P8 (§16), the round-1 corrections (§13), the round-2 preflight P1-P6 (§14); the non-blocking round-3 residuals are recorded in §17 and are not round-4 scope. History: round 2 (SHA-256 `91E073CA96C42D5BEFA91572A9E94402F02B585F2DD709219B7BE746DBE355D1`) was rejected by the dual gate (Grinch `CHANGES_REQUIRED` M1-M2 with residuals R1-R8; owning Rust dev `PLAN_APPROVED` with residuals R1-R14) and treated in §15; round 1 (SHA-256 `78AD249FA17E3585EAE291DBFD47C8B11B907B1FEA8BDE44EC1577E6AF4C5DD1`) history is kept in §13.

Issue: [mblua/AgentsCommander#1551](https://github.com/mblua/AgentsCommander/issues/1551), "Settings: coding-agent auto-update status list (supported agents, per-agent config, installed version, live updating state)" (OPEN, no comments).

Objective: give Settings one read-only place that answers, per update-capable coding agent, "is auto-update on, off, or never asked", "is the CLI installed and which version", and "is it being updated right now", make the blocking startup overlay show a staged per-agent timeline instead of a bare spinner (Option 2: every agent of the pass as a node that is `Pendiente`, `Actualizando...` with its exact update command, `Listo` with the version transition when both versions were read, or `Falló` with the reason inline; a global `n de N completados` counter with a progress bar; a final summary that stays until the user closes it), and make the startup prompt behave as ONE prompt across the desktop window and browser remote tabs (first answer wins everywhere). The existing per-registered-agent Auto-update dropdown (#1342/#1345) stays the only settings write path besides the prompt answer that already exists.

---

## 1. Frozen authority and entry gate

Working tree: `repo-AgentsCommander`, branch `feature/1551-agent-update-status`, created from `main`. Verified base at authoring time (2026-08-25 UTC, re-verified cold for round 3): branch head == `main` == `origin/main` == `92afbfe6463c0b780fd8b760523920769d645f86`, `git status --porcelain` empty. Codebase Memory gate `ready` (project `D-0_repos-AgentsCommander_iac-.ac-wg-13-ac-dev-team-v3-repo-AgentsCommander`, 24,597 nodes / 132,439 edges, indexed at the same SHA; the round-3 gate run returned the same numbers).

Root `.gitignore` line 11 ignores `/plans/`; this new file must be added with `git add -f plans/1551-agent-update-status.md` (the only `-f` allowed in this plan; never widen the ignore rule).

The implementers must repeat the authority ritual: fetch `origin/main` and stop for current-base review if `origin/main`, the committed branch head, or their merge base no longer equals the frozen SHA. Every line number below is at 92afbfe6 (all of §3.1 and §3.3 re-derived in round 3 from a numbered print, so the drift Rust R5 reported is corrected); if a number no longer matches the quoted text, re-anchor on the quoted text, never on the number.

Every decision the coordinator fixed in the round-1 dispatch is binding here and repeated where it lands (catalog-order rows without dedup, tri-state display, built-in argv map, richer backend install distinction, asynchronous bounded probes, additive snapshot plus per-command events, unchanged dropdown write path, registration marker, blocking overlay kept, Tauri and WebSocket parity, no manual trigger, no version comparison, no Gemini row, no migration, no version bump). The round-3 dispatch adds: an atomic, two-surface prompt lifecycle with `Ok(false)` for superseded and late answers; a version parser that skips empty and ANSI-only lines; single-lock probe scheduling; a pass-end transition that can never expose `finished` with the old cache generation; and testability without false assumptions. The round-4 dispatch (2026-08-26, the one extra round the user authorized after the round-3 dual `CHANGES_REQUIRED`) replaces the discriminator of one test with a synchronous waker probe (§17) and changes nothing else: no wire, state, ordering, ownership, gate, inventory, or delivery decision moves. The round-5 dispatch (2026-08-26 03:22 UTC) makes the user's Option 2 the complete visual and semantic authority for the update experience: the timeline is presentation only (the backend keeps running every update concurrently), `Pendiente` is a known-but-not-started node and never a serialization, the prototype's review aid `html-component-blink-debug` and the prototype file itself stay out of the product code, the dependencies, the bundle, and this inventory, and the five divergences the prototype declared against round 4 are each resolved in §18 with real data sources and observable fallbacks (no invented order, version, or command). The round-6 dispatch (2026-08-26 05:10 UTC) consolidates the two round-5 verdicts into this text without reopening the design: a monotonic node merge with `skippedNodes` (FE B1/M1, Grinch R2), install events accepted for any pass node and the deferred toasts of a mid-pass snapshot (FE N2, Grinch R3), the exact literals and the exact CSS transcription of Option 2 with one recorded accessibility deviation (FE M2-M8, N4 decided by the coordinator), a deterministic T6 (Grinch M1), the corrected `agent_updates_started` payload wording plus an end-to-end Rust pin (Grinch M2), the seven-line arc record of the prescribed `lib.rs` spelling (Grinch R4) and the editorial alignments (FE N1, N6, N9; Grinch R1, R5); §19 lists each item.

## 2. Issue and objective

The startup pass (#1327, `src-tauri/src/agent_update.rs`) runs each registered command's `updateCommands` behind a full-document overlay that reads `Actualizando coding agents...` and gives no per-agent progress; the snapshot `AgentUpdateStatus {inProgress, prompt, results}` cannot say which commands are running; no code anywhere resolves a coding-agent CLI on PATH or reads its version; the Coding Agents screen lists only registered agents and collapses the per-command policy tri-state to Yes/No; the browser (WebSocket) client receives none of the agent-update events and cannot invoke the agent-update or catalog commands; and because this plan routes the prompt to browser clients too, a prompt answered on one surface would today stay open on every other surface, where Enter/Escape would persist a second, contradicting policy (Grinch round-2 M1). The 2026-08-25 incident (a killed `codex update` left codex uninstalled, invisible in the UI) is the concrete failure.

Required outcomes:

- **(A) List.** Settings > Coding Agents shows a read-only "Auto-update" table with one row for every catalog entry whose (backfilled) `updateCommands` is non-empty, in catalog order, duplicates included, cursor excluded by construction.
- **(B) Tri-state.** Per row the configured policy renders `Yes`, `No`, or `Will ask at startup` (absent key), read from the modal draft so the table and the dropdown never disagree before Save.
- **(C) Install state.** Per row: the detected version, `Not installed` when the command does not resolve or its version probe fails, `Installed` when it resolves but is deliberately not probed, `Checking...` while a probe is pending. Detection is asynchronous, bounded (15 s per probe), parallel, cached per session with a 10 minute TTL, re-asked on every Coding Agents screen open (served from the cache without a process while fresh, re-probed once expired), never awaited by any IPC or WebSocket call, never waiting on the update gate, never overlapping an update of the same CLI, and never running two probe processes for one command at a time (single-lock scheduling, §5.2).
- **(D) Live state.** `Updating...` on rows whose update step is running, `Updated` / `Update failed` for this boot's outcome, cleared/clean on the next boot; the startup overlay shows the pass as the Option 2 timeline ((H) below).
- **(E) Write path unchanged.** The #1345 dropdown and `agentAutoUpdateByCommand` save flow are untouched; the list writes nothing.
- **(F) Transport parity.** Browser clients get the catalog, status, answer, and overview commands and every agent-update event; nothing falls back silently to placeholder data in browser mode.
- **(G) One prompt across surfaces.** A startup prompt shown on the desktop and on browser tabs is resolved by exactly one answer: the first answer to persist wins, it closes the prompt on every surface (`agent_update_prompt_closed` carrying the command, emitted BEFORE the prompt loop is released, so the closure of one prompt is never delivered after the next prompt on a WebSocket client, and a client never clears a prompt the closure does not name), any later answer for that command returns `false` and persists nothing, the accepted late-after-timeout semantics stay (persist for future boots, `false`, current pass unaffected), no client can resurrect a closed prompt from an older snapshot while a new prompt still appears, and a surface whose prompt was closed does not answer it (buttons and Enter/Escape are inert once the prompt is gone).
- **(H) Option 2 overlay (round 5, user decision).** While the pass runs and no prompt is shown on that surface, the overlay card is the staged-progress view: a header with a small spinner, the title `Actualizando coding agents...`, the counter `n de N completados` (plus ` · k falló` / ` · k fallaron` when k > 0) and a progress bar; below it a vertical timeline with exactly one node per agent of the pass, in pass order (catalog order), each node showing its label on one line and, on one line below it, the state word followed, when a detail exists, by a visible ` · ` and the detail; each node in one of four states: `Pendiente` (known for the pass, its update not started or its start not yet observed by this surface; dimmed), `Actualizando...` (emphasized: accent left border, `--sidebar-active` background, the exact update command(s) the pass runs, an indeterminate bar), `Listo` (check mark; `<before> → <after>` when a version was read before and after the update, the honest partial forms of §5.10 otherwise, nothing when no version source exists), `Falló` (cross; `Falló · <reason>` on one line, the runner's reason inline, full text in `title`). When the pass ends with at least one result (`results.length > 0` in §5.9; equivalent to "at least one node" under a correct backend, because every node that stays in the pass yields exactly one result and a skipped node yields none), the card becomes the final summary (the small spinner replaced by a small green ring with a check, title `Coding agents actualizados`, counter and bar final, every node terminal) and stays on screen until the user closes it with `Cerrar`, Enter, or Escape on that surface; the failure toasts appear after the close. Nothing is serialized: all targets still start together, and `Pendiente` only reports what this surface has not yet seen start.

## 3. Evidence (measured at 92afbfe6, not predicted)

### 3.1 Backend: gate, runner, startup flow, commands

- `src-tauri/src/agent_update.rs`: imports `:21-30` (`std::collections::{BTreeMap, HashMap, HashSet}`, `PathBuf`, `Stdio`, `Arc`/`Mutex`, `Duration`, `tauri::{AppHandle, Emitter, Manager}`, `load_catalog_for_settings`/`primary_project_root`, `SettingsState`; `std::path::Path` is NOT imported); constants `:34-41` (`PROMPT_TIMEOUT` 60 s, `UPDATE_STEP_TIMEOUT` 300 s, `OUTPUT_TAIL_BYTES` 8 KiB, `READER_JOIN_TIMEOUT` 5 s). Types: `AgentUpdateResult` `:46-52` (`error` skipped when `None`), `AgentUpdateStatus` `:57-65`, `AgentUpdatePrompt` `:70-73`, `AgentUpdateGate {state: Mutex<GateState>, release: Notify}` `:77-80`, `GateState {started, finished, results, prompted: HashSet<String>, pending: HashMap<String, oneshot::Sender<bool>>, pending_prompt: Option<AgentUpdatePrompt>}` `:82-92`. Gate methods: `new` `:95-107`, `mark_started` `:109-114`, `register_prompt(&self, command, label) -> tokio::sync::oneshot::Receiver<bool>` `:119-134` (inserts `prompted`, `pending`, `pending_prompt`; `debug_assert!` against a duplicate pending command), `drop_pending` `:138-148` (removes the sender and the matching `pending_prompt`), `resolve_answer(&self, command, enabled) -> bool` `:155-168` (`pending.remove` else `false`; clears the matching `pending_prompt`; returns `tx.send(enabled).is_ok()`, i.e. `false` once the receiver is gone), `was_prompted` `:170-176`, `mark_finished` `:183-192` (clears `pending`/`pending_prompt`, sets `finished`, REPLACES `results`, `notify_waiters`), `snapshot` `:194-201` (`in_progress: state.started && !state.finished`), `wait_until_done` `:206-214` (loops on `finished`). `UpdateTarget` `:225-230`, `AgentUpdatePlan` `:233-236`, `build_update_plan` `:252-290` (registered-only, first occurrence per command, first non-empty sequence, `None` answer -> prompt, `true` -> update, `false` -> skip): one prompt per command per boot, never two.
- `run_update_sequence(&UpdateTarget, Duration)` `:304-551`: each step is a whole shell string through `cmd.exe /C` or `sh -c`; `stdin(null)`, piped stdout/stderr drained concurrently, `kill_on_drop(true)`, `creation_flags(0x0800_0000)` on Windows (mandatory: the GUI has no console), `process_group(0)` on Unix, `crate::pty::job::JobObject::for_child` on Windows through `child.id().and_then(JobObject::for_child)` (`child.id()` is `Option<u32>`, `for_child(pid: u32) -> Option<JobObject>` at `pty/job.rs:69`, non-Windows stub `:152-154`), `tokio::time::timeout(step_timeout, child.wait())`, timeout -> `job.terminate()` + `child.kill()` + group `SIGKILL`; output is log-only. Log anchors: `[agent-update] running '<step>' for <label> (<command>)` `:308`, `[agent-update] step ok for <label> (<command>)` `:468`, `step FAILED` `:478`, `step TIMED OUT` `:521`. Pinned by six `runner_*` tests (harness `run_in_tmpdir` `:932-945`, `runner_timeout_kills_tree` `:985` uses `ping -n 30 127.0.0.1 >NUL` / `sleep 30` with a 200 ms step and a 10 s bound).
- Round-5 anchors (same SHA): `UpdateTarget {command, label, commands: Vec<String>, cwd: PathBuf}` `:225-230` (`commands` = the update sequence, the exact shell strings the runner executes); `AgentUpdatePlan {prompts: Vec<UpdateTarget>, updates: Vec<UpdateTarget>}` `:233-236`; `build_update_plan` (`:252-290`) walks the CATALOG once in catalog order (skips unregistered commands, first occurrence per command wins, the sequence comes from the first same-command entry with a non-empty `update_commands`, `None` answer -> `prompts`, `true` -> `updates`, `false` -> dropped): the pass order is therefore catalog order, and `prompts`/`updates` are each in catalog order. The result's `error` (`AgentUpdateResult.error`) is one short reason built by the runner, never an output tail: `exit code <n>` (the `reason` next to the `step FAILED` anchor `:478`), the spawn/wait error string, `timed out after <s>s (killed)` (next to `step TIMED OUT` `:521`), or `update task panicked` (the `Err(_)` arm `:691-696`); output tails go to the log only. `FinishGuard::complete(&mut self, results)` (`:568-570`) only stores the results; the guard is dropped at the end of `run_startup_updates` (`:701` is its last statement), so anything that must run after `finished` needs an explicit `drop(guard)` first (§5.4 step 9b).
- `FinishGuard {gate, app, emit_finished, results}` `:556-565`; `complete` `:568-570`; `Drop` `:574-584` calls `gate.mark_finished(results)` first and, iff `emit_finished`, `app.emit("agent_updates_finished", {results})`.
- `run_startup_updates(app, gate)` `:592-702`: the guard is created FIRST with `emit_finished: false` (`:594-599`); settings cloned out of `SettingsState` (`:601`); catalog via `load_catalog_for_settings` (`:602`); an empty plan returns silently (`:620-623`), which drops the guard and therefore runs `mark_finished(vec![])` WITHOUT an emit: `finished` becomes true on every boot path (quiet boot within milliseconds of startup, real pass at its end), and it is never reset; `gate.mark_started()` `:625`, `app.emit("agent_updates_started", ())` `:626`, `guard.emit_finished = true` `:627`; sequential prompt phase `:647-673` (`register_prompt` `:648`, `app.emit("agent_update_prompt", ..)` `:649-655`, `tokio::time::timeout(PROMPT_TIMEOUT, rx)` `:662`, `Ok(Ok(true))` pushes the target `:663`, `Ok(Ok(false))` skips `:664`, timeout/dropped-channel arm `:665-671` runs `gate.drop_pending` `:669` and `app.emit("agent_update_prompt_closed", ())` `:670`; the comment `:666-668` documents the accepted late-answer semantics: "was_prompted stays true so a late answer still persists and returns Ok(false)"); update phase spawns one `tokio::spawn(run_update_sequence(...))` per target (`:678-684`), `join_all` (`:685`), the result mapping `:686-698` turns a panicked task into `{ok:false, error:"update task panicked"}` (`Err(_)` arm `:691-696`); `guard.complete(results)` `:701` is the last statement. All five emits use `app.emit` (Tauri windows only). A PROMPT ANSWERED through `agent_update_answer` emits nothing today: only the timeout arm emits `agent_update_prompt_closed`. `run_startup_updates` is spawned exactly once per process, inside `setup` (`lib.rs:2216-2223`), before the web server is started (`lib.rs:2308`, `block_on(web::start_server(..))`).
- `src-tauri/src/commands/config.rs`: `get_coding_agent_catalog` `:506-511`, `list_reseedable_agent_commands` `:519-521`, `persist_narrow_settings_update(settings: &SettingsState, mutate_candidate: impl FnOnce(&mut AppSettings)) -> Result<(), String>` `:2252-2257` (`pub(crate)`; delegates to `persist_narrow_settings_update_with_saver` `:2259-2270`, which takes the settings write lock, clones, mutates the candidate, calls the saver (`save_settings`, the real file write), and replaces the in-memory settings; the `_with_saver` variant is the existing test seam for a failing saver), `get_update_status` `:2411-2415` (the cached-read precedent), `get_agent_update_status` `:2422-2426` (`gate.snapshot()`), `agent_update_answer` `:2437-2453` (`was_prompted` guard -> `persist_narrow_settings_update` -> `resolve_answer`: a check-then-act with the persist in between; two clients can pass the check together, both persist, and the last persist wins while the first resolve wins). ### 3.2 Catalog and settings

- `src-tauri/src/config/coding_agents_catalog.rs`: `CodingAgentDefinition` `:79-121` (`key` documented unique, `update_commands: Vec<String>` with `#[serde(default)]`), `load_catalog` `:271-305` (absent file -> embedded; present -> `validate_and_filter` + backfill of entries whose command matches an embedded one; drops duplicate keys; never re-inserts built-ins, so a project catalog that lists only custom entries yields exactly those entries), `primary_project_root` `:313-326` (`pub(crate)`: the FIRST non-empty `project_paths` entry, else `project_path`), `load_catalog_for_settings(&AppSettings)` `:361-368` (project `.ac`, else config dir, else embedded; the parsed path applies the #1546 in-memory backfill). `backfill_update_commands_from_embedded_default` (`:237-256`) fills ONLY an entry whose `updateCommands` is empty, from the embedded entry with the same `command`; a non-empty user-authored sequence always wins (test `load_catalog_never_overwrites_user_update_commands`, `:1863-1877`), so a project catalog entry `{command: "codex", updateCommands: ["codex-up-stub"]}` keeps its stub sequence while its `command` still resolves through PATH (the basis of the T6 fixture, §9.4.2). ### 3.3 Web transport gap

- `src-tauri/src/web/commands.rs`: imports `:1-16` (already `use crate::web::event_broadcast::broadcast_all;` and `use super::broadcast::WsBroadcaster;`), `WsState {session_mgr, pty_mgr, settings: SettingsState, broadcaster: WsBroadcaster, app_handle}` `:20-26`, `route_web_command` `:88-113`, `dispatch` `:118-123`, `dispatch_inner` `:125-733` whose FIRST statement (`:126-132`) returns `Err("selectionCoordinatorBusy")` for EVERY command while `RestoreInProgress` is set; a private `broadcast_all_to_managed` helper `:849-861`; helpers `require_str` `:870`, `require_json` `:914`; tests from `:943` (`ws_state_for` `:1246-1273` builds a mock app with `crate::test_support::test_builder()`, a real `WsBroadcaster` and a receiver). There is no arm for `get_agent_update_status`, `agent_update_answer`, `get_coding_agent_catalog`, or `list_reseedable_agent_commands`.
- `src-tauri/src/web/mod.rs:256-303` `handle_ws_connection`: `recv_task` runs `while let Some(Ok(msg)) = next().await { handle_text_message(..).await }`, so `commands::dispatch` is awaited SEQUENTIALLY per connection: any arm that awaited a process probe would stall every other command of that browser client (including `pty_write`) for the probe's duration. `handle_text_message` `:306-337` writes the command response through `broadcaster.broadcast_event("__cmd_response", ..)`, i.e. the SAME per-client queue as events: for one WebSocket client, an event emitted before a response was computed is delivered before that response.
- `src-tauri/src/web/event_broadcast.rs:19-27`: `broadcast_all(app: &AppHandle, broadcaster: &WsBroadcaster, event: &str, payload: &Value)` = Tauri emit + `broadcaster.broadcast_event` (frame `{"event","payload"}`, `web/broadcast.rs:45`). Its test pins the exact module set that file may name: do not edit it. `web/broadcast.rs`: `#[derive(Clone)] WsBroadcaster { senders: Arc<Mutex<Vec<mpsc::Sender<WsOutMsg>>>> }` `:17` (clones share the senders), `WS_CLIENT_QUEUE_CAP = 1024` `:4`, `subscribe()` `:36-41` (bounded tokio mpsc per client), `broadcast_event` `:45-52` is a synchronous `try_send` per client executed on the emitting thread: per-connection event order is preserved, but a full queue drops the frame AND removes that client's sender (`retain(.. try_send .. is_ok())`). Delivery is therefore ordered, not guaranteed.
- Restore runs under `RestoreInProgress` for the whole update pass whenever there are sessions to restore (`lib.rs:1085-1093`), so a browser client that cannot bypass the guard can never answer the startup prompt.
- `src/shared/transport-ws.ts:189-191` dispatches `{event, payload}` frames to `listen(event)` callbacks by name: broadcast events reach browser listeners with no client change. `src/shared/transport-tauri.ts:64-80`: `invoke` and `listen` both await `this.ready` and then call Tauri's `core.invoke` / `event.listen`; an invoke response (IPC callback) and an event (webview eval) carry no ordering guarantee relative to each other: on Tauri an older response can land after a newer event, and a response can land before an event that was emitted before the response was computed.

### 3.5 Real-machine version probes (dev box, Windows 11, warm)

`claude` -> `%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\claude.exe`, `2.1.245 (Claude Code)`, 1.07 s; `codex` -> `%APPDATA%\npm\codex.cmd`, `codex-cli 0.149.1`, 0.35 s; `pi` -> `pi.cmd`, `0.84.3`, 0.49 s; `opencode` -> `opencode.cmd`, `1.18.23`, 0.74 s; `agy` -> `%LOCALAPPDATA%\agy\bin\agy.exe`, `1.1.20`, 0.18 s; `hermes` -> `%LOCALAPPDATA%\hermes\hermes-agent\venv\Scripts\hermes.exe`, `Hermes Agent v0.17.0 (2026.6.19) ...` plus three more lines, 0.73 s. `--version` is correct for all six; output shapes are heterogeneous and every one carries its version token on the first non-empty line; the bare name `agent` resolves to grok's `agent.exe` before cursor-agent's `agent.cmd` (cursor is not listed, so it is never probed). These numbers are the authoring-time snapshot, not acceptance values (§9.4 records the actual values at test time).

Detector `repo-personal/ObsidianVault/Coding Agents/IA-Programming/rust/01-rust_module-dependency-cycles.mjs` (exit 1 = cycles exist, normal): `modulesResolved: 190`, `moduleEdges: 3696`, `moduleCycles: 1` (one 85-member SCC), `functionsResolved: 4278`; `scripts/02-module-arc-record.mjs` (`npm run record:arcs`, `package.json:28`) regenerates `src-tauri/module-arcs.txt` byte-identical (1030 lines). NOT members: `web::event_broadcast`, `web::broadcast`, `pty::job`.
