### 3.7 Testable-app conventions (for §9.4)

`docs/testing/01-project-lifecycle.md:9,:17` and `docs/testing/02-onboarding-and-coding-agents.md:5,:15,:68`: the deterministic black-box mode is `target\release\agentscommander_testeable.exe --app --ui-automation`; `agentscommander_testeable.exe test-reset --confirm-testeable` resets its disposable state and may run only while the testable GUI is not active; `window-info` identifies the target; "Product actions must be performed through the GUI. CLI is allowed only for harness control, semantic UI automation, screenshots, logs, and read-only verification"; each case records preconditions, steps, evidence, and cleanup under an evidence root in the tester's replica. The testable executable is a renamed copy of an ordinary release build, unlocked by its filename (plan #1539 §9 item 1); the executable name also selects the config directory, mutex, and ports, so the testable instance is isolated from a production instance by construction.

Build contract at 92afbfe6: `package.json:10` `build:prod:no-bundle` = `cross-env BUILD_PROFILE=prod tauri build --no-bundle --config src-tauri/tauri.prod.conf.json && node scripts/copy-testable-binary.mjs`; `package.json:11` `build:testable` = the copy script alone; `scripts/copy-testable-binary.mjs` copies `<repo>/target/release/agentscommander.exe` to `<repo>/target/release/agentscommander_testeable.exe` with `copyFile` (it fails when the source is missing and performs no hash check of its own); `src-tauri/tauri.prod.conf.json` sets `mainBinaryName: agentscommander`; the cargo target directory is the repository root `target/` (`.cargo/config.toml` sets only `jobs`; CI caches `. -> target`); `src-tauri/build.rs` embeds no git commit stamp; CI (`.github/workflows/pr-regression-gates.yml:247-250`) runs `npm run build:prod:no-bundle` and then `npm run smoke:cli-release-windows` (logs under `artifacts/cli-release-smoke`).

## 4. Scope

### In scope

1. New sink module `src-tauri/src/agent_version.rs`: built-in argv map, bounded probe runner, line-wise sanitizer and version parser (first non-empty sanitized line), session cache with TTL, single-lock probe scheduling (`lookup_or_begin`), in-flight tickets, generation-based invalidation, monotonic commit sequence.
2. Shared program resolver `config::agent_command::resolve_program` (lifted from `commands/session.rs`).
3. `agent_update.rs`: `running`, `answered` and (round 5) the pass node set `nodes` in the gate and snapshot, the catalog-order node builder `pass_nodes`, the `command_skipped` event and its `skip_prompted_target` helper in the prompt loop, the pre-update install probe inside `run_update_target`, the post-pass probe scheduling `schedule_post_update_probes` (§5.4 step 9b), `is_finished` accessor, per-command started/finished events, dual-transport `emit_all` for every event (the prompt-phase emits included), `agent_update_prompt_closed` carrying the closed command, the serialized answer flow `answer_prompt` (prompt state machine: first persisted answer wins, superseded answers return `false` without persisting, late answers after the timeout keep the accepted semantics; closure ownership: whoever removes the pending entry inside the gate's serial section, the answer's claim or the loop's expiry, emits the single closure, and an answer emits it before releasing the loop), `run_update_target` wrapper plus `settle_joined_update` for panicked tasks, overview row builder, overview orchestration with injectable probe that schedules probes only after the pass is finished and only through the single-lock cache operation, the pass-end transition `finish_pass` (invalidate, then mark finished, then emit), the probe task body, a scrape test that pins "no emit outside `emit_all`".
4. `commands/config.rs`: `get_agent_update_overview` command and `_inner` helpers for the WebSocket router (the answer helper delegates to `answer_prompt` with the existing persist function injected); `lib.rs`: module, managed cache, handler registration.
5. `web/commands.rs`: five agent-update/catalog arms routed BEFORE the `RestoreInProgress` guard.
6. Frontend: types and IPC wrappers (`prompt_closed` payload, `answered` snapshot field), `agentUpdateStore` extension with a monotonic snapshot merge that covers `running`, `results`, `inProgress`, and closed prompts, conditional prompt clears (a closure or a completed answer clears only the prompt it names), overview store with per-mount freshness, trailing single-flight refresh, sequence-ordered install-state merge and a bounded re-poll, pure row derivation, `AgentAutoUpdateStatusList` component mounted in the Coding Agents screen (session opened synchronously in the component body), the Option 2 overlay (header with counter and progress bar, staged timeline, per-node command, version transition and inline error, persistent summary with explicit close, §5.13), the store's `nodes`/`skippedNodes`/`installAfter`/`summary` state, the monotonic snapshot merge of §5.9 and the `command_skipped`/`install_state_changed` listeners (§5.9), the pure timeline derivations (§5.10), the overlay's `false`-answer toast read from the persisted policy, CSS (§5.14), tests.
7. Docs: `docs/features/agent-auto-update.md`, `docs/integrations/coding-agents.md`, `docs/reference/settings.md`, `docs/reference/architecture.md` (two table rows), the stale `updateCommands` comment in `src/shared/types.ts`.
8. `src-tauri/module-arcs.txt` regenerated (seven new lines: the six new arcs plus the `lib -> agent_version` line of the §5.7 spelling, §11).

### Out of scope (binding)

- No manual "update now" trigger, no version comparison / "update available" logic, no Gemini catalog row, no catalog schema field (no `versionCommand`), no `agents.json` write or migration, no `settings.json` schema change, no CLI verb, no version bump, no release note requirement.
- No change to `build_update_plan`, `run_update_sequence`, the prompt phase's structure (sequential, catalog order, one prompt per command per boot, `PROMPT_TIMEOUT` 60 s, default No on timeout), the 60 s / 300 s constants, the gate wait in `create_session_inner_impl`, the overlay's blocking semantics, or the Enter/Escape prompt semantics (Enter/Escape still answer `No` while a prompt is displayed on that surface). What DOES change in the prompt phase is limited to §5.4 steps 3 and 9: its emits go through `emit_all`, `agent_update_prompt_closed` carries the command, the two arms that drop a prompted target (answer `No`, expiry) also emit `agent_update_command_skipped` (round 5), and the answer command is redesigned. Round 5 adds no per-step progress event: `run_update_sequence` stays byte-identical and the timeline shows a running agent's whole update sequence as configured.
- No change to the #1345 dropdown, `setAgentAutoUpdate`, `saveCurrentSettingsDraft`, `persist_narrow_settings_update` (still the only settings writer of the answer path), `web/event_broadcast.rs`, `web/broadcast.rs` (its queue-full behavior is pre-existing and accepted; §7 heals it).
- The wire result of `agent_update_answer` stays a `boolean` (coordinator decision, round 3): a superseded or late answer is `false`; the persisted policy is exposed additively through the snapshot instead.
- No i18n layer: Settings copy stays English, overlay copy stays Spanish (dispatch decision).
- No update history, timestamps, or persistence of results; results are process-scoped exactly as today.
- No persisted "last known version": the version transition of a finished node comes from two live probes of this boot (before and after the update, §5.4 steps 5 and 9b); with no probe source (explicit path, unknown stem) the node shows no version text, never a guessed one. No prototype artifact enters the repository: `index-V1.html` and the `html-component-blink-debug` skill are review aids outside this delivery (no file, dependency, bundle entry, or test references them).
- No renaming, moving, or editing of any user-installed binary or npm shim by any agent (§9.4 uses process-level `PATH` and disposable stubs only), and no vendor CLI is ever updated by the validation (every real prompt is answered `No`).

## 5. Decided solution

### 5.1 Wire contract (single source of truth for both implementers)

Commands (Tauri `invoke` name == WebSocket `cmd`):

| Command | Args | Result | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `get_agent_update_status` | none | `AgentUpdateStatus` | unchanged name; response gains `running` and `answered` |
| `agent_update_answer` | `{command: string, enabled: boolean}` | `boolean` | unchanged args and result type; now also routed over WebSocket; result semantics below |
| `get_agent_update_overview` | none | `AgentUpdateOverviewRow[]` | NEW; instant; schedules probes in the background only once the startup pass is finished |
| `get_coding_agent_catalog` | none | `CodingAgentDefinition[]` | unchanged; now routed over WebSocket |
| `list_reseedable_agent_commands` | none | `string[]` | unchanged; now routed over WebSocket |

`agent_update_answer` result semantics (exact): `true` iff THIS call resolved the pending prompt of `command` (its choice was persisted and this boot acts on it). `false` in exactly two cases: (a) the prompt of `command` had already expired (60 s timeout, or the pass ended) and no answer had been recorded yet: the choice IS persisted for future boots, this boot is unaffected (the accepted late-answer semantics); (b) an answer for `command` was already recorded this boot (by any surface, resolving or late): NOTHING is persisted, the earlier answer stands. Errors: `'<command>' was not prompted this boot` (never persisted), or the persist error string (nothing changed; the prompt stays pending and the call may be retried).

JSON shapes (Rust serde `camelCase`; TS interfaces in §5.8):

```
AgentUpdateCommandRef  { command: string, label: string }
AgentUpdateNode        { command: string, label: string, updateCommands: string[], installBefore?: InstallState }   (round 5: one agent of this boot's pass, in pass order; installBefore present once its pre-update probe ran)
AgentUpdateStatus      { inProgress: boolean, prompt: AgentUpdatePrompt | null, results: AgentUpdateResult[], running: AgentUpdateCommandRef[], answered: Record<string, boolean>, nodes: AgentUpdateNode[] }
InstallStatus          "checking" | "missing" | "installed" | "probeFailed" | "unprobed"
InstallState           { status: InstallStatus, version?: string, path?: string, detail?: string, seq: number }   (options absent when None; seq always present)
AgentUpdateOverviewRow { key, label, command, color: string, updateCommands: string[], install: InstallState }
AgentInstallStateChanged { command: string, install: InstallState }
```

`answered` = the policy recorded by the answer that won for each prompted command this boot (resolving or late), keyed by command; empty until an answer is recorded; never cleared during the process (a late answer after the pass is recorded too). It is the value the overlay reads to explain a `false` result (§5.13).

`nodes` (round 5) = the agents of this boot's pass in pass order (catalog order, §3.1 `build_update_plan`): every target with a decided `true` policy plus every prompted target, set when the pass starts, pruned of a prompted target the moment its prompt is answered `No` or expires, never re-ordered, never cleared by `mark_finished` (the summary keeps the order). Empty before the pass and on a quiet boot. `installBefore` is the install state read by the pre-update probe of that node (§5.4 step 5), `seq: 0` because it is never cached.

Events (every one emitted through `emit_all`, i.e. Tauri windows AND WebSocket clients):

| Event | Payload | When |
|---|---|---|
| `agent_updates_started` | `{ nodes: AgentUpdateNode[] }` (was `null`) | round 5: carries the pass nodes so every mounted surface can paint the timeline at once; the only consumer at 92afbfe6 (`onAgentUpdatesStarted`, §3.4) ignores the payload, so the change is compatible |
| `agent_update_prompt` | `AgentUpdatePrompt` | unchanged: a prompt became pending (one per command per boot, sequential) |
| `agent_update_prompt_closed` | `AgentUpdateCommandRef` (was `null`) | every time a prompt stops being pending, emitted exactly once by whoever removed the pending entry inside the gate's serial section: the answer that claimed it (`answer_prompt`, §5.4 step 9, which emits BEFORE releasing the prompt loop) or the loop's own expiry after 60 s. Carries the closed prompt's command and label so a client clears only the prompt it names; the only existing consumer (`onAgentUpdatePromptClosed`) ignores the payload today, so the change is compatible |
| `agent_update_command_skipped` | `AgentUpdateCommandRef` | NEW (round 5): a prompted target left the pass because its prompt was answered `No` (persisted `false`) or expired (60 s, default No). Emitted by the prompt loop only (the single owner of this boot's decision), in program order after that prompt's `agent_update_prompt_closed` and before the next `agent_update_prompt`; never for a `true` answer, never for a target that was never prompted |
| `agent_update_command_started` | `AgentUpdateNode` | NEW: a command's update sequence starts; the payload is that node with `installBefore` filled by the pre-update probe that just finished (round 5) |
| `agent_update_command_finished` | `AgentUpdateResult` | NEW: that sequence ended (ok, failed, or panicked) |
| `agent_updates_finished` | `{results: AgentUpdateResult[]}` | unchanged; now emitted strictly AFTER the install cache was invalidated AND the gate was marked finished (§5.4 step 4) |
| `agent_install_state_changed` | `AgentInstallStateChanged` | NEW: a probe result was committed to the install cache (a Settings-triggered probe, or, round 5, the post-pass probe the backend schedules for every updated command right after `agent_updates_finished`, §5.4 step 9b) |

Ordering guarantee per boot: `agent_updates_started` (carrying `nodes`); then, per prompted command in catalog order, `agent_update_prompt(A)` strictly before `agent_update_prompt_closed(A)`, `agent_update_prompt_closed(A)` strictly before `agent_update_command_skipped(A)` when A leaves the pass (the loop emits the skip after the closure, in program order, in both the `No` arm and the expiry arm), and both strictly before `agent_update_prompt(B)` of the next prompted command: the loop registers and emits B only after it was released, and it is released either by its own expiry arm (which emits the closure of A itself, in program order before B) or by the answer that claimed A (which emits the closure BEFORE the `deliver` that releases the loop); every closure emission runs inside the gate's serial section (`answer_serial`), so on each WebSocket client queue and in the backend's emit order the closure of A precedes the prompt of B; then for each updated command `agent_update_command_started` strictly before its `agent_update_command_finished` (different commands interleave); then exactly one `agent_updates_finished` after every `command_finished`. Exactly one `agent_update_prompt_closed` is emitted per prompted command per boot: the pending entry is removed exactly once (by the answer's claim or by the loop's expiry, both under the gate's state lock inside the serial section) and only the remover emits (§5.4 step 9). Tauri's own event delivery is not assumed to preserve order: a client clears a shown prompt on a closure only when the payload names that prompt's command (an older backend's `null` payload clears unconditionally, which is safe because such a backend emits the closure only from the loop, before any next prompt). `agent_install_state_changed` is emitted exactly once per COMMITTED cache state (§5.2) and carries that state's `seq`; it follows an overview call, or (round 5) the post-pass scheduling that runs after `agent_updates_finished` in the pass task's program order, so every post-pass install event of an updated command is delivered after `agent_updates_finished` on each WebSocket queue and after `finished` became observable in the backend.

`InstallState` meaning: `checking` = no committed state for the command yet (a probe is scheduled, in flight, or deferred until the pass finishes); `missing` = the command token does not resolve to a file; `installed` = resolved and the probe returned a version; `probeFailed` = resolved but the probe failed (non-zero exit, spawn error, timeout, or no version token); `unprobed` = resolved but deliberately not executed (explicit-path command, or no built-in probe for that program). `path` is the resolved program for every state except `checking`/`missing`; `detail` is a bounded, sanitized diagnostic (§5.2). `seq` is the install cache's commit counter for that state: `0` for `checking`, `>= 1` for every committed state, strictly increasing across commits for the life of the backend process and never reset by invalidation. Clients apply an incoming install state to a row only when its `seq` is greater than the row's current `seq` (§5.11); that single rule makes the response/event merge independent of delivery order.

### 5.2 `src-tauri/src/agent_version.rs` (new; owner ac-dev-rust-v3)

Module doc (verbatim intent): `//! #1551 - installed-version probe engine and session cache for coding-agent CLIs. Deliberately a SINK: it names no crate module except pty::job, so the commands/config, agent_update and web SCC does not grow (plan #1551 §11). Callers resolve programs and build rows; this module only runs <program> <fixed argv> with a bound, parses, sanitizes, and caches.`

Constants:

```rust
pub const PROBE_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(15);
pub const INSTALL_CACHE_TTL: Duration = Duration::from_secs(600);
pub const PROBE_OUTPUT_CAP: usize = 4 * 1024;      // bytes retained per stream
pub const DETAIL_MAX_CHARS: usize = 160;           // sanitized diagnostic length
const READER_JOIN_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(5);
```

Built-in argv map (the ONLY strings ever placed in probe argv):

```rust
/// #1551 - fixed probe argv per known program stem (lowercase, extension stripped).
/// No catalog/user/project string ever reaches argv. Cursor (`agent`) is absent on
/// purpose: it has no update command and its bare name collides with other vendors.
pub fn version_probe_args(program_stem: &str) -> Option<&'static [&'static str]> {
    match program_stem {
        "claude" | "codex" | "hermes" | "pi" | "opencode" | "agy" => Some(&["--version"]),
        _ => None,
    }
}
```

Types: `InstallStatus` (`#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]`, variants `Checking, Missing, Installed, ProbeFailed, Unprobed`; derive `Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize`) and

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
pub struct InstallState {
    pub status: InstallStatus,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] pub version: Option<String>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] pub path: Option<String>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] pub detail: Option<String>,
    /// #1551 - commit counter of the install cache that stored this state; 0 until
    /// `ProbeTicket::complete` commits it (so `checking` and un-committed probe results
    /// are 0). Clients keep the highest `seq` per command (plan §5.11).
    #[serde(default)] pub seq: u64,
}
```

with constructors `checking()`, `missing(detail: String)`, `installed(version: String, path: &Path)`, `probe_failed(path: &Path, detail: String)`, `unprobed(path: &Path, detail: String)` (`path` stored as `path.display().to_string()`; every constructor sets `seq: 0`).

Text helpers (one line-wise algorithm shared by parsing and diagnostics; exact, no other rule):

- `fn strip_ansi_and_controls(line: &str) -> String` (private): remove every ANSI CSI sequence (`ESC [` followed by parameter/intermediate bytes `0x20-0x3F` and one final byte `0x40-0x7E`) and every OSC sequence (`ESC ]` up to and including `BEL` or `ESC \`); a lone `ESC` or an unterminated sequence is removed up to the end of the line; then drop every remaining control character (`< 0x20` and `0x7F`); then `trim()`.
- `pub fn text_lines(text: &str) -> impl Iterator<Item = String> + '_`: split `text` on every `\n` and every `\r` (each is a line break, so `\r\n` yields an empty line between), map each piece through `strip_ansi_and_controls`, and yield ONLY the non-empty results, in order. Empty lines and lines that become empty after stripping (an ANSI-only line such as `ESC[?25l`) are skipped, never terminate the scan.
- `pub fn first_text_line(text: &str) -> Option<String>`: `text_lines(text).next()`.
- `pub fn parse_version_token(text: &str) -> Option<String>`: `first_text_line(text)` and then `Regex::new(r"\bv?(\d+(?:\.\d+)+)")` (compiled once via `std::sync::OnceLock`) on THAT line only, returning capture 1; `None` when there is no text line or the first text line carries no token (a banner line before the version is reported as `no version in output: <that line>`; every built-in of §3.5 prints its token on its first text line). Fixtures that must hold: `2.1.245 (Claude Code)` -> `2.1.245`; `codex-cli 0.149.1` -> `0.149.1`; `0.84.3` -> `0.84.3`; `Hermes Agent v0.17.0 (2026.6.19) · upstream` -> `0.17.0`; `1.1.20` -> `1.1.20`; `\n\n  1.18.23\n` -> `1.18.23`; `\r\n\r\n1.2.3\r\n` -> `1.2.3`; `\n\x1b[?25l\n2.0.1\n` -> `2.0.1`; `\x1b[32mv1.2.3\x1b[0m\nsecond` -> `1.2.3`; `hello\n1.2.3` -> `None`; `hello world` -> `None`; `""` -> `None`; `"\x1b[?25l\n"` -> `None`.
- `pub fn sanitize_detail(raw: &[u8]) -> String`: decode lossy UTF-8, take `first_text_line` (empty string when there is none), then truncate to `DETAIL_MAX_CHARS` characters (char boundary; append `...` when truncated). Never returns more than one line; callers substitute `<empty>` / `<no output>` when it is empty. Fixtures: `"\x1b[32mv1.2.3\x1b[0m\nsecond"` -> `v1.2.3`; `"\n\x1b[?25l\n2.0.1\n"` -> `2.0.1`; 400 non-blank chars -> 160 chars + `...`; `""` -> `""`.

Probe runner:

```rust
pub enum ProbeOutcome { Version(String), Failed(String) }   // Failed carries the sanitized detail
pub async fn probe_version(program: &Path, args: &[&str], timeout: Duration) -> ProbeOutcome
```

Spawn discipline (mirrors `run_update_sequence` `:315-345` by design; the runner is not reused because it executes shell strings, is pinned by six tests, and has a different failure contract): `tokio::process::Command::new(program)` + `.args(args)` (argv, never a shell string; std runs `.cmd`/`.bat` through `cmd.exe` with its own escaping, and `--version` is a fixed literal), `.stdin(Stdio::null())`, `.stdout(piped)`, `.stderr(piped)`, `.kill_on_drop(true)`, `#[cfg(windows)] creation_flags(0x0800_0000)` (mandatory: without it six console windows flash on every refresh), `#[cfg(unix)] process_group(0)`, no `current_dir` (inherits the daemon's cwd; `--version` does not care). After `spawn`: Windows `let job = child.id().and_then(crate::pty::job::JobObject::for_child);` (`child.id()` is `Option<u32>`; `for_child` takes `u32`, `pty/job.rs:69`; the `Option<JobObject>` is held across the awaits inside the task exactly as the runner does); two reader tasks that drain each pipe to EOF but retain only the first `PROBE_OUTPUT_CAP` bytes; `tokio::time::timeout(timeout, child.wait())`:

- `Ok(Ok(status))`: join readers bounded by `READER_JOIN_TIMEOUT` (on a join timeout drop the job / group-kill exactly as the runner does and join again, else empty buffers). `status.success()` -> `parse_version_token(&String::from_utf8_lossy(&stdout))`, falling back to `parse_version_token` over stderr when stdout yields no token; `Some(v)` -> `Version(v)`, `None` -> `Failed(format!("no version in output: {}", sanitize_detail(&stdout) or "<empty>"))`. Non-zero -> `Failed(format!("exit code {}: {}", code or -1, sanitize_detail(&stderr), else sanitize_detail(&stdout), else "<no output>"))`.
- `Ok(Err(e))` -> `Failed(format!("spawn failed: {e}"))`.
- `Err(_)` (timeout): `job.terminate()`, `child.kill().await`, Unix `libc::kill(-pid, SIGKILL)`, bounded `child.wait()`, bounded reader join, then `Failed(format!("timed out after {}s (killed)", timeout.as_secs()))`.

Logging: `log::info!("[agent-version] {} -> {} version {}", program.display(), ..)` on success; `log::warn!("[agent-version] probe failed for {}: {detail}", ..)` on failure; never the full output.

Cache (std `Mutex`, never held across an await; `#[derive(Default)]` on both structs so `new()` is `Self::default()` and `clippy::new_without_default` is satisfied under `-D warnings`):

```rust
#[derive(Default)]
pub struct AgentInstallCache { inner: Mutex<CacheInner> }
#[derive(Default)]
struct CacheInner { generation: u64, seq: u64, entries: HashMap<String, CachedInstall>, in_flight: HashSet<String> }
struct CachedInstall { probed_at: Instant, state: InstallState }
pub enum CacheLookup { Fresh(InstallState), InFlight, Absent }
/// #1551 - result of the single-lock scheduling operation.
pub enum Scheduling { Fresh(InstallState), InFlight, Began(ProbeTicket), Deferred }
pub struct ProbeTicket { cache: Arc<AgentInstallCache>, command: String, generation: u64, live: bool }
/// #1551 - result of closing a ticket: the state is committed to the current generation,
/// or the cache was invalidated meanwhile and the SAME ticket continues (renewed) for a retry.
pub enum Completion { Committed(InstallState), Stale(ProbeTicket) }

impl AgentInstallCache {
    pub fn new() -> Self { Self::default() }
    /// Read-only. Fresh iff an entry exists and `now.duration_since(probed_at) <= ttl` (the stored state, seq included);
    /// else InFlight iff a ticket is open for `command`; else Absent (an expired entry is removed on the way).
    pub fn lookup(&self, command: &str, now: Instant, ttl: Duration) -> CacheLookup;
    /// #1551 - the ONLY production scheduling primitive: ONE critical section decides between serving a fresh
    /// entry, reporting an open ticket, opening the ticket (iff `may_begin`; `in_flight` insert + generation
    /// capture in the same lock take), or deferring. Two concurrent callers can never both receive `Began` for
    /// the same command, and a commit that lands before this call is always served as `Fresh`: there is no
    /// observable state between "looked up" and "began".
    pub fn lookup_or_begin(self: &Arc<Self>, command: &str, now: Instant, ttl: Duration, may_begin: bool) -> Scheduling;
    /// #1551 - after a startup pass every entry is stale: `generation += 1`, `entries.clear()`. Tickets stay open
    /// (their next `complete` returns `Stale`) and `seq` is NOT reset, so every post-invalidation commit outranks
    /// every pre-invalidation state on the clients.
    pub fn invalidate_all(&self);
    /// Test seeding only (opens a ticket without consulting entries); production never schedules through it.
    #[cfg(test)] pub fn try_begin(self: &Arc<Self>, command: &str) -> Option<ProbeTicket>;
    #[cfg(test)] pub fn generation(&self) -> u64;
    #[cfg(test)] pub fn seq(&self) -> u64;
    #[cfg(test)] pub fn in_flight_len(&self) -> usize;
}
impl ProbeTicket {
    /// Under the cache lock: if `generation` still matches, `seq += 1`, stamp `state.seq`, insert the entry
    /// (`probed_at = now`), remove `command` from `in_flight`, set `live = false`, return `Committed(stamped)`.
    /// Otherwise adopt the current generation and return `Stale(self)`: the ticket is NEVER released between
    /// the rejection and the retry, so no competing `lookup_or_begin` can take the slot in between.
    pub fn complete(mut self, state: InstallState) -> Completion;
}
impl Drop for ProbeTicket {
    /// Removes `command` from `in_flight` ONLY while `live` (an un-completed ticket: task panicked, aborted,
    /// or a second `Stale` was dropped). A committed ticket already cleared `live`, so its drop can never free
    /// the slot of a later ticket for the same command.
}
```

`lookup_or_begin` decision order inside its single lock take: (1) entry present and fresh -> `Fresh(state)`; (2) entry present and expired -> remove it and continue; (3) `in_flight` contains `command` -> `InFlight`; (4) `may_begin` -> insert into `in_flight`, capture `generation`, `Began(ticket)`; (5) else `Deferred`. Keys are the catalog `command` strings (the update unit, like `agentAutoUpdateByCommand`). Invariant used by §5.11's proof: a state is inserted only by a commit, every commit increments `seq`, and `seq` survives invalidation; hence "higher `seq`" == "committed later", for the whole process lifetime, across every command. Single-flight invariant (R3-M3): at most one open ticket per command at any time, because every ticket is opened inside the cache lock by `lookup_or_begin` (or the test-only `try_begin`) only when `in_flight` does not contain the command, and a slot is freed only by a commit or by the drop of a live ticket.

### 5.3 Shared program resolver: `src-tauri/src/config/agent_command.rs` and `commands/session.rs`

Add to `agent_command.rs`, immediately before `pub struct NormalizedAgentCommand` (`:23`):

```rust
/// #1551 - true when `token` is a bare program name (no path separator, not absolute):
/// the only form resolved through PATH, and the only form the version probe executes.
pub fn is_bare_program_token(token: &str) -> bool {
    !(token.contains('/') || token.contains('\\') || Path::new(token).is_absolute())
}

/// #1551 - resolve a program token to a file. Lifted byte-equivalently from the
/// `resolve_token_to_file` helper that `commands::session` used for the claude token:
/// explicit path (separator or absolute) -> Some iff it is a file, never consulting
/// PATH; bare name -> `which::which` (PATH, plus PATHEXT on Windows, so npm `.cmd`
/// shims resolve). The GUI process PATH is what is consulted (documented caveat).
pub fn resolve_program(token: &str) -> Option<PathBuf> {
    let p = Path::new(token);
    if !is_bare_program_token(token) {
        return if p.is_file() { Some(p.to_path_buf()) } else { None };
    }
    which::which(token).ok()
}
```

(`std::path::{Path, PathBuf}` is already imported at `:2`.)

`commands/session.rs`: delete the nested `fn resolve_token_to_file` (`:750-764`, including its comment) and change the call at `:792` to `crate::config::agent_command::resolve_program(&claude_token)`. `looks_like_wrapper_extension` (`:740-748`) and everything else in that function stay byte-identical. `which` remains a crate dependency (now used from `agent_command.rs`).

Tests (`agent_command.rs` tests module): `resolve_program_explicit_path_requires_a_file` (tempfile path -> `Some`; `tempdir/missing.exe` -> `None`; the tempdir itself -> `None`), `resolve_program_bare_name_uses_path` (`cmd` on Windows / `sh` elsewhere -> `Some(path)` whose file stem equals the token, case-insensitive), `resolve_program_bare_unknown_is_none` (`definitely-not-a-program-1551` -> `None`), `is_bare_program_token_cases` (`claude` true; `./claude`, `C:\x\claude.exe`, `/usr/bin/claude`, `bin\claude` false).

### 5.4 `src-tauri/src/agent_update.rs` (owner ac-dev-rust-v3)

Imports to add after `:30` (exactly the names the new code uses, no more: clippy runs with `-D warnings`): from `crate::agent_version` the items among `probe_version, version_probe_args, AgentInstallCache, Completion, InstallState, ProbeOutcome, ProbeTicket, Scheduling, INSTALL_CACHE_TTL, PROBE_TIMEOUT` that this section references; `crate::config::agent_command::{is_bare_program_token, normalize_legacy_agent_command, resolve_program}`; `crate::web::broadcast::WsBroadcaster`; `crate::web::event_broadcast::broadcast_all`; plus `std::path::Path` (needed by step 7; only `PathBuf` is imported today), `std::future::Future` (steps 8 and 9), `std::pin::Pin` (step 8), `std::time::Instant`, `serde_json::{json, Value}`, and `crate::config::coding_agents_catalog::CodingAgentDefinition`. `tokio::sync::Mutex` is used fully qualified (step 1) so it cannot be confused with the std `Mutex` already imported.

1. **Types.** After `AgentUpdatePrompt` (`:73`): `pub struct AgentUpdateCommandRef { pub command: String, pub label: String }` (derive `Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize`, camelCase). `pub struct AgentUpdateNode { pub command: String, pub label: String, pub update_commands: Vec<String>, #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] pub install_before: Option<InstallState> }` (round 5; derive `Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize`, camelCase; doc `/// #1551 - one agent of this boot's pass in pass order; install_before is the pre-update probe result once it ran (never cached, seq 0).`). `AgentUpdateStatus` (`:57-65`) gains `/// #1551 - commands whose update sequence is running, in start order.` `pub running: Vec<AgentUpdateCommandRef>` and `/// #1551 - policy recorded by the winning answer per prompted command this boot (resolving or late).` `pub answered: BTreeMap<String, bool>`, and (round 5) `/// #1551 - the agents of this boot's pass in pass order (catalog order); pruned of prompts answered No or expired; kept after the pass for the summary.` `pub nodes: Vec<AgentUpdateNode>`. A private `struct PendingPrompt { tx: tokio::sync::oneshot::Sender<bool>, label: String }` replaces the bare sender: `GateState` (`:82-92`) becomes `{started, finished, results, prompted: HashSet<String>, pending: HashMap<String, PendingPrompt>, pending_prompt: Option<AgentUpdatePrompt>, running: Vec<AgentUpdateCommandRef>, answered: BTreeMap<String, bool>, nodes: Vec<AgentUpdateNode>}` (new fields initialised empty in `new`). `AgentUpdateGate` (`:77-80`) gains `/// #1551 - serializes `answer_prompt` (classify -> persist -> settle) per gate. A tokio mutex because it is held across the persist await; the std `state` lock is never held across an await.` `answer_serial: tokio::sync::Mutex<()>` (initialised with `tokio::sync::Mutex::new(())` in `new`). Public items after the gate: `pub enum PromptState { NotPrompted, Pending, Expired, Answered(bool) }` (derive `Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq`); `pub struct ClaimedPrompt { closed: AgentUpdateCommandRef, tx: tokio::sync::oneshot::Sender<bool> }` (private fields; `pub fn closed(&self) -> &AgentUpdateCommandRef`; `pub fn deliver(self, enabled: bool) -> bool` = `self.tx.send(enabled).is_ok()`, the ONLY way to release the prompt loop from a claim, consumed exactly once); `pub enum AnswerClaim { Claimed(ClaimedPrompt), Recorded }`. After `AgentUpdatePlan` (`:236`): `pub struct AgentUpdateOverviewRow { pub key, pub label, pub command, pub color: String, pub update_commands: Vec<String>, pub install: InstallState }` (derive `Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize`, camelCase).

2. **Gate methods** (inside `impl AgentUpdateGate`):
   - `register_prompt` (`:119-134`): stores `PendingPrompt { tx, label: label.to_string() }`; signature and every other line unchanged.
   - A private locked helper `fn take_pending(state: &mut GateState, command: &str) -> Option<PendingPrompt>`: removes the pending entry and clears `pending_prompt` when it names `command` (the exact body that `resolve_answer` `:157-166` and `drop_pending` `:140-147` share today).
   - `resolve_answer(&self, command, enabled) -> bool` (`:155-168`): keeps its signature and semantics byte-for-byte (`take_pending` then `p.tx.send(enabled).is_ok()`, else `false`); it does NOT record `answered` and production never calls it after this change (the three tests of §3.1 still pin it; it stays `pub`, so no dead-code warning).
   - `drop_pending(&self, command: &str) -> Option<AgentUpdateCommandRef>` (`:138-148`): body delegates to `take_pending` and returns `Some(AgentUpdateCommandRef { command, label })` iff THIS call removed the pending entry (the caller then owns the single closure of that prompt and must emit it), `None` otherwise; the sender is dropped either way. The existing test calls it as a statement and stays green (`Option` is not `#[must_use]`).
   - `/// #1551 - one lock take, never sends. Records the persisted policy and, when the prompt is still pending, CLAIMS it: the entry leaves the map here, so the closure has exactly one owner, and the sender is handed back unsent so the caller can emit the closure BEFORE releasing the loop.` `pub fn claim_answer(&self, command: &str, enabled: bool) -> AnswerClaim`: `let taken = take_pending(&mut state, command); state.answered.insert(command.to_string(), enabled); match taken { Some(p) => AnswerClaim::Claimed(ClaimedPrompt { closed: AgentUpdateCommandRef { command: command.to_string(), label: p.label }, tx: p.tx }), None => AnswerClaim::Recorded }`. `Recorded` covers "no pending entry": expired by `drop_pending` (the loop's expiry arm) or `mark_finished`, or claimed earlier. A claimed entry whose receiver already died (the `timeout` future dropped `rx` an instant before the expiry arm could take the serial section) is still `Claimed`: the caller emits the closure and `deliver` reports `false`. It records UNCONDITIONALLY, which is why only `answer_prompt` may call it in production, after classifying inside the serial section.
   - `/// #1551 - classification for `answer_prompt`, one lock take.` `pub fn prompt_state(&self, command: &str) -> PromptState`: `answered.get(command)` -> `Answered(*v)`; else `pending.contains_key(command)` -> `Pending`; else `prompted.contains(command)` -> `Expired`; else `NotPrompted`. (`answered` is checked first: once a policy is recorded, no further answer may persist.)
   - `pub fn mark_command_started(&self, command: &str, label: &str, install_before: Option<InstallState>) -> AgentUpdateNode` (round-5 signature): in ONE lock take, push `AgentUpdateCommandRef` onto `running` unless an entry with that `command` exists, store `install_before` into the node of `nodes` with that `command` (when the node exists and the argument is `Some`), and return a clone of that node (or, when no node carries the command, a synthesized `AgentUpdateNode { command, label, update_commands: vec![], install_before }` that is NOT inserted: production always has the node, step 3); the return value is the `agent_update_command_started` payload.
   - `pub fn mark_command_finished(&self, result: AgentUpdateResult)`: in ONE lock take (Rust R6): remove the `running` entry with `result.command`, then replace the `results` entry with the same `command` if present, else push. No `snapshot()` can observe a command in both `running` and `results`. Mid-pass `snapshot().results` therefore means "finished so far".
   - `/// #1551 - round 5: the pass starts with its node set in ONE lock take, so no snapshot can observe started without nodes.` `pub fn mark_started_with_nodes(&self, nodes: Vec<AgentUpdateNode>)`: sets `started = true` and REPLACES `nodes`; `mark_started` (`:109-114`) stays byte-identical (it is `mark_started_with_nodes(vec![])` in effect and keeps the existing tests green).
   - `/// #1551 - round 5: a prompted target leaves the pass (answer No, or expiry). Returns the ref iff THIS call removed the node, so the loop emits agent_update_command_skipped exactly once per skipped command.` `pub fn mark_command_skipped(&self, command: &str) -> Option<AgentUpdateCommandRef>`: removes the first node with that `command` from `nodes` and returns `Some(AgentUpdateCommandRef { command, label })`, `None` when absent. It never touches `running`, `results`, `prompted`, `pending`, or `answered`.
   - `/// #1551 - true once `mark_finished` ran: at the end of a real pass, or at the quiet-boot guard drop. Never reset.` `pub fn is_finished(&self) -> bool` (reads `state.finished`).
   - `mark_finished` (`:183-192`): additionally `state.running.clear()` (a panic or lost event can never leave a stuck `Updating...`); it still clears `pending` (dropping the senders) and `pending_prompt`, REPLACES `results` with the final vector (existing test `gate_prompt_registry_and_snapshot` keeps its "replaces results" assertion), and leaves `prompted`, `answered` and (round 5) `nodes` untouched (a late answer after the pass is `Expired`, persists, and is recorded; a second one is `Answered`; the nodes keep the summary's order and their `install_before`).
   - `snapshot` (`:194-201`): add `running: state.running.clone()`, `answered: state.answered.clone()` and `nodes: state.nodes.clone()`.
   - `was_prompted` (`:170-176`) unchanged (still `pub`; no production caller after this change).

3. **Dual-transport emit** (private, placed before `FinishGuard`):

```rust
/// #1551 - every agent-update event reaches Tauri windows AND WebSocket clients.
/// The broadcaster is managed by `lib.rs` before `setup`; the fallback keeps unit
/// tests that build a bare mock app working. The scrape test
/// `agent_update_emits_only_through_emit_all` (step 10) pins that this is the only
/// `.emit(` call site of the production part of this file.
fn emit_all(app: &AppHandle, event: &str, payload: Value) {
    match app.try_state::<WsBroadcaster>() {
        Some(broadcaster) => broadcast_all(app, &broadcaster, event, &payload),
        None => {
            let _ = app.emit(event, payload);
        }
    }
}
```

   Replace the four legacy `app.emit(..)` calls: `:625-626` -> `let nodes = pass_nodes(&catalog, &plan); gate.mark_started_with_nodes(nodes.clone()); emit_all(&app, "agent_updates_started", json!({ "nodes": nodes }));` (round 5: `mark_started()` at `:625` is replaced by the node-carrying variant; `pass_nodes` is the pure builder of step 5a and reads `plan` before the loop moves `plan.updates`; the emit runs after the gate holds the nodes, so a client that reacts to the event with a snapshot sees them); `:649-655` -> `emit_all(&app, "agent_update_prompt", json!(AgentUpdatePrompt { command: pending.command.clone(), label: pending.label.clone() }))`; the timeout arm `:665-671` -> `{ let _serial = gate.answer_serial.lock().await; if let Some(closed) = gate.drop_pending(&pending.command) { emit_all(&app, "agent_update_prompt_closed", json!(closed)); } } skip_prompted_target(&app, &gate, &pending.command);` (P7: the expiry competes with answer claims for the single pending entry inside the same serial section as `answer_prompt`, §5.4 step 9; it emits the closure only when it removed the entry; no await between the removal and the emit; the skip helper of step 5a runs AFTER the serial block, outside the lock, so the closure of A precedes the skip of A in program order; the `Ok(Ok(true))` arm `:663` is unchanged, and the `Ok(Ok(false))` arm `:664` becomes `skip_prompted_target(&app, &gate, &pending.command)` (round 5): the answer that released the loop already emitted the closure, and the loop, the only owner of this boot's decision, emits the skip); `FinishGuard::drop` `:580-582` -> the step-4 helper below. `agent_update_prompt` payload bytes are unchanged (`&AgentUpdatePrompt { .. }` and `json!(AgentUpdatePrompt { .. })` serialize identically); `agent_updates_started` changes from `null` to `{ nodes }` (round 5, §5.1) and `agent_update_prompt_closed` from `null` to the command reference (§5.1, §8); the `started` payload and the observable order of the whole pass are pinned end to end by `run_startup_updates_emits_started_with_nodes_then_command_events_then_finished_then_post_probe` (step 10, round 6).

4. **Pass-end transition: invalidate, THEN finish, THEN announce** (R3-M4; supersedes the round-2 `invalidate_cache_then`). New private items, placed before `FinishGuard`:

```rust
/// #1551 - the two effects of the pass end, named fields so `FinishGuard::drop` cannot
/// pass them in the wrong slots.
struct PassEnd<I: FnOnce(), E: FnOnce()> { invalidate: I, emit: E }

/// #1551 - the ONLY transition to `finished`. Order is load-bearing (happens-before):
/// (1) invalidate the install cache while the gate is still un-finished, so an overview
///     that later observes `is_finished() == true` (gate lock acquired after `mark_finished`
///     released it) necessarily observes generation G+1 on its cache lock take (the cache
///     lock was released by `invalidate_all` before the gate lock was taken by `mark_finished`);
/// (2) mark the gate finished (results replaced, `running`/`pending` cleared, waiters released);
/// (3) announce. A client refreshing on the event therefore sees `finished` AND G+1, and no
///     ticket opened after `finished` became observable can carry the pre-pass generation.
fn finish_pass<I: FnOnce(), E: FnOnce()>(gate: &AgentUpdateGate, results: Vec<AgentUpdateResult>, end: PassEnd<I, E>) {
    (end.invalidate)();
    gate.mark_finished(results);
    (end.emit)();
}
```

   `FinishGuard::drop` (`:574-584`) becomes: `let results = self.results.take().unwrap_or_default(); let cache = self.app.try_state::<Arc<AgentInstallCache>>(); let app = self.app.clone(); let emit_finished = self.emit_finished; let announced = results.clone(); finish_pass(&self.gate, results, PassEnd { invalidate: || { if let Some(cache) = cache.as_ref() { cache.invalidate_all(); } }, emit: || { if emit_finished { emit_all(&app, "agent_updates_finished", json!({ "results": announced })); } } });`. The invalidation runs on EVERY guard drop (real pass and quiet boot alike: on a quiet boot the cache is necessarily empty, because no ticket is opened before `finished`, so the bump is free and the transition has one shape); the emit stays conditional on `emit_finished`, exactly as today. Order inside `drop` is therefore fixed: `invalidate_all` -> `mark_finished` -> emit. The remaining review item is that `drop` wires the two named fields (step 10 test `finish_pass_invalidates_before_the_gate_is_finished_and_emits_after` pins the helper's order deterministically; `finish_guard_drop_invalidates_marks_finished_and_emits_only_on_a_real_pass` pins the effects of the real drop).

5. **Per-command wrapper and panic settlement** (new, before `run_startup_updates`):

```rust
/// #1551 - one update task: probes the pre-update install state (round 5), marks the gate,
/// emits the per-command events around the unchanged `run_update_sequence`, and returns its
/// result for `join_all`.
async fn run_update_target(app: AppHandle, gate: Arc<AgentUpdateGate>, target: UpdateTarget) -> AgentUpdateResult {
    // #1551 round 5 - read the installed version BEFORE the update, directly (no cache ticket,
    // no generation): the node stays `Pendiente` meanwhile, and this target's update starts
    // only after its own probe ended, so a probe never overlaps an update of the same CLI.
    let install_before = probe_command_install_state(&target.command).await;
    let node = gate.mark_command_started(&target.command, &target.label, Some(install_before));
    emit_all(&app, "agent_update_command_started", json!(node));
    let result = run_update_sequence(&target, UPDATE_STEP_TIMEOUT).await;
    gate.mark_command_finished(result.clone());
    emit_all(&app, "agent_update_command_finished", json!(result));
    result
}

/// #1551 - a task that panicked never ran `mark_command_finished`: settle it here so the
/// row leaves `running` and every surface receives its `command_finished` (Rust R14).
fn settle_joined_update(app: &AppHandle, gate: &AgentUpdateGate, target: &UpdateTarget, joined: Result<AgentUpdateResult, tokio::task::JoinError>) -> AgentUpdateResult {
    match joined {
        Ok(result) => result,
        Err(_) => {
            let result = AgentUpdateResult { command: target.command.clone(), label: target.label.clone(), ok: false, error: Some("update task panicked".to_string()) };
            gate.mark_command_finished(result.clone());
            emit_all(app, "agent_update_command_finished", json!(result));
            result
        }
    }
}
```

   Step 4 of `run_startup_updates` (`:678-684`) spawns `tokio::spawn(run_update_target(app.clone(), Arc::clone(&gate), target))`; the result mapping (`:686-698`) becomes `joined.into_iter().zip(updates.iter()).map(|(r, t)| settle_joined_update(&app, &gate, t, r)).collect()` (the `Err(_)` arm `:691-696` moves into the helper with the same result bytes).

   **5a. Pass nodes and the skip helper** (round 5; new, before `run_startup_updates`):

```rust
/// #1551 - the agents of this boot's pass in catalog order: every target of `plan.updates`
/// (decided `true`) and `plan.prompts` (to be asked), one node per command (first catalog
/// occurrence, like `build_update_plan`). Pure; the order is the timeline order on every surface.
pub fn pass_nodes(catalog: &[CodingAgentDefinition], plan: &AgentUpdatePlan) -> Vec<AgentUpdateNode> {
    let mut seen: HashSet<&str> = HashSet::new();
    let mut nodes = Vec::new();
    for entry in catalog {
        if !seen.insert(entry.command.as_str()) {
            continue;
        }
        if let Some(target) = plan.updates.iter().chain(plan.prompts.iter()).find(|t| t.command == entry.command) {
            nodes.push(AgentUpdateNode { command: target.command.clone(), label: target.label.clone(), update_commands: target.commands.clone(), install_before: None });
        }
    }
    nodes
}

/// #1551 - a prompted target leaves the pass (answered No, or expired): prune its node and tell
/// every surface, exactly once. Called by the prompt loop only, after that prompt's closure.
fn skip_prompted_target(app: &AppHandle, gate: &AgentUpdateGate, command: &str) {
    if let Some(skipped) = gate.mark_command_skipped(command) {
        emit_all(app, "agent_update_command_skipped", json!(skipped));
    }
}
```

   `pass_nodes` runs right after `build_update_plan` in `run_startup_updates` (the empty-plan early return `:620-623` is kept, so a quiet boot never sets nodes) and before `let mut updates: Vec<UpdateTarget> = plan.updates;` moves the vector, exactly as today; a target is a node whether it will be asked or not, so the timeline knows the whole pass from its first frame.

6. **Overview rows** (pure, after `build_update_plan`):

```rust
/// #1551 - one row per catalog entry with a non-empty (backfilled) update sequence, in
/// catalog order, NO dedup (duplicate-command entries show identical command-keyed
/// install state). Cursor drops out because it ships no update command. Commands
/// without an install entry are `checking` (seq 0).
pub fn build_update_overview_rows(catalog: &[CodingAgentDefinition], install_by_command: &HashMap<String, InstallState>) -> Vec<AgentUpdateOverviewRow>
```

7. **Install probe for one command** (after the row builder):

```rust
/// #1551 - resolve + probe policy for one catalog command. Executes a process ONLY for a
/// bare token whose stem has a built-in probe (§5.2): a project-authored catalog can name
/// `claude` and get the user's own PATH `claude`, but can never point the probe at a bundled
/// binary. Explicit paths and unknown stems report presence only (`unprobed`).
pub async fn probe_command_install_state(command: &str) -> InstallState
```

   Steps: `normalize_legacy_agent_command(command)` `Err` -> `missing("empty command")`; `token = norm.shell`; `resolve_program(&token)` `None` -> `missing(format!("'{token}' was not found on PATH"))` for a bare token, `missing(format!("'{token}' is not a file"))` otherwise; `stem = Path::new(&token).file_stem()` lowercased; `!is_bare_program_token(&token)` -> `unprobed(path, "explicit path: version not probed")`; `version_probe_args(&stem)` `None` -> `unprobed(path, format!("no built-in version probe for '{stem}'"))`; else `probe_version(&path, args, PROBE_TIMEOUT).await` -> `Version(v)` => `installed(v, path)`, `Failed(d)` => `probe_failed(path, d)`. The returned state has `seq: 0`; the ticket stamps it at commit.

8. **Overview orchestration** (after 7), with an injectable probe so the scheduling tests of step 10 control timing deterministically:

```rust
/// #1551 - the probe a scheduled task runs; production = `probe_command_install_state`.
pub type ProbeFn = Arc<dyn Fn(String) -> Pin<Box<dyn Future<Output = InstallState> + Send>> + Send + Sync>;
pub fn production_probe() -> ProbeFn { Arc::new(|command| Box::pin(async move { probe_command_install_state(&command).await })) }

/// #1551 - instant: never awaits a probe, never waits on the gate, never holds a lock across an await.
/// Probes are scheduled ONLY once the startup pass is finished, so a version probe can never
/// overlap an update of the same CLI (correction 7), and ONLY through the single-lock
/// `lookup_or_begin`, so two overlapping calls can never open two tickets (R3-M3).
pub async fn update_overview(app: &AppHandle, settings: &SettingsState, gate: &AgentUpdateGate, cache: &Arc<AgentInstallCache>) -> Vec<AgentUpdateOverviewRow> {
    update_overview_with(app, settings, gate, cache, production_probe()).await
}
pub async fn update_overview_with(app: &AppHandle, settings: &SettingsState, gate: &AgentUpdateGate, cache: &Arc<AgentInstallCache>, probe: ProbeFn) -> Vec<AgentUpdateOverviewRow>
```

   Body of `update_overview_with`: `let settings = settings.read().await.clone();` (guard dropped), `let catalog = load_catalog_for_settings(&settings);`, `let pass_finished = gate.is_finished();` (read ONCE, before any cache operation), `let now = Instant::now();`, `let mut install_by_command = HashMap::new();`. For each catalog entry with non-empty `update_commands`, once per distinct `command` (first occurrence): `match cache.lookup_or_begin(command, now, INSTALL_CACHE_TTL, pass_finished)`: `Fresh(s)` -> insert `s` (served from the cache, no process, whether or not a pass is running); `InFlight` -> leave absent (`checking`; another call's probe is running); `Deferred` -> leave absent and schedule nothing (during the prompt and update phases every uncached row stays `checking`; the client refresh triggered by `agent_updates_finished` schedules the probes, §5.12); `Began(ticket)` -> spawn the probe task (below). Return `build_update_overview_rows(&catalog, &install_by_command)`. The gate's `running` set is not consulted here: `running` is non-empty only while `!is_finished()`, which already schedules nothing.

   Proof of no probe/update overlap: a probe process exists only inside a task spawned by `update_overview_with` after it observed `is_finished() == true`; `finished` is set exactly once per process by `mark_finished`, which `finish_pass` runs after `join_all` returned (every update sequence has ended; no fallible operation sits between the spawns `:678-684` and `join_all` `:685`, so no other path reaches the guard drop while a sequence runs) or at the quiet-boot return (no sequence ever runs); the pass never starts after `finished` because `run_startup_updates` is spawned once (`lib.rs:2216-2223`). Hence no update sequence can be running while a probe process exists, and no probe can be in flight when an update sequence starts.

   Proof of single-flight (R3-M3): a ticket is opened only inside the cache lock by `lookup_or_begin`, which in the same critical section checks the entry, the TTL, and `in_flight`; therefore for two overlapping calls A and B on an `Absent` command exactly one receives `Began` and the other `InFlight`, and if A's probe commits before B's call, B receives `Fresh` (the commit inserted the entry and cleared `in_flight` under the same lock). The round-2 window (lookup Absent in both, commit of A, then B's separate `try_begin` succeeding) does not exist because there is no second lock take.

   Proof of the pass boundary (R3-M4): a call reads the gate first and the cache second. Relative to the transition of step 4 (cache lock released by `invalidate_all`, then gate lock taken and released by `mark_finished`) only three interleavings exist: (a) gate read before `mark_finished` and cache read before `invalidate_all`: a pre-pass `Fresh` may be served, nothing is scheduled (`may_begin == false`); (b) gate read before `mark_finished`, cache read after `invalidate_all`: `Deferred` (`Absent`, not finished), nothing scheduled; (c) gate read after `mark_finished`: the cache read is after `invalidate_all` by transitivity (program order in the dropping thread plus the two lock hand-offs), so any ticket opened carries G+1. "Gate read after `mark_finished` with cache read before `invalidate_all`" is impossible. Test `overview_at_the_pass_boundary_never_opens_a_pre_pass_ticket` exercises the three positions deterministically.

   Probe task (`tauri::async_runtime::spawn`, never `tokio::spawn`, so it runs from any caller context; round 5 wraps this block as `fn spawn_probe_task(app: &AppHandle, command: &str, ticket: ProbeTicket, probe: ProbeFn)` so the post-pass scheduler of step 9b shares the one spawn site):

```rust
let app = app.clone(); let command = command.clone(); let probe = Arc::clone(&probe);
tauri::async_runtime::spawn(async move {
    probe_commit_announce(&app, &command, ticket, move |c| probe(c)).await;
});
```

   with the task body extracted so tests can inject invalidations between attempts:

```rust
/// #1551 - the probe task body. Announces `agent_install_state_changed` ONLY for a state
/// committed to the current cache generation (correction 6): a completion rejected by an
/// invalidation is re-probed once on the same (renewed) ticket; a second rejection emits
/// nothing and drops the ticket, which frees the slot so the next overview call schedules
/// fresh work. At most two processes per command per overview call.
async fn probe_commit_announce<F, Fut>(app: &AppHandle, command: &str, ticket: ProbeTicket, probe: F)
where
    F: Fn(String) -> Fut,
    Fut: Future<Output = InstallState>,
{
    let committed = match ticket.complete(probe(command.to_string()).await) {
        Completion::Committed(state) => Some(state),
        Completion::Stale(retry) => match retry.complete(probe(command.to_string()).await) {
            Completion::Committed(state) => Some(state),
            Completion::Stale(abandoned) => {
                drop(abandoned);
                None
            }
        },
    };
    if let Some(install) = committed {
        emit_all(app, "agent_install_state_changed", json!({ "command": command, "install": install }));
    }
}
```

   Because `complete` renews the ticket atomically on rejection (§5.2), "a retry ticket cannot be acquired" is impossible by construction; there is no competing owner to defer to and no stale state is ever emitted. In production `invalidate_all` runs once per process at the guard drop, BEFORE `finished` is observable, and no ticket exists before `finished` (step 8 scheduling rule), so in production no ticket is ever open at invalidation time: the `Stale` paths are reachable only in tests (Rust R3), and the rule is still total.

9. **The answer flow** (R3-M1, P7; new `pub` fn after step 8; the ONLY answer path, used by the Tauri command and the WebSocket route through `commands::config::agent_update_answer_inner`, §5.5):

```rust
/// #1551 - one prompt across every surface. One critical section per gate, serialized by
/// `gate.answer_serial` (tokio mutex, FIFO; held across the persist await; the std `state`
/// lock is taken twice, briefly, never across an await): classify -> persist -> claim ->
/// emit the closure -> release the loop. The prompt loop's expiry arm takes the SAME serial
/// section before it removes a pending entry, so the pending entry has exactly one remover
/// and that remover is the only emitter of `agent_update_prompt_closed`.
/// - First answer to persist wins: it records the policy, claims the pending entry, emits the
///   closure to every surface, and only then delivers the answer to the loop (which may then
///   show the next prompt): the closure of A always precedes the prompt of B.
/// - A later answer for a command with a recorded policy returns Ok(false) WITHOUT persisting.
/// - An answer for an expired prompt (timeout or pass ended) persists for future boots, is
///   recorded, and returns Ok(false); this boot is unaffected (accepted late semantics).
/// - A failed persist changes nothing (prompt still pending, nothing recorded) and returns
///   Err; the next answer in the queue proceeds and can win.
/// No await sits between claim, emit and deliver: a cancelled call (a WebSocket connection
/// dropped mid-command) either never claimed or completed all three.
pub async fn answer_prompt<P, Fut>(app: &AppHandle, gate: &AgentUpdateGate, command: &str, enabled: bool, persist: P) -> Result<bool, String>
where
    P: FnOnce() -> Fut,
    Fut: Future<Output = Result<(), String>>,
{
    let _serial = gate.answer_serial.lock().await;
    match gate.prompt_state(command) {
        PromptState::NotPrompted => {
            return Err(format!("agent_update_answer: '{command}' was not prompted this boot"));
        }
        PromptState::Answered(_) => {
            log::info!("[agent-update] answer for '{command}' (enabled={enabled}): ignored, already answered this boot");
            return Ok(false);
        }
        PromptState::Pending | PromptState::Expired => {}
    }
    persist().await?;
    match gate.claim_answer(command, enabled) {
        AnswerClaim::Claimed(claimed) => {
            // (1) the closure is enqueued on every surface (WebSocket queues, Tauri emit) ...
            emit_all(app, "agent_update_prompt_closed", json!(claimed.closed()));
            // (2) ... and only now is the loop released; `false` iff the receiver is already gone.
            let applied = claimed.deliver(enabled);
            log::info!(
                "[agent-update] answer for '{command}' (enabled={enabled}): {}",
                if applied { "applied this boot" } else { "recorded for future boots (prompt already expired)" }
            );
            Ok(applied)
        }
        AnswerClaim::Recorded => {
            log::info!("[agent-update] answer for '{command}' (enabled={enabled}): recorded for future boots (prompt already closed)");
            Ok(false)
        }
    }
}
```

   The `[agent-update] answer for '<command>'` line is written exactly once per `agent_update_answer` call that passed the `not prompted` check (whatever the outcome), which is what T4/T5-prompt count.

   State machine per prompted command (the gate is the single owner; the pending entry is removed exactly once):

   | State | Set by | `answer_prompt` behavior |
   |---|---|---|
   | `NotPrompted` | initial | `Err(not prompted)`, nothing persisted |
   | `Pending` | `register_prompt` (prompt loop) | persist; `claim_answer` -> `Claimed`: the entry is removed and the policy recorded, the closure is emitted, then `deliver`: `true` -> `Ok(true)`; receiver already gone (the timeout elapsed during the persist; its expiry arm is queued behind this call) -> `Ok(false)` (late; the closure is ours, the queued expiry then finds no entry and emits nothing). `Recorded` cannot follow `Pending`: the expiry arm cannot remove the entry while this call holds the serial section |
   | `Expired` | `drop_pending` (the loop's expiry arm, inside the serial section) or `mark_finished` (pass ended) removed the entry | persist; `claim_answer` -> `Recorded`; `Ok(false)`, no emit (the remover already emitted the closure) |
   | `Answered(v)` | `claim_answer` (any surface) | `Ok(false)`, nothing persisted, no emit |

   Why the persisted policy and this boot's action can never diverge, and why exactly one closure precedes the next prompt (R3-M1 items 2 and 4, P7): the persist, the claim, the closure emit and the delivery of one answer happen inside one `answer_serial` critical section, and the loop's expiry arm enters the same section before removing an entry (step 3), so for one prompted command exactly one of these removes the pending entry: an answer's claim or the expiry. (a) A (first in the mutex queue) persists successfully and claims: `answered[cmd] = a`, the closure of `cmd` is emitted, then `deliver` hands `a` to the loop; B then classifies `Answered` and persists nothing; the loop woke from `rx` only after the closure was already in every WebSocket queue and in the Tauri emit order, so the prompt of the next command is emitted after `prompt_closed(cmd)`: persisted == acted == `a`. (b) A's persist fails: A returns `Err`, the gate is untouched (`Pending`), B is the first to persist and claims: persisted == acted == `b`; A's surface keeps its prompt for a retry. (c) The timeout elapses while A is inside `persist`: the loop's expiry arm blocks on `answer_serial`; A's `claim_answer` still finds the entry (`Claimed`), records `a`, emits the closure, and `deliver` returns `false` (the `timeout` future dropped `rx`): `Ok(false)`; the expiry arm then runs `drop_pending` -> `None`, emits nothing, and registers the next prompt AFTER the closure was emitted; `a` applies to future boots and this boot skipped `cmd` (accepted late semantics, item 3). (d) The expiry arm runs before any answer: it removes the entry, emits the closure, and registers the next prompt in program order; a later answer is `Expired` -> persists, `Recorded`, `Ok(false)`, no emit. In every case the entry has one remover, the remover is the only emitter, and every closure is emitted before the loop can register the next prompt. No std lock crosses an await: `prompt_state`, `claim_answer` and `drop_pending` each take and release `state` synchronously; the persist awaits the settings `RwLock` and the file write while only the tokio `answer_serial` is held; the expiry arm awaits only `answer_serial`; no task holds `answer_serial` while waiting for something an `answer_prompt` caller or the loop holds (the settings lock is never held by a waiter of `answer_serial`, and the loop holds nothing while it waits), so no cycle. Cancellation: the only await points are the serial lock and the persist; claim, emit and deliver are synchronous and consecutive, so a dropped `answer_prompt` future never leaves a claimed-but-unclosed prompt.

9b. **Post-pass probe scheduling** (round 5; placed after step 9 and before the tests; new private fn before `run_startup_updates`):

```rust
/// #1551 round 5 - after the pass is finished, re-read the install state of every UPDATED
/// command in the background through the cache (single-flight with any Settings-triggered
/// probe), so the timeline's finished nodes can show `<before> → <after>` and Settings sees
/// the post-update state without asking. Runs only after `finish_pass` returned (program
/// order in the pass task), so every ticket it opens carries the post-pass generation and
/// no probe can overlap an update (every update ended before `finished`).
fn schedule_post_update_probes(app: &AppHandle, updated: &[UpdateTarget]) {
    let Some(cache) = app.try_state::<Arc<AgentInstallCache>>() else { return; };
    let now = Instant::now();
    for target in updated {
        if let Scheduling::Began(ticket) = cache.lookup_or_begin(&target.command, now, INSTALL_CACHE_TTL, true) {
            spawn_probe_task(app, &target.command, ticket, production_probe());
        }
    }
}
```

   `spawn_probe_task` is the step-8 probe task (the `tauri::async_runtime::spawn` block around `probe_commit_announce`) extracted into a private fn that step 8's `Began` arm calls too (one spawn site, two schedulers). `Fresh`/`InFlight`/`Deferred` schedule nothing (`Deferred` is unreachable here because `may_begin` is `true`; `InFlight` means the Settings refresh triggered by `agent_updates_finished` already opened the ticket, which is the single-flight rule of R3-M3 doing its job). Only the commands that actually ran an update are re-probed (`updated` = the `updates` vector after the prompt phase): a skipped or unregistered command is not touched, so a boot that updated nothing spawns no process. The tail of `run_startup_updates` (`:701`) becomes `guard.complete(results); drop(guard); schedule_post_update_probes(&app, &updates);` (the explicit `drop` runs `finish_pass`, step 4, so `finished`, the invalidation and the `agent_updates_finished` emit all precede the scheduling; nothing after the `drop` can leave the gate un-finished).

   Why the pass-boundary proofs of step 8 still hold: the pre-update probes of step 5 are direct calls (no ticket, no cache entry, no generation), so "no ticket is open before `finished`" is still true; the post-pass tickets are opened after `mark_finished` in the dropping task's program order, i.e. interleaving (c) of the boundary proof, and carry G+1; a Settings overview racing with them gets `InFlight` or `Fresh`, never a second ticket (single-lock `lookup_or_begin`). Why a probe never overlaps an update: a target's pre-probe ends before its own `run_update_sequence` starts (sequential in the same task), the concurrent updates of other targets touch other CLIs, and every post-pass probe starts after `join_all` returned (all updates ended).

   `ReleaseProbe` (test-only, defined inside `mod tests`, which already opens with `use super::*;` at `:706`, so the production imports of this file (`Arc`, `Pin`, `Future`, `json`, `Value`, `WsBroadcaster`) are in scope; the module additionally imports `std::sync::atomic::AtomicU8`, `std::task::{Context, Wake, Waker}` and `crate::web::broadcast::WsOutMsg`, which production never names):

```rust
/// #1551 - waker of the prompt's `oneshot::Receiver` in
/// `answer_prompt_enqueues_the_closure_before_releasing_the_loop`. tokio's
/// `oneshot::Sender::send` runs it INLINE (`Inner::complete` -> `wake_by_ref`), i.e. inside
/// `ClaimedPrompt::deliver`, before `answer_prompt` executes anything else, so it sees the
/// WebSocket queue at the instant the loop is released. `seen`: 0 = never woken, 1 = woken
/// while no closure frame was enqueued (the superseded send-before-emit order), 2 = woken
/// with `agent_update_prompt_closed {claude}` already enqueued (the plan's order).
struct ReleaseProbe {
    probe_rx: std::sync::Mutex<tokio::sync::mpsc::Receiver<WsOutMsg>>,
    seen: AtomicU8,
}

impl Wake for ReleaseProbe {
    fn wake(self: Arc<Self>) {
        self.wake_by_ref();
    }

    fn wake_by_ref(self: &Arc<Self>) {
        let frame = self.probe_rx.lock().unwrap().try_recv().ok();
        let closed = frame.is_some_and(|frame| match frame {
            WsOutMsg::Text(text) => serde_json::from_str::<Value>(&text).ok().is_some_and(|frame| {
                frame["event"] == "agent_update_prompt_closed" && frame["payload"]["command"] == "claude"
            }),
            WsOutMsg::Binary(_) => false,
        });
        self.seen.store(if closed { 2 } else { 1 }, std::sync::atomic::Ordering::SeqCst);
    }
}
```

   No std lock is held when `deliver` runs (`claim_answer` released `state`, `broadcast_event` released the broadcaster's senders lock), so the probe's own mutex and the `try_recv()` inside the waker cannot deadlock; `WsOutMsg` is `Send`, so `Arc<ReleaseProbe>` satisfies the `Send + Sync + 'static` bound of `Waker::from`; the decode reads the same `{event, payload}` JSON text that `broadcast_event` builds (`web/broadcast.rs:45-52`, `WsOutMsg::Text`), and `is_some_and` is already used by this file at 92afbfe6 (`drop_pending`, `resolve_answer`). `tokio::sync::oneshot::Receiver` is `Unpin`, so `Pin::new(&mut rx)` is valid; `send` stores the value before `complete()` wakes the probe, so the later `rx.await` is `Ready(Ok(true))` at once and never wakes the probe a second time: `seen` is written exactly once, inside the send.

### 5.5 `src-tauri/src/commands/config.rs` (owner ac-dev-rust-v3)

Next to `get_agent_update_status` (`:2422`), with #1327-style doc comments:

```rust
/// #1551 - shared by the Tauri command and the WebSocket router. `try_state` so an unmanaged test app errors instead of panicking.
pub fn agent_update_status_inner(app: &AppHandle) -> Result<crate::agent_update::AgentUpdateStatus, String>
/// #1551 - the only answer path: `agent_update::answer_prompt` with the existing narrow persist injected.
pub async fn agent_update_answer_inner(app: &AppHandle, settings: &SettingsState, command: String, enabled: bool) -> Result<bool, String> {
    let gate = app.try_state::<Arc<crate::agent_update::AgentUpdateGate>>().ok_or_else(|| "agent update gate is not managed".to_string())?;
    crate::agent_update::answer_prompt(app, &gate, &command, enabled, || {
        persist_narrow_settings_update(settings, |candidate| {
            candidate.agent_auto_update_by_command.insert(command.clone(), enabled);
        })
    })
    .await
}
pub async fn agent_update_overview_inner(app: &AppHandle, settings: &SettingsState) -> Result<Vec<crate::agent_update::AgentUpdateOverviewRow>, String>  // gate + cache via try_state, then `crate::agent_update::update_overview`
pub async fn coding_agent_catalog_inner(settings: &SettingsState) -> Vec<CodingAgentDefinition>   // the existing body of `get_coding_agent_catalog`

#[tauri::command]
pub async fn get_agent_update_overview(app: AppHandle, settings: State<'_, SettingsState>) -> Result<Vec<crate::agent_update::AgentUpdateOverviewRow>, String> {
    agent_update_overview_inner(&app, settings.inner()).await
}
```

`get_agent_update_status` keeps its signature and stays `Ok(gate.snapshot())`. `agent_update_answer` (`:2437-2453`) becomes `(app: AppHandle, settings: State<'_, SettingsState>, command: String, enabled: bool)` delegating to `agent_update_answer_inner` (invoke args `{command, enabled}` and the `boolean` result unchanged); the `was_prompted` check, the unconditional persist, and the trailing `resolve_answer` of the current body are gone: classification, persist, and settlement now happen inside `answer_prompt`'s critical section (§5.4 step 9), and the `was not prompted` error text is preserved there. `persist_narrow_settings_update` is unchanged (still the only writer of the answer path). `get_coding_agent_catalog` delegates to `coding_agent_catalog_inner`. `list_reseedable_agent_commands` is unchanged (the router calls it directly).

### 5.6 `src-tauri/src/web/commands.rs` (owner ac-dev-rust-v3)

Add, immediately above `dispatch_inner` (`:125`):

```rust
/// #1551 - agent-update surface parity. These arms read gate/catalog state or answer the
/// startup prompt; none touches sessions or the selection coordinator, and the prompt
/// phase runs WHILE restore is parked on the gate (`lib.rs:1085-1093`), so they must stay
/// reachable while `RestoreInProgress` is set: otherwise a browser client can never answer
/// the prompt and never observes the pass. Every other command keeps the guard.
async fn dispatch_agent_update_command(state: &WsState, cmd: &str, args: &Value) -> Option<Result<Value, String>>
```

Arms: `"get_agent_update_status"` -> `agent_update_status_inner(&state.app_handle)` serialized; `"agent_update_answer"` -> `require_str(args, "command")`, `require_json::<bool>(args, "enabled")`, `agent_update_answer_inner(&state.app_handle, &state.settings, command, enabled).await` -> `json!(applied)`; `"get_agent_update_overview"` -> `agent_update_overview_inner(&state.app_handle, &state.settings).await` serialized; `"get_coding_agent_catalog"` -> `coding_agent_catalog_inner(&state.settings).await` serialized; `"list_reseedable_agent_commands"` -> `crate::commands::config::list_reseedable_agent_commands()` serialized; `_ => None`. All five reference `crate::commands::config::*` only (no new module reference from this file). The answer arm's serialization behind `answer_prompt` is compatible with the per-connection sequential dispatch of §3.3: it waits at most for one other answer's settings write.

`dispatch_inner`: insert as its FIRST statement, before the `RestoreInProgress` guard (`:126`): `if let Some(result) = dispatch_agent_update_command(state, cmd, args).await { return result; }`.

Tests (module `tests`; a new helper `ws_state_with_agent_update(settings, restore_in_progress: bool, pass_finished: bool)` builds the mock app like `ws_state_for` and additionally manages `Arc<crate::RestoreInProgress(AtomicBool::new(restore_in_progress))>`, an `Arc<crate::agent_update::AgentUpdateGate>` on which `mark_finished(vec![])` was called iff `pass_finished`, `Arc<crate::agent_version::AgentInstallCache>`, and the SAME `WsBroadcaster` that goes into `WsState` (clones share senders), with the receiver subscribed before building (test-only references; the detector ignores test modules, §11); the helper returns the gate too, so tests can seed prompts). No route test persists: the route reaches the real `persist_narrow_settings_update` (the instance settings file, `save_settings` `:851`/`:889`), so every persisting path is pinned in `agent_update.rs` with the injected persist (§5.4 step 10) and the route tests use only the non-persisting outcomes:
- `agent_update_routes_bypass_restore_guard`: with `restore_in_progress = true`, `list_sessions` -> `Err("selectionCoordinatorBusy")` while `get_agent_update_status` -> `Ok` with `inProgress: false`, `running: []`, `results: []`, `answered: {}`, `nodes: []`, and `get_coding_agent_catalog` -> `Ok` array.
- `agent_update_answer_route_rejects_unprompted_command`: `Err` containing `was not prompted`; `agent_update_answer_route_rejects_missing_args`: `{}` -> `Err`.
- `agent_update_answer_route_returns_false_for_an_already_answered_prompt` (R3-M1): seed the helper's gate with `register_prompt("claude", "Claude")` + `claim_answer("claude", true)` (the returned claim is dropped: closure emission is not under test here); route `agent_update_answer {command: "claude", enabled: false}` -> `Ok(false)` (classified `Answered` before any persist); `get_agent_update_status` afterwards reports `answered == {claude: true}` and `prompt == null`.
- `get_coding_agent_catalog_route_returns_backfilled_catalog`: settings with `project_paths = [tempdir]` (no catalog file) -> 7 rows, six with non-empty `updateCommands`, cursor empty; `list_reseedable_agent_commands` -> the same array the Tauri command returns.
- `get_agent_update_overview_route_is_instant_and_probes_in_background`: `pass_finished = true`, the `bob-1551-missing` fixture of §5.4 -> first response one row `checking` (`seq 0`), poll (bounded 5 s) until `missing` with `seq 1`, and the broadcast receiver got an `agent_install_state_changed` frame whose `payload.command` is `bob-1551-missing`.
- Event delivery to WebSocket clients is pinned once, in `agent_update.rs` (`run_update_target_emits_started_then_finished_and_updates_gate`, `answer_prompt_first_answer_resolves_persists_and_closes_every_surface`, and `emit_all_reaches_websocket_subscribers_and_survives_no_broadcaster`); no duplicate here.

`types.ts`: after `AgentUpdatePrompt` (`:817`):

/** #1551 - a command in the startup update pass (start order); also the payload of `agent_update_prompt_closed`. */
export interface AgentUpdateCommandRef { command: string; label: string; }
export type InstallStatus = "checking" | "missing" | "installed" | "probeFailed" | "unprobed";
/** #1551 - resolved install state of one catalog command; `path`/`detail` absent when the backend has none.
 *  `seq` is the backend cache's commit counter (0 = `checking`); a higher `seq` is always the newer state. */
export interface InstallState { status: InstallStatus; version?: string | null; path?: string | null; detail?: string | null; seq: number; }
/** #1551 - one update-capable catalog entry (catalog order, duplicates kept). Configured/registered/live facts are FE-derived. */

export interface AgentInstallStateChanged { command: string; install: InstallState; }
/** #1551 round 5 - one agent of this boot's startup pass, in pass (catalog) order; also the payload of `agent_update_command_started`.
 *  `installBefore` is the pre-update probe result (absent until that probe ran; `seq` 0, never cached). */
export interface AgentUpdateNode { command: string; label: string; updateCommands: string[]; installBefore?: InstallState | null; }
