Replace the stale sentence "Inert until the follow-up update-check feature reads it." in the `updateCommands` doc (`:394-399`) with "Consumed by the #1327 startup update pass (`build_update_plan`) and listed by the #1551 Settings Auto-update table." and, in the `autoUpdate` doc (`:400-401`), "Inert until the follow-up feature reads it." with "Inert: the runtime reads only `AppSettings.agentAutoUpdateByCommand`."

### 5.9 `src/sidebar/agent-update.ts`: store and wiring (correction 4, R3-M1 item 5)

Store shape becomes `AgentUpdateState = { inProgress: boolean; prompt: AgentUpdatePrompt | null; running: AgentUpdateCommandRef[]; results: AgentUpdateResult[]; finishedSeen: boolean; closedPrompts: string[]; nodes: AgentUpdateNode[]; skippedNodes: string[]; installAfter: Record<string, InstallState>; summary: "none" | "shown" | "dismissed" }` (round 5 adds `nodes`, `installAfter` and `summary`; round 6 adds `skippedNodes` `/** #1551 round 6 - commands whose node left this boot's pass (agent_update_command_skipped), recorded even when the node was never in the store; every later snapshot merge filters them out, exactly as closedPrompts protects prompts. Cleared by agent_updates_started. */`: `nodes` `/** #1551 - the pass nodes in pass order (from agent_updates_started or the snapshot); only ever shrinks during a pass (skips). */`, `installAfter` `/** #1551 - post-update install state per finished command, highest seq wins (from agent_install_state_changed). */`, `summary` `/** #1551 - the persistent final view: shown from agent_updates_finished until the user closes it on this surface. */`); export `agentUpdateInitialState()` returning the full initial object (`finishedSeen: false`, `closedPrompts: []`, `nodes: []`, `skippedNodes: []`, `installAfter: {}`, `summary: "none"`) and `resetAgentUpdateForTests()` (= `setAgentUpdateStore(agentUpdateInitialState())` plus clearing the module-level failure-toast set below; the two existing test files switch to it, a mechanical edit). The failure-toast dedup set `shownFailures` moves from a local of `wireAgentUpdateListeners` to a module-level `const shownFailures = new Set<string>()` so the summary's close can toast (below); its semantics (one toast per failed command per instance) are unchanged. `finishedSeen` (`/** #1551 - `agent_updates_finished` was applied this boot; an older snapshot may then only merge results. */`) and `closedPrompts` (`/** #1551 - commands whose prompt was closed this boot (answered on any surface, or expired); a snapshot may never re-open one of them. */`) are store state so tests can set and read them.

Pure helpers exported for tests: `upsertResult(list, result)` (replace by `command`, else append), `withoutRunning(list, command)`, `unionRunning(a, b)` (`a` in order, then the entries of `b` whose `command` is absent from `a`), `addUnique(list, value)`, (round 5) `withoutNode(nodes, command)`, `upsertNode(nodes, node)` (a node with that `command` exists: keep its position, label and `updateCommands`, and take the incoming `installBefore` when present; else append), `mergeNodes(current, incoming)` (`current` empty -> `incoming`; else `current` filtered to the commands present in `incoming`, each taking the incoming `installBefore` when its own is absent: nodes only shrink, so a snapshot can never resurrect a skipped node, and a snapshot that predates a skip carries a superset the filter ignores), `newerInstall(map, command, install)` (`true` iff `map[command]` is absent or has a lower `seq`), and

```ts
/** #1551 - monotonic merge of a status snapshot into the store: events already applied are never
 *  downgraded by an older snapshot; a snapshot after `agent_updates_finished` may only add results;
 *  a snapshot's prompt is applied only when that prompt was not closed this boot. */
export function mergeSnapshot(current: AgentUpdateState, status: AgentUpdateStatus): AgentUpdateState {
  const results = status.results.reduce(upsertResult, current.results);
  if (current.finishedSeen) return { ...current, results };
  const running = unionRunning(current.running, status.running ?? []).filter((r) => !results.some((x) => x.command === r.command));
  const prompt = status.prompt && !current.closedPrompts.includes(status.prompt.command) ? status.prompt : current.prompt;
  const incomingNodes = status.nodes ?? [];
  // #1551 round 5 - a snapshot computed BEFORE the pass started (inProgress false, nodes []) can be delivered
  // AFTER agent_updates_started on Tauri (§3.3): it carries no node information and must never shrink the store.
  // Only a snapshot computed during the pass (inProgress true) can report a skip this surface missed.
  const mergedNodes = status.inProgress ? mergeNodes(current.nodes, incomingNodes) : (current.nodes.length > 0 ? current.nodes : incomingNodes);
  // #1551 round 6 - a skip this surface already saw wins over any snapshot, even one that seeds an empty store.
  const nodes = mergedNodes.filter((node) => !current.skippedNodes.includes(node.command));
  return { ...current, results, running, inProgress: current.inProgress || status.inProgress, prompt, nodes };
}

/** #1551 - the overlay's own answer closed this prompt (either outcome): protect it from an older snapshot. */
export function markPromptClosed(command: string): void   // set("closedPrompts", (list) => addUnique(list, command))
```

Why it is correct in both race directions: `mark_command_started` precedes the `command_started` emit and `mark_command_finished` precedes the `command_finished` emit (§5.4 step 5), so (a) a store `running` entry absent from the snapshot means the snapshot was computed before the mark and is older: the union keeps it; (b) a snapshot `running` entry absent from the store means the event has not arrived (or was lost): the union adds it; (c) a command present in `results` of either source has finished, and finished is newer than running in every interleaving, so the filter removes it; (d) results are written once per command per pass with identical values on both paths, so the upsert is order-independent; (e) `finished` is the last event of the only pass of the process, so after `finishedSeen` any snapshot still reporting `inProgress`, `running`, or a `prompt` was computed earlier and must not resurrect them; (f) prompts: each command is prompted at most once per boot (§3.1 `build_update_plan`), so "the prompt of `X` was closed" is a monotonic fact of this boot; a snapshot carrying `prompt: X` after `closedPrompts` contains `X` was computed before the closure (the gate clears `pending_prompt` in the same lock take that resolves or drops the prompt, before the closed event is emitted) and is ignored, while a snapshot carrying a prompt `Y` not in `closedPrompts` is newer than every closure the store knows and is applied; a snapshot with `prompt: null` keeps the store's prompt (it may predate a prompt event that already arrived), and the prompt is cleared only by `prompt_closed`, `finished`, or the overlay's own answer; (g) a closure names its prompt, so a `prompt_closed(A)` delivered after `prompt(B)` (possible only on Tauri, whose event order is not assumed; never on a WebSocket queue, §5.1) records `A` and leaves `B` shown, and symmetrically the overlay's completed `answer(A)` clears only while `A` is still shown (§5.13). `status.running ?? []` tolerates a backend that predates this field. (h) nodes (round 5, corrected in round 6 by FE B1/M1 and Grinch R2): the backend sets the node set once, at `mark_started_with_nodes`, before the `started` emit, and only removes nodes afterwards (skips), so for one boot the node set is monotonically non-increasing FROM `started` on; a snapshot with `inProgress: false` is either pre-start (`nodes: []`, computed before `mark_started_with_nodes` and possibly delivered after the `started` event on Tauri, §3.3: it carries no node information and is ignored) or post-finish (its nodes seed a store that has none and never replace a set the store already holds); only an in-pass snapshot (`inProgress: true`) filters: `mergeNodes` then keeps the store's set and drops what the snapshot no longer lists (a skip the store missed), takes the snapshot's set when the store has none (a mount after `started`), and never adds a node the store lacks (that node was skipped and the store already saw the skip; a snapshot computed earlier is older); on top of that, `skippedNodes` (written by `command_skipped` even when the node is not in the store yet, cleared by `started`) is filtered out of EVERY merged set, so a skip delivered before an older snapshot into an empty store can never resurrect the node; `installBefore` only ever goes from absent to present, so taking it from either source is safe; `installAfter` and `summary` are never written by a snapshot (a snapshot cannot say whether THIS surface showed the pass). `status.nodes ?? []` tolerates a backend that predates the field.

`wireAgentUpdateListeners` (same subscribe-then-snapshot order; four more listeners than 92afbfe6: `command_started`, `command_finished`, and, round 5, `command_skipped` and `install_state_changed`; snapshot through `mergeSnapshot`):
- `onAgentUpdatesStarted(payload)`: `set({ inProgress: true, running: [], results: [], finishedSeen: false, closedPrompts: [], nodes: payload?.nodes ?? [], skippedNodes: [], installAfter: {}, summary: "none" })` (a pass starts clean and paints its nodes at once; `prompt` untouched; an older backend's `null` payload leaves `nodes` empty and the timeline is then built defensively from the per-command events, §5.10).
- `onAgentUpdatePrompt(prompt)`: `set("prompt", prompt)` (unchanged).
- `onAgentUpdatePromptClosed(closed)`: `const shown = store.prompt; if (!closed || shown?.command === closed.command) set("prompt", null); if (closed) set("closedPrompts", (list) => addUnique(list, closed.command));` (P7: a closure clears THIS surface's prompt only when it names the shown prompt, whoever answered; a delayed closure of A never erases a newer prompt B; the command is always recorded when the backend names it; a `null` payload from an older backend still clears unconditionally, which is safe because that backend emits closures only from the loop, before any next prompt).
- `onAgentUpdateCommandStarted(node)`: append `{command, label}` to `running` unless an entry with `node.command` exists, and `set("nodes", (list) => upsertNode(list, node))` (records `installBefore`; appends a node only when the store never received it: lost `started` payload or older backend).
- `onAgentUpdateCommandSkipped(ref)` (round 5, round 6): `set({ nodes: withoutNode(store.nodes, ref.command), skippedNodes: addUnique(store.skippedNodes, ref.command) })` (the node leaves the timeline and the command is recorded whether or not the node existed, so every later snapshot filters it; nothing else changes; a `command_started` for a skipped command cannot occur, because a skipped target never runs).
- `onAgentInstallStateChanged({command, install})` (round 5; the store wires this listener for the overlay, independently of the Settings block's own listener): applied only when `nodes` has an entry for `command` (a node of this pass, finished or not) and `newerInstall(store.installAfter, command, install)`: `set("installAfter", command, install)` (FE N2, round 6: after `started` no install event for a pass node can be pre-pass, because §5.4 step 8 opens no cache ticket before `finished` and the pre-update probes emit nothing, so such an event is always that node's post-pass probe, even when Tauri delivers it before the node's `command_finished`; the value is kept, and `deriveTimelineNodes` shows it once the result arrives). Install events for commands outside `nodes` are ignored here (they belong to the Settings table).
- `onAgentUpdateCommandFinished(result)`: `set({ running: withoutRunning(store.running, result.command), results: upsertResult(store.results, result) })`.
- `onAgentUpdatesFinished({results})`: `const showSummary = store.inProgress && results.length > 0; set({ inProgress: false, prompt: null, running: [], results, finishedSeen: true, summary: showSummary ? "shown" : store.summary });` (authoritative); then, when `showSummary` is `false`, `showAgentUpdateFailures(results, shownFailures)` as today (a surface that was not showing the pass, or a pass with zero updated targets, keeps the round-4 behavior: no summary, immediate toasts); when it is `true` the toasts are deferred to the close (`dismissAgentUpdateSummary`, below), which is where the prototype shows them.
- Snapshot: `set(mergeSnapshot(store, status))`, then, ONLY when `status.inProgress` is `false`: `showAgentUpdateFailures(status.results, shownFailures)` as today (a genuine post-pass mount, which never shows a summary, keeps the immediate toasts). A snapshot received while the pass runs (`status.inProgress === true`: a surface mounted in the middle of the pass) merges its partial results and toasts NOTHING (Grinch R3, round 6): that surface now shows the pass, receives `agent_updates_finished`, enters the summary, and its toasts appear at the close (`dismissAgentUpdateSummary`), or at `finished` when no summary enters (zero results). F8 catch unchanged.

Exported action `dismissAgentUpdateSummary(): void` (round 5): `if (store.summary !== "shown") return; set("summary", "dismissed"); showAgentUpdateFailures(store.results, shownFailures);` (one toast per failed command, deduplicated against the snapshot path by the same set). The close is local to the surface: no IPC, no backend state, and another surface's summary stays open until closed there.

### 5.10 `src/sidebar/agent-update-status.ts` (new, pure)

```ts
export type ConfiguredState = "yes" | "no" | "ask";
export type InstalledState = "checking" | "installed" | "missing" | "probe-failed" | "unprobed";
export type LiveState = "idle" | "updating" | "ok" | "failed";
export interface AutoUpdateRowView { key; label; command; color: string; registered: boolean; configured: ConfiguredState; installed: { state: InstalledState; label: string; title?: string }; live: { state: LiveState; label: string; title?: string }; }
export type NodeState = "pending" | "updating" | "ok" | "failed";   // round 5 (Option 2 timeline)
export interface TimelineNodeView { command: string; label: string; updateCommands: string[]; state: NodeState; stateText: string; detail: string | null; detailTitle: string | null; }
export interface TimelineHeaderView { total: number; done: number; failed: number; percent: number; text: string; }

export const CONFIGURED_LABELS = { yes: "Yes", no: "No", ask: "Will ask at startup" } as const;
export const LIVE_LABELS = { idle: "-", updating: "Updating...", ok: "Updated", failed: "Update failed" } as const;
export const NODE_STATE_LABELS = { pending: "Pendiente", updating: "Actualizando...", ok: "Listo", failed: "Falló" } as const;
export const VERSION_MISSING_LABEL = "no instalada";
export const VERSION_UNDETECTED_LABEL = "versión no detectada";
export const UNKNOWN_ERROR_LABEL = "unknown error";
export const NOT_INSTALLED_LABEL = "Not installed";

export function configuredState(map: Record<string, boolean>, command: string): ConfiguredState   // true -> yes, false -> no, absent -> ask
export function installedView(install: InstallState): AutoUpdateRowView["installed"]
export function liveView(command: string, running: AgentUpdateCommandRef[], results: AgentUpdateResult[]): AutoUpdateRowView["live"]
export function deriveAutoUpdateRows(rows: AgentUpdateOverviewRow[], input: { autoUpdateByCommand: Record<string, boolean>; registeredCommands: string[]; running: AgentUpdateCommandRef[]; results: AgentUpdateResult[] }): AutoUpdateRowView[]
export function describeInstall(install: InstallState | null | undefined): string | null
export function versionTransitionText(before: InstallState | null | undefined, after: InstallState | null | undefined): string | null
export function deriveTimelineNodes(nodes: AgentUpdateNode[], running: AgentUpdateCommandRef[], results: AgentUpdateResult[], installAfter: Record<string, InstallState>): TimelineNodeView[]
export function deriveTimelineHeader(views: TimelineNodeView[]): TimelineHeaderView
```

`installedView`: `checking` -> `{state:"checking", label:"Checking..."}`; `installed` -> `{state:"installed", label: version, title: path}`; `missing` -> `{state:"missing", label: NOT_INSTALLED_LABEL, title: detail}`; `probeFailed` -> `{state:"probe-failed", label: NOT_INSTALLED_LABEL, title: `Version check failed: ${detail} (${path})`}`; `unprobed` -> `{state:"unprobed", label:"Installed", title: `${detail} (${path})`}`. `liveView`: running contains the command -> `updating`; else the result for the command -> `ok` (title none) / `failed` (title = `error ?? "unknown error"`); else `idle`. `deriveAutoUpdateRows` keeps catalog order and duplicates; `registered = registeredCommands.includes(command)`; `configured` is the literal map value (`ask` for an absent key even when the row is not registered: the cell shows the stored policy, and registration is shown by the same row's marker and the hint, Grinch R2 accepted in §10). `describeInstall`: `installed` with a `version` -> the version string; `missing` -> `VERSION_MISSING_LABEL`; `probeFailed` -> `VERSION_UNDETECTED_LABEL`; `unprobed`, `checking`, a version-less `installed`, `null`/`undefined` -> `null` (no claim). `versionTransitionText(before, after)`: `b = describeInstall(before)`, `a = describeInstall(after)`; both non-null -> `${b} → ${a}` (`2.1.34 → 2.1.35`; an unchanged version reads `2.1.35 → 2.1.35`, the honest "nothing changed"; a broken install after the update reads `2.1.34 → no instalada`, the 2026-08-25 incident); only `b` -> `b` (the post-pass probe has not reported, or reports nothing describable: the text grows to the arrow form when the event arrives); only `a` -> `a`; neither -> `null`. Never an invented value, never a placeholder that looks like data. `deriveTimelineNodes`: one view per `nodes` entry in that order: `state` = `failed`/`ok` when `results` has the command (its `ok` flag), else `updating` when `running` has it, else `pending`; `stateText = NODE_STATE_LABELS[state]`; `detail`: `pending`/`updating` -> `null`; `ok` -> `versionTransitionText(node.installBefore, installAfter[command])`; `failed` -> `[result.error ?? UNKNOWN_ERROR_LABEL, versionTransitionText(node.installBefore, installAfter[command])].filter(Boolean).join(" · ")` (`exit code 1 · 2.1.34 → no instalada`; the node's visible line is then `Falló · exit code 1 · 2.1.34 → no instalada`, §5.13); `detailTitle = detail` (the full text for hover; the cell wraps, never truncates). Then, defensively (older backend, lost `started` payload), every command present in `running` or `results` but absent from `nodes` is appended after the nodes, in `running` order then `results` order, with `updateCommands: []` and its label from the ref/result; no command yields two views. `deriveTimelineHeader(views)`: `total = views.length`, `done` = views in `ok` or `failed`, `failed` = views in `failed`, `percent = total ? Math.round((done / total) * 100) : 0`, `text` = `${done} de ${total} completados` plus ` · ${failed} falló` when `failed === 1` and ` · ${failed} fallaron` when `failed > 1` (the prototype's `n de 5 completados · 1 falló`).

### 5.11 `src/sidebar/stores/agent-update-overview.ts` (new; corrections 1, 2, 3)

```ts
/** #1551 - retry/reconciliation interval (PROBE_TIMEOUT 15 s plus a 5 s IPC/UI margin): while the latest
 *  response still reports a `checking` row, the store re-asks the backend once per interval. It is a
 *  re-ask cadence, NOT a completion bound: a re-ask may schedule a replacement probe that itself takes up
 *  to PROBE_TIMEOUT, and while the startup pass is unfinished nothing is scheduled at all. */
export const OVERVIEW_REPOLL_MS = 20_000;

interface OverviewState { rows: AgentUpdateOverviewRow[] | null; loading: boolean; error: string | null; }
// module-level: const [state, setState] = createStore<OverviewState>({ rows: null, loading: false, error: null });
// non-reactive module state: let session = 0; const latest = new Map<string, InstallState>(); let inFlight: Promise<void> | null = null;
//                            let wanted = false; let repoll: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null = null;

export const agentUpdateOverviewStore = {
  state,                                       // read-only accessor for components
  open(): void,                                // one Coding Agents screen mount: session += 1, latest.clear(), cancel repoll, state := { rows: null, loading: false, error: null }
  close(): void,                               // the mount is gone: session += 1 (a response still in flight is discarded on arrival), cancel repoll
  refresh(): Promise<void>,                    // trailing single-flight, below
  applyInstallState(command: string, install: InstallState): void,   // seq rule, below
  resetForTests(): void,                       // close(); open(); inFlight = null; wanted = false  (only between tests)
};
```

Rules (all exact):

- **Sequence rule** `noteInstall(command, install): boolean`: `const cur = latest.get(command); if (cur && install.seq <= cur.seq) return false; latest.set(command, install); return true;`. `latest` is the highest-`seq` state seen per command in this open, from responses and events alike.
- **Response** `applyResponse(rows)`: `for (row of rows) noteInstall(row.command, row.install)`; then `setState({ rows: rows.map((row) => ({ ...row, install: latest.get(row.command) ?? row.install })), error: null })`; then `armRepoll(rows.some((row) => row.install.status === "checking"))` (the test is on the RESPONSE rows: a `checking` in the response means a probe is pending or deferred, even when the displayed cell keeps an older committed state).
- **Event** `applyInstallState(command, install)`: `if (!noteInstall(command, install)) return; if (state.rows) setState("rows", (row) => row.command === command, "install", install);` (every row with that command, so duplicate-command rows move together; an event for a command not in `rows` is remembered in `latest` and applied if a later response lists it).
- **Trailing single-flight** `refresh()`: `wanted = true; if (inFlight) return inFlight; inFlight = (async () => { try { while (wanted) { wanted = false; const mySession = session; setState("loading", true); try { const rows = await AgentUpdateAPI.getOverview(); if (mySession === session) applyResponse(rows); } catch (err) { if (mySession === session) setState("error", err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)); } finally { if (mySession === session) setState("loading", false); } } } finally { inFlight = null; } })(); return inFlight;`. Property: every `refresh()` call is followed by an invoke that STARTS after the call (either the call itself started the loop, or `wanted` was set while an invoke was in flight and the loop runs once more after it); concurrent callers never produce more than one in-flight invoke; `rows` are kept on failure (`null` on a first failure, so the error note shows).
- **Re-poll** `armRepoll(needed)`: cancel the pending timer; if `needed`, `repoll = setTimeout(() => { repoll = null; void refresh(); }, OVERVIEW_REPOLL_MS)`. The timer is armed only by responses and cancelled by `close()`/`open()`; one extra instant, process-free call can happen when events resolved every row before the timer fired (accepted).
- **Sessions**: `open()`/`close()` bump `session`; a response or error whose `mySession` no longer matches is discarded (it belongs to a mount that no longer exists), and `latest` from a previous open is never consulted (cleared by `open()`).

Why the merge is order-independent (proof used by §7): for one command, every committed state carries a unique `seq`, increasing in commit order for the life of the backend (§5.2). A response row is the committed state at the time the response was computed (or `checking`, `seq 0`); an event is a committed state. Whatever the arrival order, the displayed state is `max-seq` over everything received in this open, so (event before response) the response cannot downgrade an already-received newer event, (response before event) the event upgrades the row, (older response after newer event, Tauri reordering) the older response is ignored per row, (newer response after older event) the response wins, and `checking` (`seq 0`) never replaces a committed state, so a post-invalidation response keeps showing the last known state until the re-probe commits a higher `seq`. Termination (liveness, not a fixed bound): while the latest response still reports `checking`, the re-poll asks again every `OVERVIEW_REPOLL_MS`; a fresh overview call re-runs §5.4 step 8, which serves a committed state if one exists, schedules a new probe when the slot is free (`Began`: absent, pass finished), or schedules nothing (`InFlight`, or `Deferred` while the pass is not finished). Therefore a cell reads `Checking...` only while no state was ever committed for that command in this open AND (a probe is in flight, or the pass has not finished); with the block mounted the store never stops re-asking, so the cell converges once any probe commits, and it is never permanent while mounted. The ONLY case with a hard bound is the normal one: the gate already finished and the first call scheduled the first probe at once, where the cell resolves within PROBE_TIMEOUT plus event delivery (the 20 s acceptance of T1, §9.4.2). Abnormal cases have no hard bound and none is claimed: an abandoned probe task (panic) frees its slot and the next re-ask schedules a replacement that may itself run up to PROBE_TIMEOUT (about 20 s + 15 s), repeated aborts repeat that, and an unfinished pass defers everything until `agent_updates_finished` (the pass has its own 300 s per step bound) and the refresh it triggers. A new open re-asks from scratch.

### 5.12 `src/sidebar/components/AgentAutoUpdateStatusList.tsx` (new) and the `SettingsModal` mount (corrections 1, 2; Grinch R4)

Props: `{ autoUpdateByCommand: () => Record<string, boolean>; registeredCommands: () => string[] }` (accessors, so the modal passes its DRAFT store and the table follows the dropdown before Save). Mount sequence (verbatim shape; the store is always addressed through the `agentUpdateOverviewStore` object so tests can spy on its methods). `open()` runs SYNCHRONOUSLY in the component body, before the rows memo is created and before the first render, so a remount never paints the previous mount's rows for a frame (Grinch R4); the listeners and the first refresh run from `onMount`:

```tsx
agentUpdateOverviewStore.open();               // component body: state is { rows: null, loading: false, error: null } before the first render
let disposed = false;
const unlisteners: UnlistenFn[] = [];
onCleanup(() => {
  disposed = true;
  for (const unlisten of unlisteners.splice(0)) unlisten();
  agentUpdateOverviewStore.close();
});
const rows = createMemo(() => deriveAutoUpdateRows(agentUpdateOverviewStore.state.rows ?? [], { autoUpdateByCommand: props.autoUpdateByCommand(), registeredCommands: props.registeredCommands(), running: agentUpdateStore[0].running, results: agentUpdateStore[0].results }));
onMount(() => {
  void (async () => {
    // Total settlement (never Promise.all): a listener that DID register is kept and unlistened on
    // disposal even when the other one rejects; each rejection is logged with its event name; the
    // overview is invoked only after BOTH settled, so no event can precede its listener.
    const settled = await Promise.allSettled([
      onAgentInstallStateChanged((payload) => agentUpdateOverviewStore.applyInstallState(payload.command, payload.install)),
      onAgentUpdatesFinished(() => { void agentUpdateOverviewStore.refresh(); }),
    ]);
    const registered: UnlistenFn[] = [];
    settled.forEach((outcome, index) => {
      if (outcome.status === "fulfilled") registered.push(outcome.value);
      else console.error(`[agent-update-overview] listener ${LISTENER_EVENTS[index]} unavailable (live updates degraded):`, outcome.reason);
    });
    if (disposed) { for (const unlisten of registered) unlisten(); return; }
    unlisteners.push(...registered);
    await agentUpdateOverviewStore.refresh();
  })();
});
```

with the module-level `const LISTENER_EVENTS = ["agent_install_state_changed", "agent_updates_finished"] as const;`. Both `listen` calls SETTLE (fulfilled or rejected) before the first overview invoke, so an `agent_install_state_changed` produced by the probe that this very call schedules is always observed by a listener that exists; a listener that registered is kept and unlistened on disposal even when the other one rejected (a partial failure leaks nothing); a `listen` that resolves after disposal is unlistened immediately; a rejected `listen` is logged per event and only that event is lost for this mount (the table still refreshes on every mount and via the re-poll; a rejected `agent_updates_finished` listener means the post-pass refresh comes from the re-poll or the next mount).

Markup (all copy English, verbatim):

```
<div class="settings-agents-actions-block settings-auto-update-block" data-ac-testid="settings.autoUpdate.block" data-ac-role="region">
  <div class="settings-agents-actions-title">Auto-update</div>
  loading  : <div class="settings-empty-note" data-ac-testid="settings.autoUpdate.loading" data-ac-role="status">Loading auto-update status...</div>      (rows === null && !error)
  error    : <div class="settings-empty-note" data-ac-testid="settings.autoUpdate.error" data-ac-role="status" data-ac-state="error">Auto-update status unavailable: {error}</div>   (error !== null; below the table when rows exist)
  empty    : <div class="settings-empty-note" data-ac-testid="settings.autoUpdate.empty" data-ac-role="status">No coding agent in the catalog supports auto-update.</div>   (rows.length === 0)
  table    : <table class="settings-auto-update-table" aria-label="Auto-update status" data-ac-testid="settings.autoUpdate.list" data-ac-role="table">
               <thead><tr><th scope="col">Agent</th><th scope="col">Command</th><th scope="col">Auto-update</th><th scope="col">Installed</th><th scope="col">Status</th></tr></thead>

"registered" : "unregistered"}><span class="settings-color-dot" style={{ background: row.color }} />{row.label}<Show when={!registered}><span class="settings-auto-update-note" title="Only registered coding agents are updated at startup">(not registered)</span></Show></td>

</div>
```

States are text plus a state attribute plus color, never color alone; the table is non-interactive (no focusable cells; the modal's focus handling is untouched). When both `rows` and `error` are set (a refresh failed after a success) the table stays and the error note renders below it with the same testid.

`SettingsModal.tsx`: import the component next to `:31`; in `renderCodingAgentsScreen`, between the Actions block's closing `</div>` (`:3668`) and `{renderAgentPresets()}` (`:3670`):

```tsx
<AgentAutoUpdateStatusList
  autoUpdateByCommand={() => settings.data?.agentAutoUpdateByCommand ?? {}}
  registeredCommands={() => (settings.data?.agents ?? []).map((agent) => agent.command)}
```

Because the screen renders under `<Show when={activeTab() === "agents"}>` (`:3939`) and the modal unmounts on close (`ActionBar.tsx:387-392`), "every Settings open" and "every switch to the Coding Agents tab" are mounts, each of which runs `open()` + listeners + `refresh()`: within the 10 minute TTL the backend answers from its cache with no process; after it, or after a pass, the backend re-probes. `renderAgentRow`, `autoUpdateEnabled`, `setAgentAutoUpdate`, `handleSave`, and the dropdown JSX stay byte-identical.

### 5.13 `src/sidebar/components/AgentUpdateOverlay.tsx`

Visual authority (round 5): the user's `Opción 2 · Progreso por etapas` of `index-V1.html` (SHA-256 `A5A7149A662EC49802CBE0A7886BA2C6CD05A8984715FDD7D72092306268CEB6`, section `#opcion-2`), a session artifact of ac-dev-webpage-ui-v3 that is NOT added to the repository. The contract below fixes every string, state, testid and rule; the prototype fixes the look (Noir tokens of `src/sidebar/styles/variables.css`, hierarchy, spacing, responsive behavior, accessibility), and §5.14 IS its exact transcription with the production tokens (round 6, FE M6, written by the prototype's author): where §5.14 and the prototype differ, §5.14 wins, and the deliberate differences are exactly the ones §10 records (the `Pendiente` state word in `--sidebar-fg`, the scrolling `max-height: 50vh`, the instant close). A literal appears only where the prototype's value has no token: the `#0a84ff` accent fallback and the mono font stack (§3.4). No fixture data, order, version or command of the prototype is copied: every value on screen comes from `nodes`, `running`, `results` and `installAfter` (§5.9).

`Show when={state.inProgress || state.prompt || state.summary === "shown"}` + `Portal` (was `inProgress || prompt`). The overlay root (`data-ac-testid="agent-update.overlay"`) carries `data-ac-state` = `prompt` (a prompt is shown), `summary` (`state.summary === "shown"`), else `pass`; the card adds the class `agent-update-card--timeline` whenever no prompt is shown (the prompt branch keeps the plain 320 px card of today). Derived: `nodeViews = createMemo(() => deriveTimelineNodes(state.nodes, state.running, state.results, state.installAfter))`, `header = createMemo(() => deriveTimelineHeader(nodeViews()))`, `title = () => state.summary === "shown" ? "Coding agents actualizados" : "Actualizando coding agents..."`.

No-prompt branch (`:66-69`) becomes (all copy Spanish, verbatim; every SVG inline, `aria-hidden`):

```
<div class="agent-update-header" data-ac-testid="agent-update.header">
  <Show when={state.summary !== "shown"}><div class="agent-update-spinner agent-update-spinner--small" aria-hidden="true" /></Show>
  <Show when={state.summary === "shown"}><div class="agent-update-done agent-update-done--small" aria-hidden="true" data-ac-testid="agent-update.done"><CheckIcon /></div></Show>
  <div class="agent-update-header-text">
    <div class="agent-update-text" role="status" aria-live="polite" data-ac-testid="agent-update.title">{title()}</div>
    <Show when={header().total > 0}><div class="agent-update-progress-text" data-ac-testid="agent-update.progress.text" data-ac-done={header().done} data-ac-total={header().total} data-ac-failed={header().failed}>{header().text}</div></Show>
  </div>
</div>
<Show when={header().total > 0}>
  <div class="agent-update-progress" role="progressbar" aria-label="Agentes completados" aria-valuemin="0" aria-valuemax={header().total} aria-valuenow={header().done} data-ac-testid="agent-update.progress"><div class="agent-update-progress-fill" style={{ width: `${header().percent}%` }} /></div>
  <ol class="agent-update-timeline" aria-label="Agentes de la actualización" data-ac-testid="agent-update.timeline" data-ac-role="list">
    <Index each={nodeViews()}>{(node) => (
      <li class="agent-update-node" data-ac-testid={`agent-update.node.${node().command}`} data-ac-role="listitem" data-ac-state={node().state} data-ac-command={node().command} title={node().state === "updating" ? undefined : node().updateCommands.join("\n") || undefined}>
        <span class="agent-update-node-marker" aria-hidden="true">{node().state === "ok" ? <CheckIcon /> : node().state === "failed" ? <CrossIcon /> : null}</span>
        <div class="agent-update-node-body">
          <div class="agent-update-node-label">{node().label}</div>
          <div class="agent-update-node-line">
            <span class="agent-update-node-state" data-ac-testid={`agent-update.node.${node().command}.state`}>{node().stateText}</span>
            <Show when={node().detail}><span class="agent-update-node-sep" aria-hidden="true"> · </span><span class="agent-update-node-detail" data-ac-testid={`agent-update.node.${node().command}.detail`} title={node().detailTitle ?? undefined}>{node().detail}</span></Show>
          </div>
          <Show when={node().state === "updating"}>
            <div class="agent-update-node-command" data-ac-testid={`agent-update.node.${node().command}.command`}><For each={node().updateCommands}>{(step) => <code>{step}</code>}</For></div>
            <div class="agent-update-node-bar" aria-hidden="true" />
          </Show>
        </div>
      </li>
    )}</Index>
  </ol>
</Show>
<Show when={state.summary === "shown"}>
  <div class="agent-update-summary-actions"><button ref={closeButton} class="modal-btn modal-btn-save" onClick={() => dismissAgentUpdateSummary()} data-ac-testid="agent-update.summary.close">Cerrar</button></div>
</Show>
```

`CheckIcon`/`CrossIcon` are two local inline-SVG components (`viewBox="0 0 16 16"`, sized by the §5.14 rules, `stroke="currentColor"`, `aria-hidden="true"`, no external asset); `Index` joins the existing `For`/`Show` imports from `solid-js`. Each node renders its label on one line and, on ONE line below it, the state word followed, when a detail exists, by a visible ` · ` separator (`aria-hidden`) and the detail: the visible text of a failed node is `Falló · exit code 1 · 1.0 → no instalada`, of a finished one `Listo · 2.1.34 → 2.1.35` (FE M4, round 6; `.state` and `.detail` keep their testids and texts, so T4/T6 read them unchanged; there is no head row: the round-5 `.agent-update-node-head` wrapper is gone). `<Index>` (by position) instead of `<For>` because `deriveTimelineNodes` returns new objects on every derivation: with `For` every `<li>` would be recreated on every event and the indeterminate bar and the spinning marker would restart; with `Index` the DOM per position is stable and only attributes and text change (no effect on testids or on the assertions of §9.2). The running node is the only one that prints its update sequence (one `<code>` per step, the exact strings of `updateCommands`: the prototype's emphasis); pending and finished nodes keep the sequence reachable in the item's `title`. With `header().total === 0` (no node known: the instant before `started` on a surface that only has `inProgress` from a snapshot, or an older backend) the card shows the spinner and the title only, exactly as at 92afbfe6. Rows never receive focus; the timeline scrolls inside the card (`max-height: 50vh`, §5.14) so a long catalog never grows the card past the viewport.

Summary and close: `agent_updates_finished` puts the store in `summary: "shown"` (§5.9); the card then shows the final counter and bar, every node terminal, the small spinner replaced by the small green ring with a check (`agent-update.done`, FE M5), the title `Coding agents actualizados`, and the `Cerrar` button, and it stays until the user closes it on THIS surface. `createEffect(() => { if (state.summary === "shown") closeButton?.focus(); })` moves focus to `Cerrar`. Enter does NOT activate the button natively (FE N1, round 6): the capturing `document` handler below sees the keydown first, calls `preventDefault()` and `stopImmediatePropagation()`, and calls `dismissAgentUpdateSummary()` exactly once; the guard `summary !== "shown"` inside `dismissAgentUpdateSummary` makes any second call inert, so there is no double close and no double toast. The scrim covers the app behind exactly as during the pass (it does not make it `inert`: Tab can leave the overlay, a pre-existing property of the prompt branch, FE N5 in §10). The close is instant (`Show` unmounts the overlay); the prototype's 0.35 s fade-out (`index-V1.html:211-213`) is deliberately not reproduced, an accepted simplification recorded in §10 (FE N6). Close paths: the button, Enter, or Escape (handler below); all call `dismissAgentUpdateSummary()` (§5.9), which hides the overlay and shows the failure toasts. No backend call, no persisted state: a browser tab and the desktop close their summaries independently, and a surface that mounts after the pass (snapshot `inProgress: false`) never shows one.

The capturing `document` `keydown` handler (`:53-60`) becomes: `if (event.key !== "Enter" && event.key !== "Escape") return; if (state.prompt) { event.preventDefault(); event.stopImmediatePropagation(); if (answering()) return; void answer(false); return; } if (state.summary === "shown") { event.preventDefault(); event.stopImmediatePropagation(); dismissAgentUpdateSummary(); }`. The prompt branch is the same behavior as today (Enter/Escape answer `No` while a prompt is displayed on that surface; after `agent_update_prompt_closed` cleared `state.prompt` on this surface the handler falls through and, with no summary shown, does nothing); the summary branch is new and reachable only when no prompt is shown. The prompt branch of the markup (`:70-105`) is untouched.

`answer(enabled)` (`:24-50`) changes ONLY its `!applied` branch (R3-M1: a `false` no longer implies "persisted", §5.1). After `AgentUpdateAPI.answer(prompt.command, enabled)` resolves: `if (state.prompt?.command === prompt.command) setState("prompt", null); markPromptClosed(prompt.command);` (P7: the local clear applies only while the answered prompt is still the shown one; a prompt B that arrived during the await stays visible with its buttons re-enabled once `answering()` drops; the answered command is always marked closed, either outcome, so an older snapshot cannot revive it). If `applied` is `false`: `const status = await AgentUpdateAPI.getStatus(); const persisted = status?.answered?.[prompt.command]; if (persisted === true) toastStore.info("Se actualizará en el próximo arranque.", { durationMs: 10_000 }); else if (persisted === false) toastStore.info("No se volverá a preguntar.", { durationMs: 10_000 });` (no toast when the snapshot carries no recorded policy: an older backend, or a failed `getStatus`, which is caught and logged with `console.error("[agent-update] getStatus after answer failed:", err)` and never re-opens the modal). The toast therefore states the policy that WAS persisted (this answer's when it was late, the other surface's when it was superseded), which is exactly true in both `false` cases because the snapshot is computed after the backend recorded the winning answer (the `false` response itself was produced after that record, inside the serialized answer flow). The `catch` of an `answer` IPC failure keeps the modal open and toasts the error, unchanged.

### 5.14 CSS (`src/sidebar/styles/sidebar.css`)

After `.agent-update-prompt-actions` (`:1525`) (round 5: the Option 2 timeline; the round-4 `.agent-update-row*` rules are superseded and never land):

```css
.agent-update-card--timeline { width: 460px; align-items: stretch; }
.agent-update-header { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: var(--spacing-md); }
.agent-update-spinner--small { width: 18px; height: 18px; border-width: 2px; flex: none; }
.agent-update-done { width: 28px; height: 28px; border-radius: 50%; border: 3px solid var(--status-waiting); color: var(--status-waiting); padding: 4px; display: grid; place-items: center; flex: none; }
.agent-update-done--small { width: 18px; height: 18px; border-width: 2px; padding: 2px; }
.agent-update-done svg { width: 100%; height: 100%; display: block; }
.agent-update-header-text { display: flex; flex-direction: column; min-width: 0; }
.agent-update-card--timeline .agent-update-text { text-align: left; }
.agent-update-progress-text { font-family: var(--font-ui); font-size: var(--font-size-sm); color: var(--sidebar-fg-dim); font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums; }
.agent-update-progress { height: 4px; border-radius: 2px; background: var(--sidebar-border); overflow: hidden; }
.agent-update-progress-fill { height: 100%; background: var(--accent-color, #0a84ff); transition: width 0.4s ease; }
.agent-update-timeline { list-style: none; margin: 0; padding: 0; display: flex; flex-direction: column; max-height: 50vh; overflow-y: auto; }
.agent-update-node { position: relative; display: grid; grid-template-columns: 22px 1fr; gap: 0 10px; padding: 6px 8px; border-radius: 6px; border-left: 2px solid transparent; font-family: var(--font-ui); color: var(--sidebar-fg); }
.agent-update-node::before { content: ""; position: absolute; left: 18px; top: 28px; bottom: -6px; width: 2px; background: var(--sidebar-border); }
.agent-update-node:last-child::before { display: none; }
.agent-update-node[data-ac-state="updating"] { background: var(--sidebar-active); border-left-color: var(--accent-color, #0a84ff); }
.agent-update-node-marker { position: relative; z-index: 1; width: 20px; height: 20px; border-radius: 50%; border: 2px solid var(--sidebar-border); background: var(--sidebar-surface); display: grid; place-items: center; padding: 3px; }
.agent-update-node-marker svg { width: 100%; height: 100%; display: block; }
.agent-update-node[data-ac-state="ok"] .agent-update-node-marker { border-color: var(--status-waiting); background: var(--status-waiting); color: var(--sidebar-bg); }
.agent-update-node[data-ac-state="failed"] .agent-update-node-marker { border-color: var(--status-exited); background: var(--status-exited); color: var(--sidebar-bg); }
.agent-update-node[data-ac-state="updating"] .agent-update-node-marker { border-top-color: var(--accent-color, #0a84ff); animation: agent-update-spin 0.9s linear infinite; }
.agent-update-node-body { min-width: 0; display: flex; flex-direction: column; }
.agent-update-node-label { font-size: var(--font-size-md); font-weight: 600; }
.agent-update-node[data-ac-state="pending"] .agent-update-node-label { color: var(--sidebar-fg-dim); font-weight: 400; }
.agent-update-node-line { font-size: var(--font-size-sm); color: var(--sidebar-fg-dim); overflow-wrap: anywhere; }
/* #1551 round 6 - coordinator decision on FE N4: the small state word keeps AA contrast; the pending label and marker stay dimmed like the prototype. */
.agent-update-node[data-ac-state="pending"] .agent-update-node-state { color: var(--sidebar-fg); }
.agent-update-node[data-ac-state="updating"] .agent-update-node-line { color: var(--sidebar-fg); }
.agent-update-node[data-ac-state="ok"] .agent-update-node-line { color: var(--status-waiting); }
.agent-update-node[data-ac-state="failed"] .agent-update-node-line { color: var(--status-exited); }
.agent-update-node-command code { display: block; margin-top: 2px; font-family: "Cascadia Code", "JetBrains Mono", monospace; font-size: 10px; color: var(--sidebar-fg-dim); overflow-wrap: anywhere; }
.agent-update-node-bar { height: 3px; margin-top: 5px; border-radius: 2px; background: linear-gradient(90deg, transparent, var(--accent-color, #0a84ff), transparent); background-size: 200% 100%; animation: agent-update-shimmer 1.2s linear infinite; }
@keyframes agent-update-shimmer { from { background-position: 200% 0; } to { background-position: -200% 0; } }
.agent-update-summary-actions { display: flex; justify-content: flex-end; }
@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  .agent-update-spinner, .agent-update-node[data-ac-state="updating"] .agent-update-node-marker { animation: none; }
  .agent-update-spinner { border-top-color: var(--accent-color, #0a84ff); border-right-color: var(--accent-color, #0a84ff); }
  .agent-update-node[data-ac-state="updating"] .agent-update-node-marker { border-top-color: var(--accent-color, #0a84ff); border-right-color: var(--accent-color, #0a84ff); }
  .agent-update-node-bar { animation: none; background: var(--accent-color, #0a84ff); opacity: 0.5; }
  .agent-update-progress-fill { transition: none; }
}
```

The timeline block above is the exact transcription of the Option 2 geometry (`index-V1.html:264-305`) by the prototype's author (round-5 FE verdict M6): card gap inherited (`.agent-update-card--timeline` does NOT redefine `gap`, so the card keeps the base `--spacing-md`, like the prototype's `.card-v2`), header gap 12 px, small spinner 18 px, node `grid 22px 1fr; gap 0 10px; padding 6px 8px` with a 2 px left border, 20 px marker with a 2 px ring, filled green/red circle with the glyph in `--sidebar-bg` (the prototype's two glyph literals `#0b1a12`/`#2a0c0c` become that token) and a spinning ring while running, label `--font-size-md` 600 (pending 400 dimmed), state line colored per state, mono command 10 px dimmed, `tabular-nums` counter, 0.4 s fill transition, and one connector segment per node (`::before`, hidden on the last node) that scrolls with its node, so the scrolling list never detaches the line. No new tokens; no existing rule changes (the reduced-motion block adds rules, it edits none); `width: 460px` with the base rule's `max-width: 92%` equals `min(460px, 92%)` (361 px at 394 px, the prototype's measurement); `max-height: 50vh` with scroll and `overflow-wrap: anywhere` are plan extensions without a prototype equivalent, kept. Every color is a `src/sidebar/styles/variables.css` token (each verified defined for both themes, §3.4) except the `#0a84ff` fallback production already uses for the spinner (`:1503`; `--accent-color` is defined nowhere under `src/`), and the mono stack is the literal `"Cascadia Code", "JetBrains Mono", monospace` that `sidebar.css` already uses (for example `:909`) because `--font-mono` is consumed but never defined (§3.4). One deliberate deviation from the prototype (coordinator decision on FE N4): the state word `Pendiente` uses `--sidebar-fg` (AA contrast for small text) while the pending label and marker keep the dimmed treatment; §9.2 pins it with a class/token assertion. With these rules the prototype's Noir mapping (its fidelity report of 2026-08-26 03:02 UTC) resolves to exactly these tokens, geometry included.

### 5.15 Docs (owner ac-technical-writer-v3, §12 step 8; copy verbatim from here, then verify every behavioral claim against the implemented branch)

- `docs/features/agent-auto-update.md`: (a) in "What it does", after the overlay sentence, add: "While the pass runs, the overlay shows every coding agent of the pass as a step of a timeline: `Pendiente` until its update starts, `Actualizando...` with the exact update command while it runs, then `Listo` or `Falló` with the reason; a counter (`2 de 3 completados · 1 falló`) and a progress bar sit above the list. When AC could read the agent's version before and after the update, the finished step shows the transition (`2.1.34 → 2.1.35`; `2.1.34 → no instalada` when the update left the CLI broken). When everything finished the card stays on screen as a summary until you press `Cerrar` (or Enter/Escape); failures are also shown as notifications after you close it. The startup question is one question across every window: answering it on the desktop or in a browser remote tab closes it everywhere, the first answer wins, and a later answer from another window changes nothing." (b) new section `## The Auto-update list in Settings` before `## Troubleshooting`: "Settings > Coding Agents shows a read-only **Auto-update** table with one row per catalog entry that ships `updateCommands` (Cursor ships none, so it is not listed). Per row: **Auto-update** shows your remembered answer (`Yes`, `No`, or `Will ask at startup` when AC has never asked), **Installed** shows the detected version, `Installed` when the command resolves but AC does not run a version probe for it, `Not installed` when the command is not found or its version check fails (hover for the reason and the resolved path), and `Checking...` until the first check completes, and **Status** shows `Updating...` while the startup pass is updating that command and `Updated` or `Update failed` for the outcome of this AC start. Rows marked `(not registered)` are supported but not registered in `agents[]`, so they are never updated at startup. The table never changes settings: use the Auto-update dropdown of the corresponding agent to change an answer. Version checks run `<command> --version` for the built-in coding agents only, in the background, with a 15 second bound, and read the version from the first non-empty line of the output. AC asks again each time you open the Coding Agents screen: within 10 minutes of the last check it answers from its cache without running anything, after that it checks again. A version check never overlaps an update of the same agent: before each startup update AC reads that agent's current version (built-in agents only, 15 second bound), the update starts only after that reading, the Settings checks wait until the pass is over, and right after the pass AC re-checks the agents it updated; the reading before and the one after are the transition the overlay shows." (c) Troubleshooting entries: **"The list says `Not installed` but I can run the agent from my shell."** AC resolves the command with the PATH of the AC process (started from Explorer or the Start menu), which can differ from your shell's PATH; a CLI installed after AC started, or into a user-only directory, reads as not installed until AC restarts. Hover the cell: the reason says whether the command was not found or its `--version` failed. **"`Not installed` right after an update."** An interrupted update can leave a broken install (for example an npm shim whose package is gone); the version check then fails and the row reports `Not installed` with the exit code. Re-run the vendor's install command. (d) "See also": unchanged.
- `docs/integrations/coding-agents.md`, after the backfill paragraph (`:90`): "Entries with a non-empty `updateCommands` are the rows of the Settings > Coding Agents **Auto-update** table (see [Coding agent auto-update](../features/agent-auto-update.md)). There is no `versionCommand` field: AC detects installed versions with a built-in `--version` probe for the built-in commands (`claude`, `codex`, `hermes`, `pi`, `opencode`, `agy`), only for bare command names resolved through PATH; a custom entry or an explicit path shows `Installed` without a version."
- `docs/reference/settings.md:80` (`agentAutoUpdateByCommand` row): append to the description: "Settings > Coding Agents shows the current value per update-capable agent in the read-only Auto-update table. The startup question writes this map once per coding agent per start: the first answer, from any window, is the one stored."
- `docs/reference/architecture.md`: row `:310` becomes `| AgentUpdateAPI | \`commands/config.rs\` | \`get_agent_update_status\`, \`agent_update_answer\`, \`get_agent_update_overview\` |`; after the `update_check.rs` row (`:727`) add `| \`agent_update.rs\`, \`agent_version.rs\` | Startup coding-agent update pass, gate and prompt answer flow; installed-version probe engine and cache (#1551) |`.

### 5.16 Rejected alternatives (recorded, closed)

- Awaiting probes inside the overview call: rejected because the WebSocket router is serial per connection (§3.3) and a 15 s stall would block `pty_write`; the two-phase design keeps every transport call instant.
- A `versionCommand` catalog field: rejected (dispatch): consent-free execution of a project-authored string.
- Extending `get_agent_update_status` with install data: rejected: it is called on every sidebar mount and must stay process-free (`answered` is a map copy, process-free).
- Deduplicating duplicate-command rows on the backend: rejected (dispatch): rows mirror the catalog.
- A non-blocking overlay: rejected (dispatch): gate semantics unchanged; progress rows go into the overlay.
- Eager re-probe after the pass without a client asking: rejected: six processes after every boot with Settings closed; the cache is invalidated and the FE refreshes on `agent_updates_finished`.
- A `refresh` argument on the overview command: rejected (YAGNI): per-mount refresh + TTL + pass invalidation + in-flight tickets cover every trigger the UI has.
- Buffering install events only while `rows === null` and applying them over `checking` cells (round-2 candidate): rejected: without a sequence a client cannot order an event against a response, and Tauri delivers an older response after a newer event; the `seq` stamp makes the merge one comparison and covers every interleaving (§5.11).
- Making the update phase wait for in-flight probes (round-2 candidate for correction 7): rejected: the pass would depend on the probe cache; scheduling probes only after `is_finished()` needs no wait and is provable (§5.4 step 8).
- Timer polling as the primary mechanism: rejected; the 20 s re-poll is only the safety net for a dropped frame (§3.3 queue-full removal), an abandoned probe task, or a pass that finished before a client had any listener.
- Separate `lookup` then `try_begin` in the overview (round 2): rejected (Rust R1): two lock takes leave a window in which a commit lands between them and a second ticket opens; `lookup_or_begin` is one critical section.
- `mark_finished` before `invalidate_all` in the guard drop (round 2): rejected (Rust R2): a caller observing `finished` between the two could open a ticket in the old generation; the inverted order makes `finished` imply the new generation (§5.4 step 4).
- A "claim then persist then commit, roll back on failure" reservation for the answer (round-3 candidate): rejected: a second answer arriving inside the claim window would have to be refused or dropped, and if the first persist then failed that second answer would be lost (its surface would believe the prompt closed while the backend still waited); the FIFO async mutex keeps the second answer queued so it wins whenever the first fails.
- Per-command answer locks: rejected (YAGNI): prompts are sequential, at most one is pending, and the critical section is a settings write of milliseconds.
- A richer `agent_update_answer` result (`applied | superseded | late`): rejected (coordinator: the result stays `boolean`); the persisted policy is exposed additively through `AgentUpdateStatus.answered`, which the overlay reads after a `false` (§5.13).
- Disambiguating a `false` answer on the client from `closedPrompts` alone: rejected: on Tauri the `prompt_closed` event and the `false` response carry no mutual order (§3.3), so the toast could name the wrong policy in a sub-second window; the snapshot read is ordered after the record by construction.
- Releasing the prompt loop inside the claim (the round-3 candidate `settle_answer`, which sent on the oneshot under the state lock and emitted the closure afterwards): rejected (P7): the send wakes the loop, which can register and emit the next prompt before the closure is emitted, so a WebSocket client could receive `prompt(B)` before `prompt_closed(A)` and an unconditional client clear would erase B; the claim hands the sender back unsent and the closure is emitted first.
- Emitting the closure under the gate's state lock to make the order structural: rejected: every emit stays outside std locks (the WebSocket broadcaster takes its own mutex and Tauri may run listeners inline); ownership of the removed entry plus the serial section gives the same order without nesting locks.
- A persisted last-known version for the `before` value (round-5 candidate): rejected: a state-file schema change (binding out-of-scope) that would show a previous boot's value as if it were current; the pre-update probe reads the real value at the cost of about one second per target (§10).
- Reading the version transition from the update command's output (round-5 candidate): rejected: the runner's output is heterogeneous, log-only and capped; no reliable source.
- Per-step progress events (round-5 candidate): rejected: `run_update_sequence` is out of scope and every built-in sequence has one step; the timeline shows the configured sequence.
- Serializing the update phase so the timeline advances one node at a time (round-5 candidate): rejected by the dispatch: the timeline is presentation, the backend stays concurrent.
- Auto-closing the summary after a delay, or restoring it from a snapshot (round-5 candidates): rejected: the user asked for an explicit close, and a snapshot cannot tell whether this surface watched the pass.
- Scheduling the post-pass probes from the overlay through `get_agent_update_overview` (round-5 candidate): rejected: it would probe every catalog row (six processes) instead of the updated commands only and would depend on a client being mounted; the backend schedules exactly the updated commands, single-flight with Settings (§5.4 step 9b).
- A separate `AgentUpdateTimeline.tsx` component (round-5 candidate): rejected (YAGNI): one consumer, one test file; the overlay's no-prompt branch is the timeline and the derivations already have their file (§5.10).
