Must stay byte-identical: `src-tauri/src/web/event_broadcast.rs`, `src-tauri/src/web/broadcast.rs`, `src-tauri/src/config/coding_agents_catalog.rs`, `src-tauri/src/config/settings.rs`, `src-tauri/resources/coding-agents/agents.default.json`, `src-tauri/Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `src/shared/agent-presets.ts`, `src/sidebar/stores/coding-agents.ts`, `src/sidebar/App.tsx`, `src/browser/App.tsx`, `src/shared/testing/fake-transport.ts`, `src/shared/testing/ui-harness.tsx`, `src/sidebar/components/SettingsModal.rail-selection.test.tsx`, `src/sidebar/components/SettingsModal.reseed.test.tsx`, `src/sidebar/components/SettingsModal.watchers.test.tsx`, every `tests/*_layering.rs`, `tauri.conf.json` and the version files. Function-level preserve set inside modified files (round 5, checked by the code review of §12 step 10): `build_update_plan`, `run_update_sequence`, `output_tail`, the six `runner_*` tests, the three existing gate tests, `resolve_answer`, `was_prompted`, `mark_started`, `persist_narrow_settings_update`, `setAgentAutoUpdate`, `handleSave`, the dropdown JSX, the overlay's prompt branch markup, and `answer()` except its `!applied` branch (§5.13). Outside the repo: no user-installed binary, npm shim, user/machine `PATH`, or production AC config directory is modified by anyone in this delivery.

## 7. Required behavior, edge cases, failure behavior

- **Rows.** Catalog order, one row per update-capable entry, duplicates kept with identical command-keyed install/live/configured cells; cursor absent; a catalog with zero update-capable entries renders the explicit empty note; a failed overview call renders the explicit error note (never the FE fallback catalog, never an empty table).
- **Tri-state.** From the modal draft: flipping the dropdown updates the table before Save; Save persists exactly as before; a prompt answered on the backend is reflected the next time the modal opens (the modal re-fetches settings on open; the shared `settingsStore` is not refreshed by `agent_update_answer`, unchanged and accepted).
- **Freshness.** Every Coding Agents screen mount (every Settings open, every switch to the tab) opens its session synchronously, registers its listeners, then invokes the overview once, plus one trailing invoke for every refresh request that arrived while an invoke was in flight (`agent_updates_finished`, the re-poll, or a remount). Within the TTL the backend answers from the cache with no process; an expired entry is removed and re-probed; a process runs only for a `Began` command after the pass is finished.
- **Install states.** First overview call after start: every uncached row `Checking...` while the six probes run in parallel (each bounded by 15 s; the whole set by max, not sum); each committed result arrives per row via `agent_install_state_changed`; within 10 minutes a second call serves the cache with no process; overlapping calls (two clients, or a remount) never open two tickets and never run two probe processes for one command (single-lock `lookup_or_begin`, §5.2; a commit that lands between two calls is served to the second call); a probe task panic frees its ticket (Drop) and the next overview call schedules a fresh probe.
- **Ordering / merge (install).** Every committed state has a strictly increasing `seq`; a client applies an incoming state (event or response row) to a row only when its `seq` is higher than the row's current `seq`; `checking` (`seq 0`) never replaces a committed state. Event-before-response, response-before-event, an older response after a newer event, and duplicate-command rows all converge to the newest committed state (§5.11 proof). While the latest response still reports a `checking` row the store re-asks every 20 s (a retry interval, not a completion bound): `Checking...` persists only while no state was ever committed for that command in this open and a probe is pending or deferred; it converges once any probe commits and is never permanent while the block is mounted (the re-ask never stops), but its duration is bounded only in the normal case (pass finished, first probe scheduled at once: PROBE_TIMEOUT plus delivery); after an abandoned probe or during an unfinished pass it lasts longer (§5.11). A new open starts over.
- **Probe policy.** A process is spawned only for a bare command token whose stem is one of the six built-ins; explicit paths and unknown stems report presence only (`Installed` without version); a missing bare token is `Not installed` with the reason; a resolved token whose `--version` exits non-zero, times out, or prints no version token on its first non-empty line is `Not installed` with the sanitized reason and path in the tooltip (the incident's dangling shim lands here). Leading blank lines and lines that are only ANSI control sequences never hide a version (§5.2 line-wise rule).
- **Prompt lifecycle (G).** One prompt per command per boot, shown on every surface that has the sidebar mounted (desktop window, browser tabs). The first answer to persist, from any surface, records the policy, claims the pending entry, emits `agent_update_prompt_closed {command, label}` to every surface, and only then releases the loop (which may show the next prompt): on every WebSocket client queue and in the backend's emit order the closure of A precedes the prompt of B, and a surface clears its prompt on a closure only when the closure names the shown prompt, so a closure can never erase a newer prompt, and Enter/Escape on a closed prompt answer nothing (the handler and the buttons are inert once `state.prompt` is null; no second `agent_update_answer` call is made). An answer that reaches the backend after the record (a keypress in the sub-second window before the closed event arrived, or a stale surface) returns `false` and persists nothing; its surface clears the prompt only if it is still the shown one and toasts the policy that was actually persisted (read from the snapshot's `answered`). An answer after the 60 s timeout (the loop's expiry arm removed the entry and emitted the closure) persists for future boots, is recorded, and returns `false` with the same toast; a second late answer is refused the same way (`false`, nothing persisted): at most one persisted answer per prompted command per boot, and the persisted policy always equals the choice the loop acted on when the loop acted at all. The expiry arm and the answer claim compete inside the gate's serial section for the single pending entry: whichever removes it emits the one and only closure of that prompt; an answer whose claim finds the receiver already dead still owns and emits the closure and returns `false`. A failed settings write leaves the prompt pending and the map untouched, returns the error to that surface (modal stays open for retry) and lets the next queued answer proceed. Two simultaneous answers are serialized (FIFO) by the gate's async mutex; no std lock is held across the persist await; no await separates claim, closure emit and release. Every answer that passed the `not prompted` check writes one `[agent-update] answer for '<command>'` log line.
- **Ordering / merge (prompt).** `prompt_closed` is monotonic per command on the client (`closedPrompts`): a snapshot computed before the closure and delivered after it cannot resurrect that prompt (Tauri response-after-event reordering included), while a snapshot or event carrying a NEW prompt (another command) still appears; a snapshot with `prompt: null` never clears a prompt the client already shows; a closure clears the shown prompt only when it names it (a delayed `prompt_closed(A)` never erases `B`; an older backend's `null` payload clears unconditionally); the overlay's own completed `answer(A)` clears only while `A` is still shown and marks `A` closed locally (either outcome), so even a snapshot that arrives before the closed event is inert. `agent_updates_started` resets `closedPrompts` (one pass per process; the reset is for tests and store hygiene).
- **During the pass.** No cache probe is scheduled while the gate is not finished (prompt phase included): uncached rows read `Checking...` and cached rows keep their pre-pass state; rows of running commands show `Updating...`. The only probes of the pass are the pre-update probes of §5.4 step 5: one per target, direct (no ticket), inside that target's own task and strictly before its update, so a probe process never overlaps an update of the same CLI (§5.4 step 8 proof, extended in step 9b). At the end of a real pass the guard runs `finish_pass`: it invalidates the cache (generation bump, `seq` kept) while the gate is still un-finished, then marks the gate finished, then emits `agent_updates_finished`; an overview that observes `finished` therefore always observes the new generation, and the refresh a client runs on the event schedules the re-probes and can never be served a pre-pass entry nor open a pre-pass ticket (§5.4 step 8 boundary proof). In production no ticket is open at invalidation time (none is opened before `finished`), so the ticket-renewal path is exercised only by tests (Rust R3). Per-command results appear as `Updated` / `Update failed` (tooltip = error) for the rest of this AC start; a late-mounting client restores `running` and `results` from the snapshot through the monotonic merge. Right after `finish_pass` the pass task schedules the post-pass probes of the updated commands (§5.4 step 9b): their `agent_install_state_changed` events follow `agent_updates_finished` on every WebSocket queue, feed the timeline's `installAfter` and Settings' cache alike, and cannot double-probe a command the Settings refresh already took (single-flight).
- **Ordering / merge (pass).** `running` is the union of the store's events and the snapshot, minus commands that have a result in either source; `agent_updates_finished` is authoritative: it clears `running` even if a `command_finished` was lost, and after it no snapshot can resurrect `inProgress`, `running`, or a `prompt` (only results merge); a `command_finished` arriving before the snapshot is not resurrected; a `command_started` event is never replaced by an older snapshot; `command_started` for an already-running command is ignored; a panicked update task is settled by `settle_joined_update` (its `command_finished` reaches every surface and it leaves `running`). Round 5 (rules corrected in round 6): `nodes` come from `started` or the snapshot and only shrink (`command_skipped`, or an in-pass snapshot (`inProgress: true`) that no longer lists a node; a snapshot computed before the pass (`inProgress: false`, no nodes) never shrinks them, a post-pass snapshot only seeds a store without nodes, and a command in `skippedNodes` is filtered out of every merged set); a `command_started` for a command absent from `nodes` appends a defensive node; `installBefore` moves only from absent to present; `installAfter` keeps the highest `seq` per pass node (finished or not: a post-pass event reordered ahead of its `command_finished` is kept and shown once the result arrives) and ignores install events for commands outside `nodes`; `summary` is entered only by the `finished` EVENT on a surface that was showing the pass and left only by the user's close on that surface.
- **Overlay (Option 2, round 5).** From `agent_updates_started` every surface without a shown prompt paints the timeline with every node of the pass `Pendiente` (dimmed) and the counter `0 de N completados`; each node turns `Actualizando...` (accent border, surface background, its update sequence printed, indeterminate bar) on its `command_started` (all targets start together after the prompt phase, each right after its own pre-update probe, so in the normal case the nodes turn within about a second of each other and `Pendiente` is the pre-probe window plus whatever this surface has not yet seen), then `Listo` (check) or `Falló` (cross, `Falló · <reason>` on one line) on its `command_finished`; the counter and the bar follow (`done/total`, ` · k falló`/`fallaron`). A prompted target answered `No` or expired leaves the timeline on `command_skipped` and the total shrinks. Nodes are in pass order (catalog order) on every surface and never re-sort; duplicates cannot exist (one node per command by construction). Finished nodes gain the version transition when the post-pass probe reports (`installAfter` by max `seq`), in the exact forms of §5.10 and never an invented value. On `agent_updates_finished` with at least one result (one per node that stayed in the pass) the card becomes the summary and stays until the user closes it on that surface (`Cerrar`, Enter, Escape); the failure toasts then appear; a pass whose every prompt was answered `No` (no node, empty results) closes the overlay on `finished` as today; a surface that never showed the pass never shows a summary; a surface mounted in the middle of the pass (snapshot `inProgress: true`) toasts nothing at mount and enters the summary on `finished` like any other surface showing the pass, while a surface mounted after the pass (snapshot `inProgress: false`) toasts at once and shows no summary (round 6). During the prompt phase the prompt branch replaces the timeline on that surface (unchanged blocking and keyboard semantics); a panicked update task still produces `command_finished` and `mark_finished` clears `running` regardless.
- **Browser mode.** The five commands work and every event arrives; the five arms bypass the `RestoreInProgress` guard so a browser client can answer the prompt and read status while restore waits on the gate; all other commands keep the guard. A browser client that opens Settings during the pass sees `Checking...`/pre-pass states and `Updating...`, then the post-pass refresh. A browser tab open during startup now sees the blocking overlay and the prompt exactly like the desktop (Rust R12; stated in the PR description, §12 step 15).
- **Failure behavior.** No new panics: every std lock uses `unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner())`, every `try_state` miss becomes an error string, every probe failure is a state (a pre-update probe included: it becomes the node's `installBefore`, never a pass error), every FE fetch failure is a visible state, a rejected `listen` is logged per event while the surviving listener is kept (and unlistened on disposal), the table degrading to per-mount refresh plus re-poll for the missing event only; a failed settings write in the answer path is returned to the caller with nothing changed. Probe processes never inherit a console window (Windows) and always die on timeout (job object / process group), and `kill_on_drop` covers task cancellation. A response from a mount that was closed meanwhile is discarded.
- **Determinism.** Same catalog, same PATH, same cache -> same rows; sorting rules are total (catalog order for the Settings table and, round 5, for the timeline: pass order == catalog order, defensive appends after it in arrival order); the merge rules are commutative per command (`max-seq`; union then results filter; closed-prompt set); the answer order is the FIFO order of the gate's async mutex.

## 8. Compatibility and security

- Additive wire changes: `AgentUpdateStatus.running`, `AgentUpdateStatus.answered`, `AgentUpdateStatus.nodes` (round 5), and `InstallState.seq` are new fields (old clients ignore them; the new client tolerates a missing `running`/`answered`/`nodes`), four new events (`command_started`, `command_finished`, `install_state_changed`, and, round 5, `command_skipped`), one new command, no change to the existing commands' arguments or result types. Two existing events change their payload: `agent_update_prompt_closed` carries `AgentUpdateCommandRef` instead of `null`, and (round 5) `agent_updates_started` carries `{ nodes }` instead of `null`; their only consumers at 92afbfe6 (`onAgentUpdatePromptClosed`, `onAgentUpdatesStarted`, §3.4) discard the payload, and the new consumers accept `null` from an older backend, so no version skew can break either side. `agent_update_command_started` carries `AgentUpdateNode` (new in this delivery; nothing at 92afbfe6 emits or consumes it). `settings.json`, `agents.json`, and every persisted file are untouched; no migration.
- Execution surface: the probe runs `<resolved program> --version` and only for bare tokens of the six built-in stems resolved through the daemon's PATH (the same `probe_command_install_state` policy for the Settings probes, the pre-update probes and the post-pass probes: round 5 adds no new way to execute anything); no catalog, settings, project, or client string reaches argv; stdin is closed; output is capped, ANSI/control-stripped, truncated, and only its first non-empty line is ever serialized or logged; the resolved path is reported so the user can see which binary answered (the `agent` collision is the motivating case, even though cursor is never probed). A probe of a CLI never runs while that CLI's updater runs: cache probes wait for `finished`, the pre-update probe of a target ends before that target's update starts, and the post-pass probes start after every update ended (§5.4 steps 5, 8, 9b), so a probe can never hold a CLI executable open while its updater replaces it.
- WebSocket: the new arms are read-only except `agent_update_answer`, which keeps the `not prompted` gate, gains "one persisted answer per prompted command per boot", and uses the existing persist path; the pre-guard route is limited to the five named commands and documented in place; browser clients already pass the WS auth layer before reaching `dispatch`. The catalog (`get_coding_agent_catalog`) and overview rows (resolved program paths) become readable by authenticated browser clients: the same trust boundary as `get_settings` (already routed), whose route omits only `root_token`; the catalog holds no secret by contract (`envs` are documented project configuration, `envs: []` in every embedded entry, §3.2), so nothing is redacted (Grinch R6 accepted in §10).
- Update commands on screen (round 5): the timeline prints the exact `updateCommands` strings of the catalog (project `.ac` or embedded), the same strings `get_coding_agent_catalog` already returns to every authenticated client: no new exposure, nothing redacted, no secret by contract; the overlay shows the user precisely what the pass executes, which is the point (a project-authored sequence is visible in Settings before it runs on the next boot and in the overlay while it runs).
- Resource bounds: at most one probe process per command at a time (single-lock scheduling for cache probes; the pass's direct pre-probe is sequenced before that command's update while no cache probe can exist), at most two per command per overview call, 15 s each, plus, per boot with updates, one pre-update probe per target (in parallel across targets, each before its own update) and one post-pass probe per updated command (through the cache, single-flight), 4 KiB retained per stream, 10 minute TTL, in-memory only; the client re-poll is at most one instant, process-free call per 20 s per mounted block and only while a response reports `checking`; the answer path holds the gate's async mutex for one settings write; the update runner, its timeouts, and the gate wait are unchanged.
- Layering: no lower layer gains a transport dependency (§11); `agent_version` is a sink; `agent_update` already owns `AppHandle` and gains the dual-transport emitter the way `commands::project_settings` did (#1265 pattern); the answer flow lives in `agent_update` and receives the persist as a closure, so `agent_update` does not name `commands::config`.

`snapshot unions running with newer command_started events` (event first: `running [codex]`, snapshot `running []` -> still `[codex]`); `snapshot adds running commands the store missed`; `results in either source remove a command from running` (both directions); ### 9.3 Gates (all must be green on the final branch head; run from PowerShell, not the Bash tool, which breaks the `file://` remote of `cli_agency_templates`)

1. `cargo check --all-targets`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --lib --bins --tests` (the CI invocations) in `src-tauri`, plus `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` locally so a red layering guard cannot hide behind an earlier failure.
2. `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run build`.
3. `npm run check:frontend-dependencies` (run by hand; CI does not run it). Red criteria: `errors` must be `0`, the fixture matrix must be `PASS`, and the tracked-source check (`scripts/check-frontend-dependencies.mjs:244-252`) must not report any skipped/excluded/unresolved `src/**/*.ts|tsx` file. The `modules` and `dependencies` numbers are REPORTED as measured (baseline `344` / `1498`; the six new `src/` files are expected to raise `modules` by six because every tracked source must resolve, but the resolver's total is what it prints, and a different measured value is not a failure by itself, Rust R10).
4. Dependency-cycle gate of §11: `moduleCycles` 1 -> 1, the 85-member SCC set-identical to the baseline, the arc record regenerated and committed, its diff exactly the enumerated lines.
5. `git diff --stat` touches only the §6 files; `Cargo.toml`, lockfiles, `package.json`, `agents.default.json`, `agent-presets.ts`, `coding-agents.ts`, `event_broadcast.rs`, `fake-transport.ts`, `ui-harness.tsx` byte-identical.

### 9.4 Post-code black-box validation (NOT owned by the implementers; correction 8)

The dev implementers own code and the §9.1-§9.3 gates only; they run no GUI, browser, or black-box check. Delivery order is fixed by the workgroup's mandatory delivery workflow (§12 steps 9-15): dev commits + §9.1-§9.3 gates -> Grinch code review of the exact plan digest and the exact implementation commit range (`PASS` required) -> 9.4.1 shipper builds the exact reviewed head -> 9.4.2 tester validates that exact receipt (interactive parts only after 9.4.3) -> branch-current gate -> PR landing. A code defect found by the tester loops back: dev rework + gates -> Grinch re-review -> new receipt -> focused retest. A finding that requires changing this frozen plan stops the loop and returns to planning and plan approval.

**9.4.1 Artifact provenance (owner ac-shipper-v3).** Input: the exact reviewed head (full SHA) that carries the Grinch code-review `PASS` (§12 step 10). On a clean checkout of that commit in `repo-AgentsCommander` (`git status --porcelain` empty, `git rev-parse HEAD` equal to the reviewed SHA), first verify the contract at that commit: `npm run` lists `build:prod:no-bundle`, `package.json` still defines it as `cross-env BUILD_PROFILE=prod tauri build --no-bundle --config src-tauri/tauri.prod.conf.json && node scripts/copy-testable-binary.mjs`, and `scripts/copy-testable-binary.mjs` still copies `target/release/agentscommander.exe` to `target/release/agentscommander_testeable.exe` (§3.7); then run exactly `npm run build:prod:no-bundle` (raw production build, no bundle, then the testable copy). Expected artifacts (repository paths, fixed): raw production `D:\0_repos\AgentsCommander_iac\.ac\wg-13-ac-dev-team-v3\repo-AgentsCommander\target\release\agentscommander.exe` and its byte-identical testable sibling `D:\0_repos\AgentsCommander_iac\.ac\wg-13-ac-dev-team-v3\repo-AgentsCommander\target\release\agentscommander_testeable.exe` (the testable mode is unlocked by the filename; config directory, mutex, and ports follow the filename). Receipt (every field mandatory): both absolute paths; the commit SHA and the clean-tree proof; the exit code of each build step (`tauri build --no-bundle`, the copy script) and of the CI-equivalent launch smoke `npm run smoke:cli-release-windows` with its log location (`artifacts/cli-release-smoke`); the SHA-256 of both files, which MUST be identical (raw-production hash comparison); file size and last-write time of both; the version printed by `agentscommander_testeable.exe --version` when the CLI exposes it, else the executable's VersionInfo `FileVersion`/`ProductVersion`; the exact commands used. Rules: the copy-only `npm run build:testable` is NOT acceptable for this delivery, because the executable embeds no commit stamp (`src-tauri/build.rs` has no git stamping, §3.7), so a pre-existing `agentscommander.exe` cannot be proven to belong to the reviewed head; no rebuild is used as verification (release builds are not byte-reproducible); the artifact under test is the one whose hash is in the receipt; the shipper never overwrites an installed or production executable outside this repository's `target/release`.

**9.4.2 Black-box validation (owner ac-cli-tester-v3), on the exact 9.4.1 artifact, in isolated state.** Derive every verb and flag from the binary's `--help` and the testing docs (§3.7), and every action and `data-ac-testid` from the LIVE DOM of that artifact through its semantic UI automation (the testids quoted below, including `settings.agent.addCustom` at `SettingsModal.tsx:3042` and `settings.save` at `:3968`, are what 92afbfe6 plus this plan define; the tester records the ones actually found and reports any mismatch as a finding instead of assuming). Use the deterministic mode `agentscommander_testeable.exe --app --ui-automation` after `agentscommander_testeable.exe test-reset --confirm-testeable` (only while no testable GUI runs); the executable name isolates config directory, mutex, and ports from any production AC. Fixtures are disposable and tester-owned under the tester's evidence root, and the launch environment is a PROCESS-level `PATH` set in the launching shell (`$env:PATH` before starting the exe), never the user or machine `PATH`:

- `F-missing`: `PATH` = the current `PATH` with every directory that contains `codex.cmd` removed (verify in that shell: `Get-Command codex -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` returns nothing).
- `F-stub`: a fixture directory prepended to `PATH` holding `codex.cmd` = `@echo off` / `exit /b 3` (verify: `codex --version; $LASTEXITCODE -eq 3`).
- `F-pass`: a disposable project folder (registered through the GUI, §3.7 rules) containing `.ac\coding-agents\agents.json` `{"schemaVersion":1,"agents":[{"key":"bob","label":"Bob","description":"disposable stub","color":"#334155","command":"bob-stub","instructionsFilename":"BOB.md","envs":[],"isolatedHome":false,"configSeed":{"enabled":false,"dest":".bob"},"removable":true,"updateCommands":["bob-stub up"]},{"key":"bad","label":"Bad","description":"disposable failing stub","color":"#7f1d1d","command":"bad-stub","instructionsFilename":"BAD.md","envs":[],"isolatedHome":false,"configSeed":{"enabled":false,"dest":".bad"},"removable":true,"updateCommands":["bad-stub up"]}]}`, plus `bob-stub.cmd` = `@echo off` / `ping -n 21 127.0.0.1 >NUL` / `exit /b 0` and `bad-stub.cmd` = `@echo off` / `ping -n 21 127.0.0.1 >NUL` / `exit /b 1` on the fixture `PATH`: each stub stays running for the observation window W of about 20 s (`ping -n 21` waits 20 one-second intervals on Windows) before exiting with its intended code; verify in the fixture shell that `bob-stub up` takes 19-22 s and returns 0 and `bad-stub up` takes 19-22 s and returns 1. PRECONDITION (Grinch R3): the disposable project must be the FIRST entry of `project_paths` in the testable instance's settings (after `test-reset` the instance has no projects, so registering exactly this one project through the GUI makes it first; verify by reading the testable config directory's settings file read-only before the relaunch); `load_catalog_for_settings` uses only the `.ac` of the first `project_paths` entry (§3.2), so with the fixture first the active catalog is exactly `[bob, bad]`: no built-in row, no built-in prompt, and no vendor CLI is ever updated. Both agents are registered as custom agents through Settings (the add-custom action, label/command fields, then Save) WITHOUT touching their Auto-update dropdown, so `agentAutoUpdateByCommand` has no key for `bob-stub` or `bad-stub` (the dropdown displays `No` for an absent key but writes only on change, §3.4 `autoUpdateEnabled`/`setAgentAutoUpdate`); before the relaunch the Auto-update table shows `Will ask at startup` (`data-ac-state="ask"`) for both rows (a (B) check); then the app is relaunched: the pass prompts for Bob, then Bad (sequential, 60 s each, §3.1), and runs the updates the answers allow. The prompts are answered `Sí` (`agent-update.prompt.yes`): on the desktop in a desktop-only run, or as T5-prompt describes when the browser tab is in play; `Sí` here runs only the disposable stubs.

Cases, each recording binary identity, environment, action, expected, observed, exit status, evidence, and cleanup (T0 = the moment `settings.autoUpdate.list` first exists after opening the Coding Agents screen; every cell is read through the semantic UI automation of the testable app by `data-ac-testid`, `data-ac-state`, text, and `title`). Prompt rule for T1-T3 (Grinch R3): if the testable profile starts with built-in agents registered and without a policy, the startup prompt appears for each of them (60 s each); the tester answers `No` (`agent-update.prompt.no`) to EVERY such prompt before T0 and records it; `Sí` is never pressed on a built-in prompt, so no vendor CLI is updated by this validation.

- **T1 Desktop table (real PATH).** Six rows `settings.autoUpdate.row.{claude,codex,hermes,pi,opencode,antigravity}` in that order. Precondition for the bound: the startup pass finished before T0 (`agent-update.overlay` absent at T0), so the first call schedules every probe at once (the normal case of §5.11). Within 20 s of T0 (PROBE_TIMEOUT 15 s + 5 s margin) no `.installed` cell reads `data-ac-state="checking"`. For each row record the actual cell: either `installed` with text matching `^\d+(\.\d+)+$` and equal to the version token of `<command> --version` run in the tester's shell with the same `PATH`, or `missing`/`probe-failed` with text `Not installed` and a non-empty `title`, or `unprobed` with text `Installed`. `app.log` of the isolated instance contains one `[agent-version]` line per probed command after T0. Reopening the screen within 10 minutes adds no `[agent-version]` line and no `Checking...` cell (cache served). Visual check (permission-gated): no console window appeared.
- **T2 Missing command (`F-missing`).** The codex row reads `data-ac-state="missing"`, text `Not installed`, `title` = `'codex' was not found on PATH`, within 20 s of T0.
- **T3 Failing probe (`F-stub`).** The codex row reads `data-ac-state="probe-failed"`, text `Not installed`, `title` starting with `Version check failed: exit code 3`, within 20 s of T0; `app.log` has a `[agent-version] probe failed for` line naming the stub path.
- **T4 Startup pass (`F-pass`, fixture project first in `project_paths`).** Observation procedure (the automation bridge has no state-predicate wait, so the tester polls): relaunch; the prompt for Bob appears (`agent-update.prompt.yes` present, prompt text naming `Bob`); answer `Sí`; the prompt for Bad follows; answer `Sí`. T0p = the moment the second prompt is answered. From T0p poll the overlay every <= 1 s (`agent-update.overlay` and its `data-ac-state`, `agent-update.node.bob-stub`, `agent-update.node.bad-stub`, each node's `data-ac-state`, its `.state` text and its `.detail` text when present, `agent-update.progress.text` text and `data-ac-done/total/failed`, `agent-update.progress` `aria-valuenow`), recording the first-seen timestamp of every state per node, for at most 90 s. Pass iff: both nodes exist in that DOM order (bob before bad: catalog order); both were observed `data-ac-state="updating"` (text `Actualizando...`) at least once, and while `updating` each node's `agent-update.node.<command>.command` text was exactly its update sequence (`bob-stub up` / `bad-stub up`) (W = 20 s of stub runtime at <= 1 s polling leaves >= 15 samples even if the bridge needs 5 s between the answer and the first poll, so a miss is a defect, not a flake; a `Pendiente` observation is recorded if it occurs but is not required here: these stubs are `unprobed`, so their pre-probe runs no process and the pending window is milliseconds; T6 pins `Pendiente` deterministically); bob-stub was then observed `ok` (`Listo`, no `.detail` element: an `unprobed` command has no version source) and bad-stub `failed` (`Falló`, `.detail` text `exit code 1`, `title` equal; the node's visible line reads `Falló · exit code 1`), each within W + 15 s of that node's first `updating` observation; the counter read `0 de 2 completados` before the first terminal state and `2 de 2 completados · 1 falló` after the last (`data-ac-failed="1"`, `aria-valuenow="2"`); the overlay root then read `data-ac-state="summary"` with the title `Coding agents actualizados`, no spinner, `agent-update.done` present, `agent-update.summary.close` present, and it was STILL present 10 s after the last terminal state with no interaction (persistence); clicking `agent-update.summary.close` removed the overlay within 2 s and the toast `Auto-update failed for Bad (bad-stub): exit code 1` appeared after the close, not before; `app.log` shows `[agent-update] prompting for 'bob-stub' (Bob)` and `prompting for 'bad-stub' (Bad)`, exactly one `[agent-update] answer for 'bob-stub'` line and exactly one for `'bad-stub'`, then `[agent-update] running 'bob-stub up' for Bob (bob-stub)` and `running 'bad-stub up' for Bad (bad-stub)` followed by `step ok for Bob (bob-stub)` and `step FAILED for Bad (bad-stub)` with timestamps about W apart. Afterwards Settings > Coding Agents shows exactly two rows (`bob`, `bad`), `.configured` = `yes`/`Yes` for both (the answers were persisted; the modal re-fetched settings on open), `.live` = `ok`/`Updated` for bob and `failed`/`Update failed` with a non-empty `title` for bad, `.installed` = `unprobed`/`Installed` for both (no built-in probe for these stems), the testable instance's settings file (read-only) has `agentAutoUpdateByCommand` = `{"bad-stub": true, "bob-stub": true}` for these keys, and no `[agent-version]` line names a built-in command (they are not rows of the active catalog) nor `bob-stub`/`bad-stub` (unprobed stems: their pre-update and post-pass checks resolve the path without a process).
- **T5 Browser mode (permission-gated browser interaction).** With the web remote enabled, the same table renders with the same six rows and identical cell states as T1 in the browser; during a `F-pass` relaunch the overlay timeline appears in the browser too with the same nodes and states, and closing the summary on one surface leaves the other surface's summary open until it is closed there (poll `agent-update.summary.close` on both; close on the desktop first, verify the browser still shows `data-ac-state="summary"` for >= 5 s, then close it there); the browser console shows no `Unknown command` for `get_agent_update_status`, `agent_update_answer`, `get_agent_update_overview`, `get_coding_agent_catalog`, `list_reseedable_agent_commands`. **T5-prompt (R3-M1; the browser-enabled form of T4's prompt phase, same relaunch):** with the `F-pass` project first and both policies ABSENT (the F-pass registration above), relaunch with the desktop window and one browser tab open: both surfaces show the prompt for Bob; answer `Sí` on ONE surface (desktop or browser, record which); the OTHER surface's prompt disappears within 2 s (poll the absence of `agent-update.prompt.no` there every <= 500 ms); then press Escape on that other surface (the surface whose prompt already closed): no prompt is shown there, no toast about the policy appears there, and the testable instance's settings file (read-only) has `agentAutoUpdateByCommand["bob-stub"] == true` both before and after the Escape (the first answer's policy), and `app.log` contains exactly ONE line matching `[agent-update] answer for 'bob-stub'` (the answering surface's): the handler does not answer a closed prompt, so the Escape sends nothing (§5.13, §7 (G)). The prompt for Bad then appears on both surfaces: answer `Sí` on the OTHER surface (roles swapped) and repeat the closure (<= 2 s), Escape, settings-file, and single-log-line checks for `bad-stub`. The pass then runs and T4's observation continues from T0p (both rows `Actualizando...` on both surfaces, then `Listo` / `Falló`). The stubs run `bob-stub up` / `bad-stub up`, so no vendor CLI is touched.
- **T6 Version transition and `Pendiente` (`F-ver`; after T4/T5, same disposable project, one more relaunch; permission-gated like T4).** Fixture: a directory `F-ver` prepended to the PROCESS `PATH` of the launching shell holding `codex.cmd` = `@echo off` / `ping -n 11 127.0.0.1 >NUL` / `if exist "%~dp0updated.flag" (echo 1.0.1) else (echo 1.0.0)` / `exit /b 0` (about 10 s of delay, always below `PROBE_TIMEOUT` = 15 s, then a version token; it ignores its arguments; Grinch M1, round 6) and `codex-up-stub.cmd` = `@echo off` / `ping -n 21 127.0.0.1 >NUL` / `echo updated> "%~dp0updated.flag"` / `exit /b 0`; `updated.flag` must NOT exist at launch (verify). Verify in that shell: `codex --version` prints `1.0.0` after about 10 s and `(Get-Command codex).Source` is the fixture's `codex.cmd` (the real codex shim is shadowed for processes started from this shell only; it is never executed and never modified). Catalog: append to the disposable project's `.ac\coding-agents\agents.json` a third entry `{"key":"ver","label":"Ver","description":"disposable version-transition stub","color":"#1e3a8a","command":"codex","instructionsFilename":"VER.md","envs":[],"isolatedHome":false,"configSeed":{"enabled":false,"dest":".ver"},"removable":true,"updateCommands":["codex-up-stub"]}` (its non-empty sequence is never backfilled, §3.2, so the pass runs `codex-up-stub`, never `codex update`); register `Ver` through Settings like Bob and Bad (its policy absent; Bob and Bad keep the `true` persisted by T4). Relaunch: exactly one prompt (`Ver`), answered `Sí` (T0v). From T0v poll as in T4 for at most 120 s. Pass iff: the timeline shows the three nodes in catalog order `bob-stub`, `bad-stub`, `codex`; `agent-update.node.codex` was observed `data-ac-state="pending"` (text `Pendiente`) at least once within the first 12 s after T0v while bob/bad were already `updating` (the pre-update probe of the stub keeps the node pending for about 10 s; a 10 s window at <= 1 s polling leaves >= 5 samples even with the 5 s bridge allowance T4 tolerates between the answer and the first poll, so a miss is a defect, not a flake); it then read `updating` with `agent-update.node.codex.command` text `codex-up-stub`; bob/bad ended as in T4; codex ended `ok` (`Listo`) and its `agent-update.node.codex.detail` text became exactly `1.0.0 → 1.0.1` within W + 15 s + 10 s + 5 s = 50 s of its first `updating` observation (the post-pass probe waits about 10 s in the stub; derived chronology from T0v: bob/bad end at about 20 s, codex starts at about 10 s and ends at about 30 s, `join_all` returns at about 30 s, the post-pass probe reports at about 40 s, i.e. about 30 s after codex's first `updating`); the 120 s cap stands; the counter read `3 de 3 completados · 1 falló`; the summary persisted 10 s and closed on `Cerrar`. `app.log` of the isolated instance contains, in this order: `[agent-update] prompting for 'codex' (Ver)`, exactly one `[agent-update] answer for 'codex'`, a `[agent-version]` line naming the fixture's `codex.cmd` path together with `1.0.0` BEFORE `[agent-update] running 'codex-up-stub' for Ver (codex)`, `[agent-update] step ok for Ver (codex)`, and a second `[agent-version]` line naming the same path together with `1.0.1` after it; the fixture's `updated.flag` exists afterwards; the testable settings file has `agentAutoUpdateByCommand["codex"] == true`; Settings > Coding Agents then shows the `ver` row with `.installed` = `installed`/`1.0.1` (the post-pass commit served from the cache: no third `[agent-version]` line on open). Cleanup adds: delete `F-ver` (with `updated.flag`) and remove the `ver` entry and the `Ver` agent together with the rest of the disposable project. Cost of the 10 s stub: the gate, and therefore the restore, waits about 10 s longer in this fixture only.

Cleanup: close the testable instance, delete the fixture directories and the disposable project, `test-reset --confirm-testeable`; nothing outside the tester's evidence root and the testable config directory was modified, and the report states so.

**9.4.3 Permission gate (owner: coordinator).** Approval of this plan is NOT permission for interactive testing. Before delegating any GUI, browser, terminal-input, semantic UI interaction, or image/screenshot capture of 9.4.2 (every case above except the `app.log`, settings-file, and `--version` shell reads), the coordinator obtains explicit user permission and records it in the dispatch. Until then 9.4.2 is `PENDING_PERMISSION`, and the completed evidence is §9.1-§9.3 plus 9.4.1. `PENDING_PERMISSION` BLOCKS §12 steps 14 and 15 (branch-current gate, PR, landing) with no exception: the required interactive cases are mandatory gates, there is no route around them, and nobody in the workgroup may skip them or authorize skipping them (P8). The block lifts only through one of two verified routes: (1) the user authorizes the interaction and ac-cli-tester-v3 executes the pending cases on the exact 9.4.1 receipt, reporting the 9.4.2 evidence; or (2) the user prefers to run them personally: the coordinator hands over the exact artifact path, its receipt (SHA-256, build head), and the verbatim steps, fixtures and expected evidence of each pending case, and the coordination WAITS for the user's concrete result, which must satisfy the same evidence fields (binary identity, environment, action, expected, observed, exit status, cleanup) before anything proceeds; a result that does not satisfy them is a finding and re-enters the §12 step 12 defect loop. Without a granted permission or a satisfying manual result the state stays `VALIDATION_PENDING`: no branch-current gate, no PR, no landing, and the delivery is never presented as validated.

## 10. Explicit decisions and accepted residuals

- Current-boot outcome IS displayed (`Updated` / `Update failed`), process-scoped, no history.
- Four backend install states plus `checking`; the visible label folds `probeFailed` into `Not installed` (acceptance text) with the reason in the tooltip.
- Probes only for bare built-in stems: a custom or path-based command shows `Installed` without a version; accepted for the consent boundary.
- Version parsing reads the FIRST non-empty sanitized line only (§5.2): a CLI that prints a banner line before its version would read `Not installed` with `no version in output: <banner>` in the tooltip; every built-in of §3.5 prints its token on the first text line, and scanning further lines would trade this for false positives (a dependency version on a later line), so the rule is fixed.
- PATH of the daemon process may differ from the shell's PATH: documented, not solved.
- No probe while the pass is not finished: uncached rows read `Checking...` and cached rows keep pre-pass states until the post-pass refresh; on the desktop the overlay hides Settings during the pass anyway, so this is observable only from a browser client.
- The pass-end invalidation is defensive in production (Rust R3): no ticket is opened before `finished`, so at invalidation time the cache is empty and no ticket is open; the `Stale` renewal path and the "cached rows keep pre-pass state during the pass" situation are reachable only when a test seeds the cache. The design keeps them because the rule is total and cheap.
- `Checking...` is shown only until the first committed state per command in one open; a later re-probe (TTL expiry, post-pass) updates the cell in place, so the previous version stays visible for up to 15 s instead of flashing `Checking...`; accepted (the Status column already shows `Updating...`/`Updated`).
- After a pass every cache entry is invalidated, not only the updated commands: conservative and simpler; six probes at most.
- The re-poll may issue one extra instant, process-free overview call after events already resolved every row; accepted.
- A browser tab whose Coding Agents screen stays open across a daemon restart keeps the old `seq` high-water marks until the screen is remounted (the new backend's `seq` restarts at 1); accepted: reopening the screen resets, and the tab's whole snapshot state is stale in that situation anyway (pre-existing).
- WebSocket queue overflow drops frames and detaches the client's broadcast sender (pre-existing, §3.3); the re-poll re-asks within 20 s and the table converges on the next answered call, the pass state heals through `agent_updates_finished`/snapshot as before; a dropped `agent_update_prompt_closed` frame on a browser client leaves that tab's prompt visible until the timeout or the next event, and any answer it then sends is refused (`false`, nothing persisted) or recorded as late; the policy can never diverge from the winning answer.
- Mid-pass `snapshot().results` now carries finished-so-far results (previously empty until the end); the FE dedup keeps failure toasts at one per command.
- Timeline order (round 5): pass order == catalog order, carried by `nodes`; the round-4 by-command sort is gone (the overlay no longer lacks a catalog view: the backend sends the nodes).
- `agent_update_answer` still emits no settings-updated event; the modal re-fetches on open; accepted (unchanged behavior).
- Unregistered rows with an absent key read `Will ask at startup` although the pass never prompts for an unregistered command (Grinch R2): accepted. The cell shows the stored policy value the issue asks to display distinctly (true/false/absent), and the same row carries the `(not registered)` marker with the hint "Only registered coding agents are updated at startup"; a fourth label would leave the tri-state contract of (B).
- Catalog `updateCommands`/`envs` and resolved program paths are readable by authenticated browser clients (Grinch R6): accepted, same trust boundary as `get_settings` (already routed), no secret by contract, nothing redacted.
- `FinishGuard::drop` must pass the invalidation in `PassEnd.invalidate` and the emit in `PassEnd.emit` (named fields, §5.4 step 4): the helper's order is test-pinned deterministically, the drop's effects are test-pinned, and the field wiring of the three-line drop body is a Grinch code-review checklist item (Grinch R8 closed to that extent).
- A `false` answer toasts the persisted policy read from the snapshot (§5.13): one extra process-free IPC call on that rare path; accepted.
- The `lib -> agent_version` arc appears in the record only when `lib.rs` names the module through a `crate::`-anchored path (the detector does not resolve bare relative paths in the crate root). §5.7 prescribes that spelling, so the expected record has seven added lines (1037 in total); a bare spelling would give 1036 and is not the prescribed one (Grinch R4, round 6); the arc is into-sink under either spelling.
- Settings copy is English (dispatch) while the issue quotes Spanish acceptance labels: the PR description maps `Not installed` <-> "no instalada", `Will ask at startup` <-> "se preguntará al arranque", `Updating...` <-> "Actualizando…" (overlay: `Actualizando...`), so the acceptance criteria are traceable (§12 step 15).
- Round 5, pre-update probe latency: each target's update starts after its own `--version` probe (about 0.2-1.1 s warm per §3.5, 15 s worst case for a hung CLI); the gate, and therefore session restore, waits that much longer per boot with updates; accepted for a real `before` value (the alternatives were a schema change or an invented one, §5.16).
- Round 5, `Pendiente` in production is short: the pre-probe window (plus the prompt phase on a surface that shows no prompt, and whatever a surface has not yet observed). It is a real state with real occurrences, pinned deterministically by unit tests and by T6's delayed stub, and never a serialization; accepted.
- Round 5, a dropped `agent_install_state_changed` frame (WebSocket queue full, pre-existing) leaves a finished node with its `before` value only (`2.1.34` instead of `2.1.34 → 2.1.35`) until the summary is closed; no re-poll in the overlay (Settings keeps its own); accepted (honest partial, never wrong).
- Round 5, zero-node pass (every prompt answered `No` or expired): no summary, the overlay closes on `finished` as at 92afbfe6; accepted (nothing to summarize).
- Round 5, the summary is per surface and never restored from a snapshot: a tab opened after the pass shows nothing (the Settings table shows the outcome); accepted.
- Round 5/6, strings: every literal the prototype contains is verbatim, reconciled by its author in the round-5 FE verdict (M2, M3): `Actualizando coding agents...` (`index-V1.html:910`), `Coding agents actualizados` (`:951`; round 5 had `Actualización completada`, the Option 3 title, corrected in round 6), `Agentes completados` (the progress bar's `aria-label`, `:914`; round 5 had `Progreso de la actualización`), `Pendiente`/`Actualizando...`/`Listo`/`Falló` (`:700`), `n de N completados · k falló`/`fallaron` (`:952-954`), `Listo · <before> → <after>` (`:943`), `Falló · <error>` (`:945`). The strings the prototype does not contain are fixed by this plan and accepted by the prototype's author: `Cerrar`, `no instalada`, `versión no detectada`, `Agentes de la actualización`.
- Round 5, the running node is the only one printing its sequence (the prototype's emphasis); other nodes keep it in `title`; accepted.
- Round 5/6, contrast (coordinator decision on FE N4): the pending node's label and marker keep the prototype's dimmed treatment (`--sidebar-fg-dim`, about 3.6:1 on `--sidebar-surface`, below AA 4.5:1; the state is also carried by `data-ac-state` and by the empty ring), while the state word `Pendiente` uses `--sidebar-fg` (AA for small text): a minimal, deliberate deviation from the prototype, pinned in §9.2 by a stable class/token assertion (the rule text and the DOM hooks), never by a computed-color test; the running, done and failed nodes and every header text meet AA.
- Round 5/6, failure toasts move from `finished` to the summary's close on surfaces that show the summary (the prototype's last step); surfaces without a summary toast at `finished` as today; a surface mounted in the middle of the pass toasts nothing at mount (its snapshot has `inProgress: true`; its toasts come at the close), a surface mounted after the pass toasts from the snapshot at once (Grinch R3); accepted.
- Round 5, `mark_command_started` changes its round-4 signature (adds `install_before`, returns the node) and `run_update_target` gains the pre-probe; no code exists yet, so nothing breaks; recorded so the reviewers' round-4 notes map cleanly.
- Round 6, the summary closes instantly (`Show` unmounts the overlay); the prototype's 0.35 s fade-out is not reproduced: an accepted simplification (FE N6).
- Round 6, `UNKNOWN_ERROR_LABEL = "unknown error"` (§5.10) is English inside the Spanish overlay: practically unreachable, because the runner always fills `error` (§3.1), and it mirrors the existing production toast literal (`agent-update.ts:37`, `result.error ?? "unknown error"`); no new translation is opened in this round (FE N3). The failure toast keeps its production literal `Auto-update failed for <label> (<command>): <error>`, with the `(command)`, which T4 asserts; the prototype's approximation without it (`index-V1.html:1056`) does not enter (FE N7).
- Round 6, residuals of the round-5 FE verdict, recorded and not in scope: N5 (the scrim does not make the app behind `inert`; Tab can leave the overlay; pre-existing in the prompt branch, and the summary has one focusable element), N10 (the scrolling timeline has no focusable content, so keyboard scrolling depends on the browser focusing scrollers, which current Chromium does; a `tabindex="0"` on overflow is a possible follow-up), N11 (`mergeSnapshot` never lowers `inProgress`, so a WebSocket surface that lost the `agent_updates_finished` frame keeps the overlay until remount: round-4 behavior, not introduced by the timeline), N12 (`mark_command_started` synthesizes an un-inserted node for an unknown command, consistent with the FE defensive append). A `command_skipped` delivered before its own pass's `started` would be reset by `started` (the same theoretical Tauri window `closedPrompts` accepts): a skip needs a human answer or 60 s after `started`, so it is not reachable in practice; accepted.

## 11. Dependency-cycle and layering statement (planning rule 8)

Measured by materialising a syntactic skeleton of every new cross-module reference of §5 on the clean twin of 92afbfe6 and re-running the detector (materialise-and-measure on a scratch twin; the shared repo was not touched):

- Baseline: `modulesResolved 190`, `moduleEdges 3696`, `moduleCycles 1` (85-member SCC), arc record byte-identical (1030 lines).
- Skeleton (the round-2 measurement, with the bare `agent_version::` spelling in `lib.rs`): `modulesResolved 191`, `moduleEdges 3729`, `moduleCycles 1`, the cyclic SCC member set IDENTICAL (85 == 85, set-equal), arc record 1036 lines; with the `crate::agent_version::` spelling that §5.7 prescribes (the round-6 measurement below) the record has 1037 lines. Diff against the base record, exactly these seven lines (the first one is the §5.7 spelling's line):

```
agentscommander_lib -> agentscommander_lib::agent_version
agentscommander_lib::agent_update -> agentscommander_lib::agent_version
agentscommander_lib::agent_update -> agentscommander_lib::config::agent_command
agentscommander_lib::agent_update -> agentscommander_lib::web::broadcast
agentscommander_lib::agent_update -> agentscommander_lib::web::event_broadcast
agentscommander_lib::agent_version -> agentscommander_lib::pty::job
agentscommander_lib::commands::config -> agentscommander_lib::agent_version
```

Per-arc classification: `agent_update -> config::agent_command`: both pre-existing members of the SCC (internal, safe). `agent_update -> web::broadcast`, `agent_update -> web::event_broadcast`, `agent_update -> agent_version`, `commands::config -> agent_version`: source inside the SCC, target a non-member that reaches no member (`web::event_broadcast` names only `web::broadcast`, pinned by its own test; `web::broadcast` and `pty::job` are referenced by members and are not members, so they cannot reach the SCC; `agent_version` names only `pty::job` and std/crates): into-sink arcs, cannot create or grow a cycle. `agent_version -> pty::job`: sink to sink. `lib -> agent_version` (the crate root, an SCC member, naming the new sink through the `crate::` path §5.7 prescribes): into-sink, safe; it is the seventh line, present whenever `lib.rs` uses that spelling (§10, Grinch R4). `commands::session -> config::agent_command`, `web::commands -> commands::config`, and `commands::config -> agent_update` already exist. Test-only references (`web::commands` tests managing the gate/cache; the `include_str!` scrape test) are ignored by the detector.

Round-2 delta: every change of that revision was module-internal and added NO cross-module reference (`AgentUpdateGate::is_finished`, `probe_commit_announce`, the revised `update_overview` and `FinishGuard::drop` live in `agent_update` and name only items of modules the skeleton already referenced; `Completion`, `seq`, `live`, and `Default` are internal to `agent_version`, which still names only `pty::job`; the frontend adds no module beyond the six counted in §9.3.3).

Round-3 delta (re-checked reference by reference): `answer_prompt`, `PromptState`, `AnswerClaim`, `ClaimedPrompt`, `PendingPrompt`, `claim_answer`, `prompt_state`, `finish_pass`, `PassEnd`, `settle_joined_update`, `ProbeFn`, `production_probe`, `update_overview_with`, and the `answer_serial` field live in `agent_update` and name only `tokio`, `serde_json`, `std`, and items of modules already referenced (`agent_version::Scheduling` replaces `CacheLookup` behind the existing `agent_update -> agent_version` arc); `agent_update` receives the persist function as a closure argument, so it does NOT name `commands::config` (no new arc, no cycle); `commands::config::agent_update_answer_inner` names `crate::agent_update::{answer_prompt, AgentUpdateGate}` behind the pre-existing `commands::config -> agent_update` arc; `lookup_or_begin`, `Scheduling`, `text_lines`, `first_text_line`, `in_flight_len` are internal to `agent_version`, which still names only `pty::job`; `web::commands` still names only `crate::commands::config::*` in production; the frontend gains no module (the overlay and store edits are in files already counted). The cross-module reference set of the round-2 skeleton is therefore unchanged and the measurement stands for round 3; the implementer criterion below re-verifies it on the real tree.

Round-5 delta (re-measured on 2026-08-26 on the same clean twin, with the round-5 items materialised as a syntactic skeleton on top of the round-2 one: `AgentUpdateNode`, `pass_nodes`, `skip_prompted_target`, `run_update_target` with the pre-probe, `schedule_post_update_probes`; detector exit 1 as expected): `modulesResolved 191`, `moduleEdges 3730` (one more edge INSTANCE than the round-2 skeleton's 3729: `pass_nodes` names `crate::config::coding_agents_catalog::CodingAgentDefinition`, a module `agent_update` already referenced at 92afbfe6), `moduleCycles 1`, `functionCyclesCrossModule 0`; distinct module pairs 1020 -> 1026, ADDED exactly the six `agent_update`/`agent_version`/`commands::config` lines above (the round-5 twin still spelled `lib.rs` bare, so the `lib -> agent_version` line was absent), REMOVED none; the regenerated arc record (1036 arcs) is byte-identical to the round-2 skeleton's record, and its diff against the base record is exactly those six lines; the cyclic SCC's member set is identical set-to-set to the baseline (85 members, recomputed with Tarjan over the detector's module edges; the detector's own member list of §3.6 is the same set; one member, `testability::window_info`, is joined only through a `cfgGated` edge, which is why a run that drops gated edges reports 84 on both sides, still identical); `agent_version` is in no cyclic SCC. Per-arc classification by transitive reachability on the post graph: `agent_update -> config::agent_command` internal to the pre-existing SCC; the other five into-sink (their targets reach no SCC member). Zero cross-boundary arcs, zero new knots. Round 5 adds no module reference of its own: every new item lives in `agent_update` and names `agent_version` (the round-2 arc), `config::coding_agents_catalog` (an arc that exists at 92afbfe6), `web::broadcast`/`web::event_broadcast` through `emit_all` (round-2 arcs), tokio, serde_json and std; the frontend adds no module (`check:frontend-dependencies` re-run on 2026-08-26 at 92afbfe6: `modules: 344, errors: 0, dependencies: 1498`, fixture matrix PASS; the six new `src/` files of §6 are the same as in round 4, and `agent-update-status.ts` keeps importing types only, never the store, so the overlay -> derivations -> types chain stays acyclic).

Round-6 delta (re-measured on 2026-08-26 on the same twin after applying the §5.7 spelling `Arc<crate::agent_version::AgentInstallCache>` / `crate::agent_version::AgentInstallCache::new()` to the twin's `lib.rs`, the only skeleton change round 6 needs; detector exit 1 as expected): `modulesResolved 191`, `moduleEdges 3732` (two edge instances more than the round-5 skeleton: the two `crate::agent_version::` references of `lib.rs`), `moduleCycles 1`, `functionCyclesCrossModule 0`; distinct module pairs 1020 -> 1027, ADDED exactly the seven lines above, REMOVED none; the regenerated record has 1037 arcs (82149 bytes), every base line present and unchanged, and its diff against the base record is exactly the seven lines; the cyclic SCC's member set is identical set-to-set (84 == 84 with the `cfgGated` edge excluded, as in round 5; the detector's own count with it is 85 on both sides); `agent_version` is in no cyclic SCC; per-arc classification by transitive reachability on the post graph: `agent_update -> config::agent_command` internal to the pre-existing SCC, the other six into-sink; zero cross-boundary arcs, zero new knots. The fan-in/fan-out table below changes in one cell: `agent_version` in 0 -> 3 (`lib`, `agent_update`, `commands::config`). Round 6 adds no module reference of its own: `skippedNodes`, the monotonic `mergeSnapshot` rule, the `nodes`-keyed install listener, the deferred snapshot toasts, the new tests and the §5.14 transcription live in files already counted (`agent-update.ts`, `AgentUpdateOverlay.tsx`, their tests, `sidebar.css`, `agent_update.rs`) and add no import beyond `Index` from `solid-js` (an already-imported package); the Rust end-to-end test lives in the `tests` module and names only items the module already names; `check:frontend-dependencies` re-run on 2026-08-26 at 92afbfe6: `modules: 344, errors: 0, dependencies: 1498`, fixture matrix PASS.

Pre/post table of the integration points (distinct module pairs from the detector graphs, `in` = fan-in, `out` = fan-out; base 92afbfe6 -> round-6 skeleton, i.e. the round-5 skeleton plus the §5.7 spelling in `lib.rs`):

|---|---|---|---|---|
| `agent_update` | 3 -> 3 (`lib`, `commands::config`, `commands::session`) | 4 -> 8 (+`agent_version`, +`config::agent_command`, +`web::broadcast`, +`web::event_broadcast`) | four out-arcs, all into sinks or SCC-internal | out 8 of 191 modules, fan-in unchanged: not a hub; the owner of the pass gains its emitter and its probe engine |

Role/layering hygiene: `agent_version` (the new lowest layer) has no `tauri`, `AppHandle`, settings, or transport dependency; `agent_update` already owned `AppHandle` + `Emitter` and gains the sanctioned dual-transport emitter (#1265 pattern, same as `commands::project_settings`) and the answer flow (which already lived in the commands layer with the same `AppHandle` surface); no config or pty module gains a transport dependency; `web::commands` gains zero module references.

Acceptance criterion for the implementer (base `92afbfe6` vs final branch head, clean tree for both; `<VAULT>` = `D:\0_repos\AgentsCommander_iac\.ac\wg-13-ac-dev-team-v3\repo-personal\ObsidianVault\Coding Agents\IA-Programming\rust`):

node "<VAULT>\01-rust_module-dependency-cycles.mjs" src-tauri --emit-graph pre.json --json > pre-summary.json

## 12. Implementation order and ownership

Two implementers plus the documentation specialist on the same branch, sequential commits, each commit green on the gates it touches (all gates from PowerShell). The contract of §5.1/§5.8 is fixed so both implementers can code against it without waiting. Steps 9-15 are the workgroup's mandatory delivery workflow; no step may be skipped or reordered.

Ownership by area (round 5): backend (`agent_update.rs` node set, skip event, pre/post probes, DTOs; the `web/commands.rs` test expectation) = ac-dev-rust-v3; frontend (types/ipc, store, derivations, overlay, CSS, tests) = ac-dev-webpage-ui-v3; docs = ac-technical-writer-v3; black-box = ac-cli-tester-v3 after the §9.4.3 permission; provenance = ac-shipper-v3. **Feasibility reviewer for the round-5 consensus: ac-dev-webpage-ui-v3**, without ambiguity: the round-5 delta is dominated by the frontend contract (every visible string, state, testid, rule and the CSS of §5.9, §5.10, §5.13, §5.14), its fidelity can only be judged against the prototype, which lives in that agent's own replica and which this author could not read (§18), and the backend delta reuses primitives the two Rust reviewers approved cold in round 4 (`emit_all`, `mark_command_*`, `probe_command_install_state`, `lookup_or_begin` + `probe_commit_announce`, `finish_pass`) with four small, listed additions (§5.4 steps 3, 5, 5a, 9b) that the Grinch code review of step 10 checks on the real diff. If the coordinator keeps the dual gate, the adversarial reviewer's backend attention goes to exactly those four additions.

## 15. Round-3 correction map (coordinator dispatch 2026-08-25 21:33 UTC) and residual treatment

Mandatory changes:

- **R3-M1 Prompt lifecycle, two surfaces, concurrent answers.** (1) Every resolving answer emits `agent_update_prompt_closed` through `emit_all` BEFORE it releases the loop: §5.4 step 9 `answer_prompt` (`Claimed` -> emit -> `deliver`), §5.1 event table (payload `AgentUpdateCommandRef`, "resolved from ANY surface or expired", one emitter: the remover of the pending entry), pinned since round 4 by the synchronous waker probe of `answer_prompt_enqueues_the_closure_before_releasing_the_loop` (§17 R4-M1). (2) A second answer to a prompt already resolved by an answer returns `Ok(false)` without persisting: `PromptState::Answered` checked first inside the serialized critical section, before any persist; §5.1 result semantics (b). (3) Late answer after the timeout: `PromptState::Expired` -> persist -> `Recorded` -> `Ok(false)`, pass unaffected; the loop's expiry arm is the emitter of that closure; §5.1 result semantics (a). (4) No check-then-act: classify -> persist -> claim -> emit -> deliver run inside `gate.answer_serial` (tokio mutex, FIFO, held across the persist await; the std `state` lock is taken synchronously), and the expiry arm enters the same section before removing the entry; persist failure = `Err`, nothing changed, next waiter proceeds; the order for two simultaneous answers, persist success, persist failure, and an expiry racing a claim is proven in §5.4 step 9 and pinned by `answer_prompt_two_simultaneous_answers_are_serialized_first_wins`, `answer_prompt_failed_persist_keeps_the_prompt_pending_and_the_next_answer_wins`, `answer_prompt_enqueues_the_closure_before_releasing_the_loop` (round 4, §17 R4-M1: its `ReleaseProbe` waker observes, inside the oneshot send that releases the loop, that the closure frame was already enqueued, red under the send-before-emit order on any runtime flavor; the separate `order_rx` then checks closed(A) before prompt(B) as behavior, not as the discriminator), and `answer_prompt_and_expiry_race_emit_exactly_one_closure_with_either_winner`. (5) Monotonic FE merge for prompts: §5.9 `closedPrompts`, `markPromptClosed`, `mergeSnapshot` rules (f)-(g), the conditional clears of §5.9/§5.13 (Grinch R1 / Rust R4 integrated, not optional; P7), §7 "Ordering / merge (prompt)". (6) Contract/events (§5.1, §5.8: `prompt_closed` payload, `AgentUpdateStatus.answered`, `onAgentUpdatePromptClosed` signature), backend state (§5.4 steps 1-3 and 9), FE state (§5.9, §5.13 `false` branch reading `answered`), docs (§5.15 (a), settings.md row, architecture row), tests: `answer_prompt_first_answer_resolves_persists_and_closes_every_surface` (closure on the WebSocket surface; the Tauri leg is the existing `broadcast_all` emit), `answer_prompt_superseded_answer_returns_false_without_persisting`, `answer_prompt_late_answer_after_timeout_persists_and_returns_false`, `answer_prompt_rejects_unprompted_command_without_persisting`, `gate_prompt_state_and_claim`, `agent_update_answer_route_returns_false_for_an_already_answered_prompt`, FE `prompt_closed carries the command: an older snapshot cannot resurrect that prompt`, `a new prompt after a closure still appears from an event and from a snapshot`, `a delayed prompt_closed(A) does not clear a newer prompt B`, `answer(A) resolving after prompt B arrived leaves B visible`, `a superseded answer toasts the policy the other surface persisted`, `the prompt closed by the store sends no answer on Escape`, and T4/T5-prompt (answer on one surface, closure on the other <= 2 s, Escape there sends nothing: exactly one `[agent-update] answer for` log line per prompted stub and the settings file unchanged).
- **R3-M2 Version parser with blank/ANSI lines.** §5.2 text helpers rewritten as one line-wise rule (`text_lines`: split on `\n` and `\r`, strip ANSI/controls per line, skip empty; `first_text_line`; `parse_version_token` on the first non-empty sanitized line; `sanitize_detail` = the same line truncated); the round-2 `first_text_line(raw)` that cut at the first line break is gone; fixtures `"\n\n  1.18.23\n"`, `"\r\n\r\n1.2.3\r\n"`, `"\n\x1b[?25l\n2.0.1\n"`, `"\x1b[?25l\n"` -> `None`; process tests `probe_version_skips_leading_blank_lines` (`cmd.exe /C "echo.& echo 1.2.3"` / `sh -c "printf '\n\n1.2.3\n'"`) and `probe_version_skips_an_ansi_only_first_line` (`powershell.exe -NoProfile -NonInteractive -Command "[char]27 + '[?25l'; '2.0.1'"` / `sh -c "printf '\033[?25l\n2.0.1\n'"`); the diagnostic stays one bounded line (`DETAIL_MAX_CHARS`); §5.15 (b) and §10 record the first-text-line rule.
- **R3-M3 Atomic single-flight.** §5.2 `lookup_or_begin(command, now, ttl, may_begin) -> Scheduling {Fresh, InFlight, Began, Deferred}` under ONE lock; `try_begin` becomes `#[cfg(test)]`; `update_overview_with` schedules only through it (§5.4 step 8, single-flight proof); TTL, generation, `seq`, ticket renewal and no-lock-across-await unchanged; deterministic tests `cache_lookup_or_begin_opens_exactly_one_ticket_under_contention` (barrier, 8 threads, one `Began`), `cache_lookup_or_begin_serves_a_commit_that_landed_before_the_second_call`, `update_overview_two_racing_calls_schedule_one_probe`, `update_overview_serves_a_commit_that_lands_between_two_calls` (the exact round-2 interleaving: one ticket, one process); §7 "Install states" no longer over-claims (the guarantee is now structural).

## 17. Round-4 correction map (coordinator dispatch 2026-08-26 00:06 UTC; the one additional round the user authorized after the round-3 dual `CHANGES_REQUIRED`)

- **R4-M1 False discriminator in `answer_prompt_enqueues_the_closure_before_releasing_the_loop` (Rust M1 and Grinch M1 of the round-3 verdicts, both `CHANGES_REQUIRED` on digest `839B15ED7F3E9B6385725F2B1F094B17B0048F7B0DD2D8D6AE51D1E9F6F40572`).** Finding: the round-3 text claimed the test "fails under the superseded send-before-emit order because the stand-in would run before the emit". False: `oneshot::Sender::send` wakes the stand-in task but does not run it; on the `current_thread` runtime of a plain `#[tokio::test]` the stand-in runs only when the test task yields, by which time the closure was emitted under either order (green under both), and on `multi_thread` another worker may or may not pick it up between the send and the emit (flaky, never reliably red). Binding decision, no alternative left open: the API and the design of §5.4 steps 1, 2, 3 and 9 stay exactly as they are (`ClaimedPrompt::closed()` + `ClaimedPrompt::deliver()`; no typestate, no `close_and_deliver`); the test's discriminator becomes a synchronous waker probe. `ReleaseProbe` (`std::task::Wake`; `probe_rx: std::sync::Mutex<tokio::sync::mpsc::Receiver<WsOutMsg>>` subscribed before the mock app; `seen: AtomicU8` with 0 = never woken, 1 = woken without the closure enqueued, 2 = woken with the closure enqueued) is registered as the waker of the prompt's `oneshot::Receiver` by exactly one manual poll that must return `Poll::Pending`; tokio runs `wake_by_ref` inline inside `Sender::send`, i.e. inside `deliver`, where the probe `try_recv()`s `probe_rx` and records whether `agent_update_prompt_closed {claude}` was already enqueued; the test asserts `answer_prompt == Ok(true)`, then `seen == 2`, then `rx.await == Ok(true)`, then mirrors the loop's advance (register and emit prompt B) and drains a SEPARATE `order_rx`: exactly closed(A), prompt(B), nothing else. Under emit-before-`deliver` `seen == 2` always; under send-before-emit `seen == 1` deterministically on any runtime flavor: that, not task scheduling, is the declared falsification; the `order_rx` check remains as the observable-order behavior test and is stated not to be the discriminator. Replacements (line numbers of digest `839B15ED...`): line 7, the Revision paragraph, rewritten for round 4; line 23 (§1), one sentence added; line 569 (§5.4 step 10 intro), the two subscriptions named; line 571 (§5.4 step 10, answer-flow group), the test description rewritten as above, followed by the new `ReleaseProbe` definition block and its lock/`Send`/`Unpin` argument; line 1046 (§12 step 10), the checklist item "the emit-before-`deliver` order" now names the probe as its mechanical pin and what the reviewer confirms; line 1081 (§15 R3-M1), items (1) and (4) name the probe; line 1116 (§16 P7), the test clause rewritten; this section added. No other sentence described the stand-in as failing under the superseded order (the whole file was swept for "stand-in", "send-before-emit", "fails under", "cannot pass" and "would run before the emit"; the only hits were the sites above).
- **Unchanged by round 4 (the binding limits of the dispatch).** §5.1 (wire contract, result semantics, event table, ordering guarantee), §5.4 step 9 (`answer_prompt`, the state machine, the four-case proof, the cancellation argument), §5.9, §5.13, P8 (§9.4.3 and §12 steps 13-15), the inventory (§6: Added 8 / Modified 22 / Removed 0, 30 paths), the preserve set, the arc statement (§11), owners, gates and the delivery order (§9, §12). `ReleaseProbe` is test-only code inside the `tests` module of `agent_update.rs`; its reference to `crate::web::broadcast::WsOutMsg` is a `#[cfg(test)]` reference the detector ignores (§3.6) and sits behind the production arc `agent_update -> web::broadcast` that §11 already counts: zero new cross-module references, no new file, no new dependency (`std::task::Wake`, `std::sync::atomic::AtomicU8` and tokio's `oneshot`/`mpsc` are already available). No placeholder marker or open alternative was introduced; the plan stays decision-complete.
- **Round-3 residuals, recorded and not round-4 scope (coordinator decision; none is applied in this revision, they stay in the immutable round-3 verdict files for a follow-up).** Rust R1 (`mark_finished` can remove a pending entry only on a boot whose prompt loop unwound: "in every non-panicking boot" wording for §5.1 and §5.4 step 2), R2 (§3.1 "all five emits" against §5.4 step 3 "four legacy `app.emit` calls": wording, the scrape test pins the truth), R3 (`impl Default for AgentUpdateGate` is at `:217`, not `:218`: the §1 rule "re-anchor on the quoted text, never on the number" applies), R4 (implementation note: `ProbeTicket::complete` lets `self` drop after the cache guard, never `drop(self)` inside the guard's scope), R5 (§5.2 `lookup` "read-only" wording: it removes an expired entry); Grinch R1 (`onAgentUpdatePrompt` does not consult `closedPrompts`, and the loop registers and emits the next prompt outside `answer_serial`: a microsecond window in which a surface could show an already-closed prompt while the backend stays consistent), R2 (the superseded-answer toast names the persisted policy, which in that case also applied this boot: UX note), R3 (the TTL-0 cache tests should pass an explicit later `now` so two equal `Instant::now()` readings cannot make them flaky).

## 18. Round-5 correction map (coordinator dispatch 2026-08-26 03:22 UTC; the user chose `Opción 2 · Progreso por etapas`)

- **Authority and limits.** The user selected `#opcion-2` of `index-V1.html` (`D:\0_repos\AgentsCommander_iac\.ac\wg-13-ac-dev-team-v3\__agent_ac-dev-webpage-ui-v3\artifacts\issue-1551\index-V1.html`, SHA-256 `A5A7149A662EC49802CBE0A7886BA2C6CD05A8984715FDD7D72092306268CEB6`, served as `http://127.0.0.1:8765/index-V1.html#opcion-2`). Option 2 is the complete visual and semantic authority for the update experience: timeline per agent by stages, global progress, emphasis on the running agent, the update command per agent, the version transition for finished agents when a real source exists, inline error per agent, the four states, a final summary that persists until the user closes it, and the exact Noir palette, hierarchy, spacing, responsive behavior and accessibility of the prototype. The backend stays concurrent (the timeline is presentation, `Pendiente` is never a serialization); `html-component-blink-debug` and the prototype file are review aids and enter nothing (no code, dependency, bundle, test, or inventory entry).
- **Divergence 1: `Pendiente` did not exist and updates run concurrently.** Resolved: the backend exposes the pass explicitly as `nodes` (§5.1, §5.4 steps 1-3, 5a): one node per target in catalog order, set atomically with `started` (`mark_started_with_nodes`) and carried by the `agent_updates_started` payload and the snapshot; a prompted target answered `No` or expired leaves the pass through `mark_command_skipped` and the new `agent_update_command_skipped` event, emitted by the loop (the owner of the decision) after that prompt's closure. `Pendiente` on a surface = a node with neither `running` nor a result (§5.10): known for the pass, its update not started (in production the pre-update probe window of §5.4 step 5, plus what the surface has not yet observed). The update phase still spawns every target together; nothing waits on the timeline. Data source: the gate; fallback: an older backend's `null` payload yields an event-built timeline (§5.9, §5.10 defensive append).
- **Divergence 2: the update command is shown.** Resolved: each node carries `updateCommands`, the exact catalog sequence `run_update_sequence` executes (`UpdateTarget.commands`, §3.1); the running node prints it (one `<code>` per step), the other nodes keep it in `title` (§5.13). No per-step event (`run_update_sequence` untouched, §4). Security: the same strings `get_coding_agent_catalog` already returns to every authenticated client; no new exposure, nothing redacted (§8).
- **Divergence 3: version transitions.** Resolved with two real sources and explicit fallbacks: the pre-update probe (`probe_command_install_state`, direct, inside `run_update_target` before the update; `AgentUpdateNode.installBefore`, §5.4 step 5) and the post-pass probe of every updated command (`schedule_post_update_probes` after `finish_pass`, through the cache, delivered by `agent_install_state_changed`; `installAfter` by max `seq`, §5.4 step 9b, §5.9). `versionTransitionText` (§5.10) prints `<before> → <after>` only when both were read, the honest partial forms otherwise (`2.1.34`, `no instalada → 2.1.35`, `2.1.34 → no instalada`), and nothing for commands without a probe source (`unprobed`: explicit path, unknown stem; the T4 stubs). Never a persisted, guessed or output-parsed version (§5.16). Cost and bound recorded in §10; the no-overlap and pass-boundary proofs extended in §5.4 step 9b.
- **Divergence 4: the summary persists until the user closes it.** Resolved: `summary: "none" | "shown" | "dismissed"` in the store, entered only by the `agent_updates_finished` EVENT on a surface that was showing the pass with at least one result (`results.length > 0`, §5.9; the same as "at least one node" under a correct backend, because every node that stays in the pass produces exactly one result, FE N9), left only by `dismissAgentUpdateSummary()` (the `Cerrar` button, Enter, or Escape when no prompt is shown) on that surface; the failure toasts move to the close on those surfaces; no backend state, no snapshot restoration, a zero-node pass closes as today (§5.9, §5.13, §7, §10).
- **Divergence 5: inline error.** Resolved: a failed node prints the runner's reason (`AgentUpdateResult.error`, one short string by construction, §3.1) on ONE line as `Falló · <reason>` (the `.state` span, a visible ` · ` and the `.detail` span, §5.13 round 6), the full text in `title`, followed by ` · <transition>` when the version transition is known (§5.10, §5.13). The Settings table keeps its tooltip (unchanged).
